## Saarvin

Saarvin, the self-titled King of Frozenfar, was a dragonborn adventurer of gold dragon descent who wandered the lands of the North and the Sword Coast North, protecting others during the late 15 century DR.

Personality
Saarvin was quite enthusiastic and boastful, and often shared fantastic stories of his exploits with his fellow travelers. His exuberance was a bit much and could rub some people the wrong way.

Possessions
[[File:Dragon_bow_WoW.png|thumb|A bow and arrow carved by Saarvin himself from the bones of a white dragon.]]Saarvin fought with bow and arrows, as well as throwing axes. He often wore a ranger's hood and a mountaineer's cloak.

During his time in Waterdeep Saarvin owned several magical arms, such as the enchanted bow Outlander and the unique axes of the slicing storm. He also owned several magical items, including a pair of bracers of archery, gloves of accuracy, the eyes of the hunter, an amulet of true strikes, and the seal of guidance. He also had armor fashioned from the scales and hide of a white dragon, as well as a mighty bow he had crafted from a white dragon jawbone with arrows carved from the teeth. He used these trophies to support his boasts about journeys through the Spine of the World.

Abilities

Saarvin was an experienced outdoorsman and quite knowledgeable about the frost-covered lands of the Icewind Dale and the Frozenfar. He was also a proficient hunter and tracker. In battle, he had a knack for outmaneuvering his opponents.

Relationships

The high-spirited wilderness guide had many friends across the North and Sword Coast, including Dasharra Keldabar the griffon-rider, the adventurers Minsc and Delina, and the druid Belvyre of Bryn Shander.

He forged a close bond with the Uthgardt barbarian Runa. While Runa originally felt indebted to Saarvin for saving her life, he felt it an act of honor between two protectors of the North. By the time Runa saved Saarvin from certain death, the reciprocation was a gesture of friendship more than one of debt.

At one time, Saarvin traveled the Waterdeep on the Sword Coast North to search for new friends and new challenges, and began performing missions on behalf of Open Lord Laeral Silverhand. During this time he developed a good-natured but mildly antagonistic relationship with Mirt, with the two finding ways to mock each other.

History
Saarvin was born in the settlement of Fireshear, and was extremely proud of his home town and its amenities.

Guide For Adventurers
Some time during the 1480s DR, Saarvin came to the aid of the Heroes of Baldur's Gate when they were ambushed by ogres. Shortly after their meeting, Saarvin fought alongside the heroes in defense of Fireshear, when it was threated by Gryttmort and his frost giant. After the giants were defeated, Saarvin used his extensive knowledge of the trails along Cold Crag Peak to guide the heroes to the lair of Nilanthe, the white dragon. To save Fireshear, Saarvin and the heroes soared to the fortress of Svardborg, and recovered the lost orb of dragonkind that was stolen by the giants.

Travels Across Icewind Dale
At the time when the Everlasting Rime afflicted the North, in the, Saarvin could be found traveling the bleak landscape of Icewind Dale. It was during one of his treks that he encountered the Uthgardt barbarian Runa and an outlander named Amos unsuccessfully battling a remorhaz. After Saarvin saved the travelers' lives, the trio returned to the Ten Towns and met with the druid Belvyre and his bodyguard Patience. The group vowed to work together to find an ancient ruined city that held powerful magic that could help Belvyre grow enough croups to help feed the beleaguered Ten Towns.

During their journey to underground city beneath the Reghed Glacier, the group was attacked by frost giant skeletons, and Runa was exposed to a growth of chardalyn that drove her into a murderous frenzy. Amos used the chaos to abduct Belvyre and steal his frostbrand walking staff for himself. Runa meanwhile attempted to slay Saarvin, and was only subdued when fellow members of the Blue Bear tribe arrived to intervene. The Uthgardt barbarians were themselves ready to kill Saarvin and Patience. They halted all aggressions however, when Runa snapped came to her senses and agree to carry on with their mission.

Once they arrived at the glacier, the Saarvin and his two companions delved into an old, seemingly-abandoned mine. They fought against groups of duergar loyal to Xardorok Sunblight ruler of the Sunblight fortress. When confronted with overwhelming odds, the trio received aid from Runa's fellow tribespeople. While they found Belvyre and Amos within the volcanic depths of the glacier, Saarvin was nearly plunged into its depths by an erupting fissure. Runa managed to grab him just in time, saving his life and honoring her debt. Reunited together, Saarvin, Runa, Patience, and Belvyre managed to climb to safety, marveling at the bountiful growth cause by the magic released from the depths of the glacier.

Rumors & Legends
Saarvin claimed to have climbed the tallest peak in the Spine of the World mountain range and stolen from the hoard of a white dragon, while it slumbered mere feet away.

## Sammaster's lash

Sammaster's lash was a spell that created a ribbon of force.

Effects
When cast, this spell created a ribbon of force. It was yellow-white in color and flew through the air. It was very thin. The caster controlled the ribbon using willpower. The ribbon was nearly invulnerable, only a dispel magic spell or a powerful magical barrier was able to stop it. When struck, a targeted creature became temporarily paralyzed.

In addition to verbal and somatic components, the spell required a material component, three hairs from a ghast braided together.

This spell was known to be used by wizards of the Cult of the Dragon.

## Sabran Cormaeril

Sabran Cormaeril was a young noble of House Cormaeril in Cormyr who was exiled for his participation in the Abraxus Conspiracy, circa the .

Description
Sabran was of small stature—thin, wiry, and quick on his feet. His hair was dark blonde but his eyebrows were black. The irises of his eyes were also very dark. He favored a clean-shaven look, dressed smartly in the latest fashion, and never went unarmed. He was often seen in the company of a small squad of bodyguards.

Personality
Sabran was a young, swaggering noble with a big ego. He believed (or at least pretended) that he was the target of assassins from Suzail who wanted to eliminate him because he was a thorn in the side of the Obarskyrs and their allies. He cared little for the more rural and wild parts of Cormyr, generally viewing the Starwater River as the dividing line between civilization and the unwashed peasantry. His ultimate goal was to rule Cormyr.

Activities
Sabran moved between Selgaunt and Westgate, never staying in one place for too long in case agents from Cormyr were looking for him.

He was the leader of a covert group of exiled, discredited, and disinherited nobles that fomented trouble in Cormyr when they could. Usually this involved sending agents that were loyal to them, or hirelings when appropriate, but occasionally the young blades would disguise themselves and brave their banishment just for the thrill of doing so. Missions included everything from carrying messages or money to more destructive acts like setting fires, stealing wagons or boats, and assassinations.

Contacting Sabran and his band was best done by leaving a message at one of the higher class taverns in Westgate or Selgaunt and waiting to be contacted by one of their agents.

History
Sabran was born in the . He was exiled from Cormyr for killing or attempting to kill several nobles during the Abraxus affair in the .

## Sammaster's conjunction

Sammaster's conjunction was a powerful spell that allowed a caster to cast two spells at the same time.

Effects
This spell had two methods for combining spells. The first method combined spells. When cast, the spell created an oval column of blue light  in height. This appeared directly in front of the caster. The caster then cast two spells into the column of light, which then vanished. When needed, the caster repeated the last words of this spell, which then cast both spells simultaneously.

The second method conjoined spells. During casting, the caster choose two memorized spells to be conjoined. Afterwards, when the caster cast one of the conjoined spells, the other one was cast as well.

Casting
In addition to verbal and somatic components, the spell required a material component: a transparent quartz crystal with faceted sides and a hair from one of the Chosen of Mystra.

History
This spell was known to be used by wizards of the Cult of the Dragon.

## Sahar

Sahar was a special form of magic practiced with colored stones taken from the Genies' Anvil in the High Desert of Zakhara.

Usage
Each stone needed to have a smooth side and a rough side to be suitable. The stone's color symbolized different aspects: black for land, blue for sea, white for woman, red for man, brown for camel. The evil eye could be cast by crushing a white or red stone while saying a person's name. Many other effects could be produced by combining different stones and uttering appropriate phrases. Casting the stone like dice allowed the practitioner to read the future. These predictions could be extremely accurate depending on the skill of the user.

This magic was used exclusively by female dwarves inhabiting the village of Yarrat in the High Desert of Zakhara.

## Saja Stillwater

Saja Stillwater was a bloodforge mage in the city of Konigheim in the Utter East during the time of the Bloodforge Wars (648–657 DR).

Saja headed a benevolent order that watched over Konigheim. They were capable combatants and nigh invulnerable whilst occupying the keep. However, when yet another catastrophic fire swept through the city, Saja's order were drawn out into the streets. The leader of the Legendary Campaign seized the opportunity to strike in a bloodforge battle.

## Sandling

Sandlings were kin to earth elementals and native to the Elemental Plane of Earth.

Description
Sandlings typically measured  in diameter. They were composed of silicates and resembled mounds of sand though they could change their color to blend in with their surroundings.

Behavior
Sandlings were known to intensely dislike being in the presence of most forms of organic matter.

Biology
[[File:Sandling-MCVII.png|thumb|273px|A sandling wanders the desert, with three palm trees in the distance.]]
Their body temperatures matched that of their typical surroundings, making them invisible to any creature that saw through infravision.

Combat
These creatures were not aggressive and would only attack if provoked or stepped upon. Given their resemblance to their surroundings, surprise was usually on their side. They were able to envelop two human-sized creatures at a time. They could strike with an abrasive appendage.

If  of water was poured onto a sandling it had the same effect as a slow spell.

Sandlings were immune to all sleep, hold, and charm spells as well as other mind-affecting attacks.

Ecology
Sandlings laid claim to territories that were only recognizable to other sandlings. Aside from territorial conflicts, they had no societal interactions with others of their kind.

These creatures reproduced by producing buds. A territory of an adult sandling could have thousands of smaller bud sandlings in the same area, but those who did not find their own territories after reaching a diameter of  risked being consumed by the parent.
Diet
Sandlings fed on minerals within the earth.
Habitats
Sandlings were known to be found in the deserts of Maztica, the Raurin desert, and the Quoya Deserts

Beyond Toril, sandlings inhabited the many deserts of the planet Oerth.

## Saraala Ithal

Queen Saraala Ithal was the first queen of Tethyr.

Relationships
Saraala was the eldest daughter of the chieftain of Clan Tarseth of the so-called "barbarian" clans of Tethyr, but her mother was an elf, the daughter of Selanlar of the Tethir tribe, who was allied with the humans against Calimshan. This made Saraala a half-elf.

Saraala fell in love with and married the new king of Tethyr Darrom Ithal of Clan Ithal, becoming the country's first queen.

Darrom and Saraala had two sons and a daughter. The first son was named after his father; the second son was named Silvam.

Her brother, Amir Tarseth I, became chieftain of Clan Tarseth after her father.

History
Saraala married King Darrom in the . Since Saraala was a member of both Clan Tarseth and tribe Tethir, the union helped maintain the unity of the fledgling country.

Saraala bore three children in the first four years of their marriage: first Darrom II, then Silvam in, and finally a daughter in . Sadly, little Darrom died from a fever in .

In the, Clan Bormul finished construction of a palace for the royal family, Ithalyr, located on the coast in the Purple Hills region. The queen moved with the rest of the court from Ithmong to this new location.

In the month of Eleasis of the, her own brother, Chieftain Amir I, became so angry with a decision made by her husband to pacify the elves of the Forest of Tethir that he orchestrated the murder of her husband by teleporting a deepspawn into his throne room.

In, during the reign of her son Silvam, Saraala was strongly opposed to his decision to appoint his cousin's wife Zahyra Bardson-Ithal as Tethyr's first court vizera.

## Sage spirit

The sage spirit haunted the forest trail near Myth Drannor.

Description
The sage spirit wore a gold headband with a red gem, and an emerald-green cloak with gold woven patterns. He carried a golden magical staff. He had dark blue eyes, was bald, and had white facial hair.

History
Around 1358 DR, the Heroes of Waterdeep navigated in the deep areas of the Forest Trail on the way to Myth Drannor, but their path was blocked by very thick trees. They were met by the ghostly form of a sage in a clearing, who was angered by the felling of some trees and threatened to kill them. If persuaded to reveal his lore, the spirit revealed that Myth Drannor was once an elven settlement, followed by its prosperity and its downfall. He then revealed that he'd died on the day that Myth Drannor was overwhelmed by monsters. He then gave a test to prove their worthiness of passing to Myth Drannor, which if failed would only make the pathway to Myth Drannor permanently blocked. If the test was passed, he unblocked the way, but told them not to cut down any more trees.

## Sanctuary (rune)

Sanctuary was a type of giant rune that created a barrier in doorways and portals. It was considered to be the reverse of the imprisonment rune.

Effects
Once activated, this rune made it impossible for creatures to enter through its inscribed door or portal by mundane. However, creatures could still exit these barriers and they could be bypassed by translocation spells, such as teleport and dimension door. The rune's effect lasted for a duration of days equal to that of the caster's level.

Unlike the imprisonment rune, there was no associated "freedom word" function for prematurely deactivating a sanctuary rune's barrier.

Components
Like most giant runes, it required no material components to cast, though the caster needed to know the giant rune triumph. The process of shaping this rune could take anywhere from one to eight days and it needed to be inscribed upon a door or portal.

## Salt (cantrip)

Salt was a useful cantrip that neophyte wizards could learn. It conjured a sprinkling of finely ground salt above a target creature or object.

Effects
This cantrip produced an amount of salt sufficient to preserve a barrel of fish, flavor a large kettle of stew, or treat a small plot of ground for weed prevention. In practical terms, it was enough to apply to about  of foodstuffs or  of turf. Smaller targets could easily become over-salted without proper application of this spell, and larger objects might receive an insufficient amount for the desired effect. Giant slugs and other creatures susceptible to contact with salt could be slightly injured by a clever application of this cantrip. The caster could target an object or creature within .

Components
This cantrip required verbal and somatic components to cast. The caster made a sprinkling gesture with one hand while making a smacking noise with their lips.

## Samuel Thunderburp

Samuel Thunderburp was a retired adventurer and the innkeep of the Five Flagons in Athkatla, during the mid–14 century DR.

Description
Samuel had a reputation as a being one of the greatest fighters in all of Highdale.

Personality
He was a friendly man who offered a warm welcome to those who entered his establishment. He liked to boast that the Five Flagons offered every beverage that could be found throughout the entirety of Faerûn.

Relationships
Samuel ran the Five Flagons with his partner Thalia.

He was well-acquainted with a number of Amnish adventurers. Among his friends the brave Truesword Mazzy Fentan, as well as the actor–adventurers Haer'Dalis and Raelis Shai.

Before he settled down in Athkatla, Samuel spent considerable time as an adventurer. In the end, Thalia managed to pull him away from that life and find one that was considerably less dangerous.

## Sausage

A sausage was a collection of seasoned minced meat, fat, and/or offal that had been packed into a stadium-shaped container, such as a pig's bladder. They were sold in Faerûn and in the Underdark.

Description

Sausages were made and sold all over Faerûn, particularly in Berdusk, Cormyr, the Dalelands, the Delimbiyr Vale, the Dessarin Valley, Khôltar, Longsaddle, Myrinjar, Silverymoon, the Sword Coast North, Tethyr, the Vast, Sembia, and Waterdeep. They were also popular elements of dwarven and halfling cuisine.

Sausages from the Western Heartlands were stuffed with copious amounts of garlic and cooked in mulled wine. Rabbit sausages were common in Rashemen. In the Sword Coast North, boar sausage was made using every piece of the animals' meat save for the trotters and tail cuts. Sembians were known exporters of spiced sausages. They were also produced and sold in the Underdark, such as in Menzoberranzan.

Sausage stalls were common sight during the Festival of Blades in Colletro of the Blade Kingdoms. In Cormyr, sausages were sold sold as street food in places like Arabel, and slices of cold sausage were sometimes served as handfood at taverns alongside grapes, biscuits, and cheeses.

Dried sausages were carried as provisions by sailors in the Moonshae Isles.

Rumors & Legends
Circa, it was discovered that Barnall Ostever of Longsaddle had been including orc and goblin meat in his sausages, leading to a legacy of dark jokes and refusal to buy any sausage from him in the years after the scandal. As of the late 15 century DR, it was said that the sausages made by the Ostever family had greatly improved, though customers were warned to "mind the tusks".

Notable Products

 Arrabaran morningfeast included sauced scrambled eggs, hand-shredded bread, and baked fish, stuffed with potatos and sausages.
 Braundlae's Best in Suzail served sausage rolls coated in silver dragon sauce.
 Beldabar's Rest in Yartar served sausages that were  long.
 Sembian wild boar sausages were stuffed with meat as well as sweet potatoes, from the creatures' own stomachs.
 Thimmer, a food product that included stripped boar meat and diced sausages.
 Corm Orp sausage, a product made from pork, squirrel meat, and ground nuts.
 Oak-Father's Boon inn in Mosstone of Tethyr served mint-scented wine sausages.
 The Old Troll's Foot in Kirinwood, Cormyr served local wild board sausages flavored with herbs (although for many, they were too herbaceous).
 Wyrmkindler Sausage Works in Cormyr was known for many fanciful-sounding sausages such as "Best Hedgehog", "Long-Simmered Snake", and "Old Wolf" (all of which they claim were not named for their ingredients) as well as for their more ordinary-named "Stag Sausage" made of venison and pork.
 Delmuth, the proprietor of Delmuth's Barrel general store in Hap, sold saveloy, a dried sausage made of heavily-spiced pigs' brains.
 The village of Halfcrag was known for spicy fried sausages on account of how satisfyingly greasy their product was.
 Salami was a type of large preserved and dried sausage available in the Western Heartlands and Baldur's Gate.
 Sausages at the Fallen Tower tavern in Neverwinter were notably spicy and salty in order to encourage diners to order more drink.
 Gelgur's Feast of Plenty south of Neverwinter was known for several kinds of sausage, including boar, duck-liver, and "upcountry dark" (filled with highly spiced blends of boar and red meat, mostly game).
 Aumador Uldon of Uldon's Cleaver in Silverymoon was known for his garlicky bird-meat sausages and his pepper sausages, the latter of which was especially popular among dwarves.
 The Inn of the Dripping Dagger in Waterdeep sold sausages drenched in sauce, and customers could specify how spicy they wanted their sauce to be.
 Smallfowl sausage, sausage created by the dwarves of Faerûn using minced meat from pheasants, grouse, goose, and duck.

Trivia
 In 1372 DR, there was a goblin-run sausage stand in Menzoberranzan.
 Sausages could be magically sliced with the cut cantrip.
 Roliver Thynd, proprietor of the Silver Taproom in Essembra, hated sausage, and it was thus the only handfood conspicuously absent from his extensive menu.

## Sacaanti

A sacaanti was a type of creature that came about as a result of magical creation. It quite quite similar to an imp or a quasit, appearing as an amalgamation of the two. They were made by wizards of the Zhentarim to be utilized as familiars.

Description
Sacaanti very much resembled small fiends. They had leathery, bat-like wings and their body was noticeably tailless.

Unlike either of their progenitor species, Sacaanti were entirely devoted to their master-creators. Each pair of master and familiar enjoyed a strong telepathic link and emotional bond with one another. If either of them were to die, the other would be devastated.

Abilities
They possessed a number of innate powers such as regeneration, the ability to "become ethereal" whenever they chose, as well as the capability to perform spellcasting.

Combat
Sacaanti most readily attacked others using their claws or venomous bite. While they were known to cast spells in battle, Sacaanti possessed a unique characteristic: their masters could cast spells through them, using their senses and position.

Ecology
In order to create a sacaanti, a mage needed to gather both an imp and a quasit in the same room. Several spells were involved in the creature's formation, including two castings of polymorph and a specifically-worded version of the wish spell.

History
The first specimens were created in Zhentarim laboratories some time during the 13 century DR.

References

## Sanasha

Sanasha was an apprentice wizard who served a Zhentarim necromancer in Athkatla during the mid–14 century DR.

Relationships
Along with her comrade Prebek, Sanasha served the mad necromancer Xzar.

Possessions
Sanasha adorned herself in a mage robe of cold resistance and was armed with a simple quarterstaff. She carried several spell scrolls on her person.

History
As of the, the trio were conducting experiments to create hideous little creatures that resembled goblins. Both young mages were jumpy and wary of intruders, apparently, at least on one occasion, Xzar sent intruders to Prebek's house in the Wave District as a test. Unbeknownst to the Zhents, they were being investigate by the Athkatlan Harpers under the leadership of Galvarey. Prebek, Sanasha, and their "creations" may have been ordered dead by Harper Rylock.

## Salander

Salander was a wizard member of the Zhentarim in the mid-14 century DR.Dragon #362 provides an exact date for the events of Halls of the High King, which did not give an exact date within itself.

Description
Salander was a notably handsome man, with blond hair and a wispy beard. His eyes gave the impression of being sharp and alert.

Personality
Salander was known to be ruthless when fighting and was very eager to prove his worth to the Zhentarim.

Abilities
Salander was known to be capable of casting the spells blindness. dispel magic, Evard's black tentacles, invisibility, magic missile, spider climb, and web.

Possessions
While guarding the gate in Voonlar he was known to possess a dagger, darts laced with sleeping poison, and a potion of gaseous form.

Due to his reputation within the organization, Salander lacked the teleport ring that wizards of a 7 level or higher were usually bestowed.

History
Salander began his career in the Zhentarim as an apprentice to the archmage Olorim at the Citadel of the Raven. He would go on to serve the organization as a caravan guard in the Stonelands, until being stationed in the Darkhold.

In 1359 DR, Salander was living inside the town of Voonlar, tasked by his superiors with guarding a gate that had been recently constructed by Gauntather, then-leader of the Risen Cult of Bane, with aid from Manshoon. This gate was in a windowless, unfurnished room and it connected to the House of the Black Hand. Salander was stationed in a room adjacent to this one, while inside it was guarded by nine ju-ju zombies and two bonebats.

Salander was given very little information regarding purpose of his task. If the area were to get breached and the situation turn dire, he was instructed to allow a bonebat to escape so that it could report the situation to Zhentil Keep. If hounded by such intruders, he was to drink his potion of gaseous form, then retreat to the nearby Blushing Beauty inn and report to its innkeeper, Ralagas Therim.

Relationships
Due due to something that transpired prior to 1359 DR, many within the Zhentarim considered Salander to be overly ambitious and deceitful. He was especially mistrusted by his fellow Zhentarim wizard Sememmon and he in turn harbored resentment towards him.

## Sash

Sashes were a type of accessory garment that could be found all across the Realms.

Description
Sashes came in a variety of colors and were typically made of either linen, silk, or wool. Occasionally they were woven from more rare or exotic materials, such as samite or shimmerweave.

They were often used to fasten an article of clothing in place, much like a belt. Such clothing included abas, caftans, dishdashahs, gowns, kimonos, and robes. Some were known to conceal weaponry in their sashes, such as chains, kyoketsuogi, and shurikens.

Availability
 Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue was known to sell linen, silk, and wool sashes in a variety of colors.
 Chalasse's Fine Clothing in Immersea sold luxury cummerbunds.
 Bazaars in the land of Zakhara sold sashes that, in the mid to late-14 century DR, could be purchased for around 3 to 1 silver pieces depending upon how good they were at bargaining.

History
In the year 1352 DR, the samurai Guiji Yui, then-shoya of the village Osata in the Fochu Peninsula, executed a farmer in front of a large crowd for having been caught wearing a silk obi. The man had been in violation of the Keian no Furegaki (a set of laws for farmers), which mandated that farmers were forbidden from wearing any form of silk clothing.

Varieties
Obi silk sashes unique to the continent of Kara-Tur.
Tale sash closely related to ballad cords, were a variety of sashes used by minstrels to aid in the memorization of stories and sometimes shown to a close-up audience. Typically made of cloth, but occasionally leather, a tale sash featured a sequence of symbols and/or actual pictures on it that were either drawn, stitched, or burned into the material. In the case of pictures, a tale sash might depict faces of characters or creatures and certain locales. besides being a memory aid, tale sashes were used to carry coded messages, with certain hues being used for the symbols and pictures to denote a hidden meaning. Additionally, some tale sashes carried enchantments or were made to store spells, but in order to activate their stored spell a tale sash needed a rune inscribed token pressed against a matching rune on the sash.

Notable Sashes
 Sash of charisma: a type of enchanted, fiery-red silk sash with a golden buckle emblazoned with the image of a peacock.
 Sash of fire resistance: a type of enchanted, red silk sash brocaded with platinum thread that was unique to the region of Zakhara.
 Sash of heat endurance: another type of enchanted sash that was unique to the region of Zakhara. They were red and made of silk, but were brocaded with with gold thread.
 Sash of protection
 Sash of Sune: a unique ancient, enchanted scarlet sash that functioned much like a spellbook. Though only a priest of Sune could tap into its power.
 Sashling: a type of elven sash that had ten pockets sewn into its folds.
 Shimmering sash: a type of enchanted, brightly colored sash unique to the region of Zakhara that projected an effect similar to blur.
 Sunite sash an enchanted sash that enabled a wearer to cast handfire.
 Whip sash: a unique double-layered silk and leather sash from Zakhara that doubled as a form of weapon.

Notable Wearers of Sashes
General
 Throughout most of the Realms, black gowns paired with a matching sash and boots were a typical cheap form of "wealthy" attire.
Classes
 Gold elf wizards in Evermeet often wore flax sashes that were woven in an intricate knotwork.
 Wizards in the nation of Calimshan often wore silk sashes, while those in Turmish were known to favor intricately woven sashes.

Groups
 Among the Bedine tribes of Anauroch, men commonly wore wide sashes.
 Members of the Brotherhood of the True Flame were known for making and wearing both the sashes of fire resistance and the sashes of heat endurance.
 Members of the Council of Farmer-Grocers were known to wear vibrant green sashes.
 In Miresk's School of Thaumaturgy a wizard's rank was typically denoted by whether they wore a white, gray, or black sash.
 The Red Sashes, despite their name, were not known to wear sashes. Though they sometimes tied up wanted criminals with red sashes.
 In the mercenary company Riders of Nesmé, sashes of both a gold and gray hue were a standard part of a member's uniform.
 The Witches of Rashemen typically fastened their hooded robes by wearing an orange sash around their waist.

Individuals
 Achtel Gnarlfingers typically wore a bright yellow sash alongside her colorful robes and red cloak.
 Ajax Firestorm
 Akura al-Hiyali favored pairing brightly colored sashes alongside his black ensemble.
 The angel Al Dimeneira was known to wear no clothing except for a long, white sash.
 Aviss typically wore a red sash across her waist.
 Callanter Rollingshoulder
 Endeir Falconhand
 The drow wizard Filzaur was known to wear a yellow sash as part of his gaudy attire.
 Giogioni Wyvernspur
 The drow magic-user Kilili typically wore a red sash across their waist.
 Haka Kinnori
 Hiro Yun
 Horaundoon: the elf-murdering archmage of Zhentarim circa 1348 DR.
 Nar-Aidiya
 The warlord Orndar Oulamn Merendil was known to wear a bright red sash.
 The wizard Presto typically fastened his green robes with a golden sash.
 The stylish halfling Regis often wore a blue sash and continued to do so after being reincarnated as Spider Parrafin.
 Rolf Trygveson was known to wear two to three brightly colored sashes alongside his lightweight clothes.
 Shadowheart

Races & Sentient Creatures
 Alhoons were often known to wear red sashes.
Regions & Settlements
 The noble djinni who inhabited the Citadel of Ice and Steel were renowned for making exceptionally beautiful silk sashes.
 In the nation of Calimshan, nearly every citizen could be seen wearing a sash, though only members of the ruling class were allowed to wear sashes that were of gold color or woven with golden threads. In both its army and navy, a reddish gold and silvery-gold sash were two of the most prestigious decorations anyone could receive.
 In the nation of Cormyr, it was considered a custom for young women to indicate their availability and search for a husband by wearing a purple sash in public.
 Colorful sashes were known to be popular among people all across the Corsair Domains.
 In the city of Delzimmer, orange sashes served as the role of a badge for local law enforcement.
 The town of Felshroun's company of rangers, the Foresters, were known to wear maroon sashes across their midriffs that bore their company symbol.
 In Shou Lung's famous martial arts tournament, Komite, sashes were part of standard tournament attire. Depending upon where a fighter ranked by the end they would recieve either a white, red, or black silk sash that was embroidered with the Emperoro's seal.
 Shou Lung's imperial soldiers were known to wear brown sashes emblazoned with an emblem of a jade hammer.
 In the land of Zakhara, waist sashes were common among all social classes. Using them to store money, tobacco, or weapons.  Each of the Free Cities of Zakhara had an official color that was represented in the clothing of most inhabitants, including sashes. For instance, the inhabitants of Muluk were known to wear purple sashes, those of Utaqa wore white, and those of Hafaya wore black.  In the Free City of Liham, red was the unofficial color and was considered fashionable for sashes.

Religions
 In the plane of Automata, white sashes were a common article of clothing among petitioners.
 The clergy of Bane always wore sashes around their waists in a variety of colors, each emblazoned with his holy symbol, to denote their rank within the Church of Bane. The lowest-ranking priests wore black sashes, those of higher-tier wore orange, senior priests wore red, and senior officers wore purple.
 The clergy of Dugmaren Brightmantle typically wore vibrant sashes with the width of a hand.
 Adventuring priests of Eldath often wore grey sashes alongside their leaf-green robes.
 The clergy of Gond typically wore crimson, leather sashes over either shoulder that ended in large pouches, even when wearing armor. These were covered in a variety of tools and equipment that Gondsmen felt could come in handy at any time.
 The clergy of Ibrandul typically wore black sashes across the waists of their dark purple robes. The adventuring clergy of Xvim were also known to wear blash sashes, though with green piping as trim.
 The clergy of Leira typically wore sashes of a russet or ochre hue, lined and streaked with green, alongside bottom-fringed robes made of the same material.
 The clergy of both Myrkul and Tyr were typically known to wear white sashes.
 The clergy of Sehanine Moonbow often wore silver-laced sashes.
 The clergy of Umberlee were known to wear sashes of mottled blue and green.
 The specialty priests of Chauntea, Lifewardens, often wore a green sash around their left arm.
 The specialty priests of Tempus, Battleforges, typically wore a blood-red sash diagonally across their plate armor chests, spanning the shoulders and dropping down to the hips.
 Waukeen, the goddess of merchants and currency, was said to commonly wear a sash that was spun from gold.

Trivia
 A red sash was featured in the noble family Irlingstar's coat of arms.

## Sated Satyr

The Sated Satyr was a tavern in the Sea Ward of Waterdeep in the .

Location
The building was located at the junction of Trollkill Street and the Street of the Singing Dolphin, directly in front of the North Tower (also known as "The Trolltower").

Structure
The Sated Satyr was a two-story, irregularly shaped building that fronted the streets or the alley on all sides but one. It adjoined the building next to it on Trollkill Street.

## Saladrex

Saladrex was an especially vain and prideful red dragon.

Description
He was old enough that he considered a few years to be a minuscule amount of time.

Personality
Saladrex viewed himself in the most grandiose terms. He enjoyed being referred to by various honorifics and was not above responding well to flattery.

He would refer to humanoid adventurers as "puny".

Possessions
He had a particularly desirable hoard, which was sought after by many adventurers. Among his treasures was the Staff of the Ram, a unique staff of striking.

History
Some time during the 1360s DR, Saladrex made his lair within the 4 level of the Watcher's Keep. He was summoned to the keep by the "Lord of the Level" Carston, by means of the Machine of Lum the Mad.

Saladrex remained within the keep because of the amusement he found in Carston's antics. He was particularly delighted by the endless battle fought by the githyanki and illithids who each had encampments nearby.

## Sand cat

Sand cats were a species of felines found in deserts.

Description
Sand cats were slightly larger than a house cat. Their fur was colored in a way that allowed them to blend in with the desert. Their most distinguishing physical features were their large ears, each ending in a long tuft of white fur.

Biology
Sand cats had the ability to jump  vertically and  horizontally. Sand cats were incredibly stealthy and their senses were very sensitive, making it near impossible to sneak up on one.

Combat
A sand cat attacked prey by pouncing on its target, pinning the prey with its teeth and front claws and raking the belly with its sharp rear claws. Sand cats only attacked larger creatures such as humanoids if they were cornered and retreated at the first chance.

Ecology
Sand cats were social creatures and lived together in familial groups called dens. Each den had two adults and their kittens. Sand cats mated once a year, and the parents remained with the kittens until they reached maturity, which usually took up to ten months.
Diet
Sand cats were obligate carnivores and nocturnal hunters. They played an essential part in their local areas, as they kept the population of rodents and vermin under control.
Habitats
Sand cats lived in small caves or burrows in deserts or steppes. They were territorial and hunted an area with a range of up to . They were commonly encountered in the Hordelands. They could also be found around the Battle of Bones battlefield in the Western Heartlands.

Usage
Sand cats could be somewhat domesticated and were kept as pets. They were trained as kittens to hunt prey for their owner. Sand cat kittens were gifted to the leaders of human tribes. A sand cat kitten could be bought for up to 2,000 gp at a market.

References

## Saber-toothed tiger

Saber-toothed tigers, also known as smilodons, were an ancient dire species of cat whose existence persisted in some places. Despite their name, these creatures were in fact not closely related to tigers and were only distantly related to the more modern feline species of the Realms.

Description
These creatures were twice as large as any lion. Their roars were likewise twice as loud as that of any lion. Two large, curved fangs jutted out from their mouths, upon which their name was derived. On average these measured around  in length.

Behavior
These creatures were very aggressive and ferocious predators, though were known to sometimes ignore prey that remained motionless.

Combat
Saber-toothed tigers rarely ever pursued prey over long distances. Often they would attack in groups, ambushing their prey. One half of a pride would startle and chase their prey, steering them straight into the direction of the other half.

History
In 640 DR, the people of Ormath and Lheshayl, as well as patrols from the Shining Plains, experienced frequent attacks from saber-toothed tigers.

In the late 15 century DR, a man in Barovia by the name of Rictavio owned a saber-toothed tiger. Around that same time, Queen Bjornhild Solvigsdottir of the Tribe of the Tiger had a pet saber-toothed tiger named Grava.

Ecology
These creatures lived in prides ruled over by female members and worked as a group, much like lions. Infighting over dominance, prey, territory, and mates was quite common. At times this could be quite lethal.

Weaker members of prides were often driven out in pairs, forcing them to set up their own hunting territories.

Diet
Saber-toothed tigers were a carnivorous species.

These creatures were most often known to prey upon giant elks, brantas, glyptodons, horses, megaloceros, and mammoths.

Habitats
These creatures were typically found in the forests of either warm or cold climate regions, as well as plains and hills. They laired within caves or small earthen dens when bearing and rearing their young.

Smilodons could be found in the Ice Spires, the Shining Plains, and much of the Savage Frontier, In the late-15 century DR, they could be found in the jungles of Chult near the River Soshenstar, where they were mistaken by locals for a variety of Chultan tiger.

Beyond Toril, they inhabited the warm marshes and swamps of the planet Oerth.

Relationships
Saber-toothed tigers were sometimes domesticated by neanderthals. In the Ice Spires region, both frost giants and the Ice Spire ogres of Dour Fissure were known to have domesticated smilodons.

Some arctic rangers were known to have smilodons as animal companions.

Usage
The fangs of smilodons were a source of ivory, primarily fossilized ivory.

Summoning
A saber-toothed tiger could be summoned by the conjure animals spell.

Trivia
 The spirit conjured by the find greater steed spell could take the form of a saber-toothed tiger.

## Salt mummy

Salt mummies were a form of undead that arose from the bodies of those that died carrying a great sin, the horror of which gradually created a linkage to the Negative Energy plane, and were buried too close to veins of salt.

Description
Salt mummies were humanoid figures, typically  tall and weighing around . Their bodies were so minerally encrusted and dried out from the salt they were buried near that it was impossible to determine what race they were in life.

Abilities
Salt mummies were supernaturally arid. They uniquely possessed the ability to evaporate moisture from anything they touched, even plants and water elementals.

All forms of water harmed salt mummies as though they were holy water.

Ecology
Salt mummies were typically found either solitarily, in pairs, or in clusters of five to nine individuals.
Habitats
Salt mummies were known to haunt some of the larger salt caverns beneath Faerûn. Most notably they could be found in the Buried Realms near the ruins of Tethyamar, beneath the Raurin desert in the Deep Raurin, and the Darklands. In particular, the area of the Darklands near the Troglodyte Warrens of Traaskl Thorog, beneath Chessenta.

## Safeguarding

Safeguarding was an abjuration spell used by wizards.

Effects
The spell protected both the caster and anyone standing near them from harm caused by rebounding spell effects from spells cast by the caster. Safeguarding protected from these rebounding effects from all but the most powerful of spells. However, it did not offer protection from spells rebounded by spell turning, nor did it protect from spells cast by anyone but the caster. This spell was extremely useful if the caster desired to cast a harmful spell in a tight area. The protective aura remained centered on the caster even if they moved about. Safeguarding would protect against area of effect spells provided they were centered outside of the safeguarding's range.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required a piece of preserved skin from a creature that possessed natural resistance to magic to cast.

Salve of undead repair was a type of magical concoction used by the necromancer drow of House Freth.

Description
Salve of undead repair was usually kept in small ceramic jars. The salve itself was a goo of green color.

Powers
Salve of undead repair was used by drow necromancers to heal and repair hurt and damaged undead shells they controlled. Interestingly enough, the salve worked not only when applied to undead, but also when used on the living. Its unpleasant smell kept most living from actually using it on themselves.

In 1369 DR, some of the salve of undead repair was left on a table in a room full of mohrgs alongside with a pair of gauntlets of necromancy deep in the Elven Catacombs under Castle Cormanthor in Myth Drannor. The deeper levels were taken over by the drow of House Freth whose necromancers used these items to control the ancient undead in the catacombs.

## Salatis

Salatis was a senator in the city-state of Innarlith in the mid-to-late 14 century DR. He became an ally of the Red Wizard Marek Rymüt, who used him to overthrow Ransar Osorkon, the ruler of the city-state in 1365 DR. Following this coup, Salatis himself became Ransar of Innarlith.  He himself was killed when Senator Pristoleph used an army to take power for himself in 1371 DR.

Description
He was tall at a height of . He had a full head of grey hair.

Personality
Salatis had a tendency to change his devotion from one deity to another very quickly. He was very religious, but often didn't follow a particular deity for long before he took up the faith of another one.

Relationships
Marek Rymüt, acting on behalf of the Red Wizards of Thay, effectively controlled Salatis once he became Ransar of Innarlith, albeit clandestinely. Salatis was well aware that he required Marek's assistance in order to keep his position in control of the city.

Among Salatis's allies on the senate included senators Inthelph and Meykhati.

History
In 1364 DR, Salatis and a group of other senators made a deal to advance Willem Korvan to the senate of Innarlith after the latter had secretly assassinated Senator Khonsu.

Following these events, Ransar Osorkon made plans to begin construction of a canal that would connect the Lake of Steam to the Nagaflow and thus connect the Sea of Fallen Stars to the oceans of Toril. The Red Wizards would not profit from this project, as it would have greatly hurt their control of the magical portal trade that previously had been used to transport ships between either body of water.

In 1365 DR, Salatis was transported by Marek Rymüt to the Red Wizard's pocket dimension, the Land of One Hundred and Thirteen. There he was shown an army of black firedrakes that Marek had created. Marek explained to Salatis that he intended to give the drakes to Salatis to use in order to become Ransar and in return Salatis was to obey any directions that Marek gave to him while he served as Ransar in Innarlith. Salatis agreed to the plan.

Not long after this, Salatis and some of his allies in the senate began openly questioning and challenging Ransar Osorkon in the senate chamber regarding the canal project.

On Highharvestide in 1365 DR, civil unrest erupted and Salatis took the opportunity to stage a coup against Osorkon. Osorkon and his guards kept within the Ransar's office in the Palace of Many Spires during the unrest. The black firedrakes, which had been polymorphed into human form as a disguise, besieged the palace and broke into the Ransar's office. Salatis arrived and called on Osorkon to surrender, but Osorkon insisted on giving battle. The polymorphed black firedrakes breathed flaming acid on him and his guards, thereby killing them.

Salatis then received confirmation from the senate to become the new Ransar of Innarlith. However, he didn't end the canal project, as he saw the benefit of it to the city-state, despite the harm it would have provided to the Red Wizards and Marek's dislike for the project. Marek eventually changed his position on the canal as he realized it would be impossible to stop it from being completed, since the idea had already been put out and embraced by the Innarlan public, regardless of who Marek was able to control in the city-state's government. Marek succeeded in getting Salatis to approve, however, of allowing for the establishment of a Thayan enclave in Innarlith. He then decided to use the canal project to the advantage of the Red Wizards by getting the canal builders to use Thayan magical items or services to help build the canal.

Salatis got the senate to dismiss Ivar Devorast from his position as chief engineer in constructing the canal and put Willem Korvan, another ally of Marek Rymüt, in his place.

Senator Pristoleph desired to make himself Ransar of Innarlith and to that effect, he gained control of Firesteap Citadel and built an army there with the intention of taking control of the city. Salatis attempted to assassinate Pristoleph by sending the assassin T'juyu in the guise of a water naga but was unsuccessful. Eventually, Pristoleph invaded and laid siege to the city. Marek betrayed Salatis and helped Pristoleph gain power. Salatis was murdered during the siege in 1371 DR in the Palace of Many Spires by the same black firedrake bodyguards that had killed Ransar Osorkon during Salatis's coup of 1365 DR.

## Saelmur

Saelmur was a city-state in the Land of the Lions region of south Faerûn, on the northern shores of the Lake of Steam.

Geography
The city-state was sat in a natural bay in the Lake of Steam, rising upwards to the crests of four hills, which were capped with various columns, towers, and spires. It was connected to two roads, the Coast Road connecting it eastward to the city-state of Ankhapur and Tyrant's Road connecting it westward to the city-state of Mintar.

Government
The city-state was ruled by the Councilium of Prelates, a governing council made up of the high priests of Saelmur's seven largest faiths – Chauntea, Helm, Ilmater, Lathander, Oghma, Torm, and Tyr.

Trade
Saelmur produced a variety of fruits and vegetables from the fields and orchards around the city, though as a port city it also produced a great deal of seafood. It produced a variety of different minerals, some of which were hard-to-find and could be made into spell components, including salt, sulfur and the magical zardazil. Other products of the city-state included dyes and inks.

In the late 14 century DR, as the city housed an influx of refugees from Mintar, many of its equipment shops were poorly stocked as demands for all manner of items skyrocketed. Expensive items could be ordered, but the wait for them was often weeks long.

Defenses
The armed forces of Saelmur were the Bresmer, who numbered 3,000 by 1370 DR. They patrolled the city, the tent city outside its walls, and the neighboring vegetable fields and orchards.
Crime
Saelmur was typically so well managed by the Councilium that thieves' guild would be uncovered and broken up by the Besmer before they could fully form. However, some criminals managed to take advantage of the city's strained resources in 1370 DR. The strongest of the groups to take a foothold were the Scarlet Throats, who made most of their money by running a very lucrative black market.

History
Circa -5100 DR, Saelmur began life as one of many garrisoned fortresses that were established by the armies of the First Kingdom of Mir. Sometime in the 25 years following the Mirran army's defeat at the hands of Jhaamdath in, on the plains north of the tiny fishing town of Ankhapur, Saelmur along with the other Mirran fortresses were overtaken by the Jhaamdathan army as they pushed the Mirrans all the way back to garrisons at Fort Saradush.

In, after a year long siege on their eastern fronts, the leaders of Mir and Coramshan came together for negotiation and came to conclude that only unification of their states would ensure their survival against Jhaamdath. United under the rulership of Ukhar IV, the Coramshite-Mirran army unleashed a devastating counterattack against the Jhaamdathans and within two months pushed them all the way back to Saelmur. The fighting continued on into, with Jhaamdath losing Saelmur. Eventually a truce was reached between the two nations, with the promise that both would abandon any claims over the Land of the Lions, leaving Saelmur and others in the region independent.

In the, Saelmur was struck by the Crimson Death and lost 16% of its population from the plague.

Centuries later, during the Eighth Age of Calimshan, Ankhapur tried to convince Saelmur and their neighbor Mintar to unite into a single country. However, these efforts failed for a multitude of regions, such as the general preference among Arnadens for local governance and independence, trade dis-agreements, and difficulties with defenses.

Over the centuries it developed a reputation as being one of the cleanest cities along the Lake of Steam.

Migrant workers from Saelmur, among other places, went to join in the construction of the canal that Innarlith was building between the Lake of Steam and the Nagaflow in the mid-1360s DR. This project was widely seen as bringing much lucrative trade to the Lake of Steam region, because it would have connected the Sea of Fallen Stars to the oceans of Toril through the Lake of Steam. When Innarlith's Ransar Salatis started to find excuses to cut funding for the project, Saelmur paid for its own workers to continue their work on the site.

In the years following Mintar's takeover by Teldorn Darkhope and his Knights of the Black Gauntlet in 1362 DR, many fled east from the city-state to seek refuge in Saelmur. Refugees packed the city's many inns and boarding houses, while others were taken in by the local temples. As the city grew too overcrowded to house further refugees, a tent city was erected outside its western walls. With Saelmur's population pushed beyond expected parameters its sewer system grew overwhelmed, leaving the once "cleanest city on the lake" to be dirty, smelly, and smoky.

In 1369 DR, as Mintar was faced with uprisings and strikes, Saelmar bolstered its defenses.

By the, the high priests of Helm, Lathander, Torm and Tyr – Genas Stoutshield, Santan Argath, Athelstan Meiados, and Thallon Geiros respectively – were in agreement that it was high time Saelmur fought Mintar and liberated it from the Church of Bane. And many mercenaries and sellswords made their way to Saelmur in hopes of being hired by the Councilium for this anticipated war, however, the cost of feeding and housing so many people taxed the city-state's coffers, making it difficult for Saelmur to pursue its aims of raising an army. And the churches were locked in debate over how best to deal with the Banite threat.

Thallon and others argued that Saelmur only stood a chance against Mintar if they could convince others to join them in the fray, such as the nations of Tethyr and Calimshan or the Shadi'ar tribes, particularly the Ala-min. Athelstan, however, grew increasingly impatient and threatened in multiple Councilium meetings to rally his Tormtar forces alone against the Banites.

In 1372 DR, though the churches were still locked in debate, Saelmar was working to organize a mutual-defense pact against Banite attacks with the other city-states in the Land of the Lions. The next year, in the midst of Sammaster's Rage of Dragons, the red great wyrm Balagos flew over the Land of the Lions and unleashed a firestorm in Mintar and Saelmur, killing over half of the population of both city-states.

Notable Locations
 The Central Market.
 The Needle in the Loft, a makeshift boarding house for refugees.
 The Knave's Nape, a high-class inn.
 Master of the Blade, a school of swordsmanship.
Religious
 Guardian Hall, a Helmite temple.
 The Hall of High Justice, a Tyrran temple.In Dragons of Faerûn it is stated that (as of 1373 DR) the hoard of the blue dragon Iryklathagra contained the "...onion dome of Anachtyr (gilded dome wrested from the top of a since-fallen temple in Saelmur...)". This implies that Tyr was worshiped in Saelmur under his aspect Anachtyr, though whether this referred to the Hall of High Justice or a preceding temple is unclear.
 The Lady's Sheaves, a Chauntean temple.
 The Library of High Justice, an Oghmanyte temple.
 The Shrine of Duty, a Tormtar temple.
 The Shrine of the Rising Sun, a Lathanderite temple.
 The Temple of the Bound Hands, an Ilmatari temple.

Inhabitants
The majority of the city-state's population was made up of humans, due in part to the influx of Mintaran refugees. It also long had a sizable number of mages, even prior to the Banite takeover of Mintar, due to the great access to mineral substance derived spell components. Many Mintaran mages made their home in Saelmar, hoping to one day return to the city-state.

Saelmar was also among the settlements in the Land of the Lions that the Shadi'ar tribes visited at least once a year, with their younger warriors taking the opportunity to indulge in city luxuries.

Notable Inhabitants
 The alchemist Surero fled here from Innarlith in the mid-1360s DR after he was excused for his attempt to murder the clandestine Red Wizard Marek Rymüt.

## Samblar's Swords

Samblar's Swords was a weapon smithy that operated in the city of Myth Drannor for hundreds of years.

Location
The shop was located on the Dragoneyes Street in the city's ward of Cormanthor, just north of Castle Cormanthor.

Structure
In 1369 DR, Samblar's Swords still stood even though damaged by the ravages of the Weeping War with its central tower shattered but still erect over the almost completely demolished second floor of the store. The tower stood supported by the thick enchanted cables of steel. Samblar himself placed the toughness enchantment on the cables.

The first floor of the structure housed Samblar's small forge he used to craft his goods.

Services
In the years before Myth Drannor opened itself up to non-elves, Samblar's only offered bladed weapons crafted in the fine, slender style of the Tel-quessir.

In the subsequent years, they began to craft and sell a wide variety of swords and other blades. A number of gnome and dwarf apprentice-smiths joined Samblar at his shop, offering contributions that led to new blades that rivaled the strength of those forged by dwarves.

History
The smithy opened up some time before the Opening of Myth Drannor, in the .

In, the ruins of Samblar's Swords were enveloped by the area of wild magic created during the fall of Myth Drannor. Only the smallest area of the building was not affected by the wild magic and that was the magical defensive ward placed by Samblar before his death in 714 DR on the vault door. That area was enchanted with the blade barrier spell that kept the creatures and looters away from the store. The ward could be deactivated by the command word lost to the time. Because of that the building still held precious magical arms created by Samblar somewhere inside.

Rumors and Legends
Adventurers claimed that the sound of hammer and anvil strikes could be heard coming from the store during nights of full moon. They told the stories of Samblar's ghost coming back to work during those times. Some even claimed to have talked to him and receiving gifts of weapons or the password to his vault.

## Saintsblood pearl

Saintsblood pearls were small round precious stones that looked like pearls, imbued with restorative magics.

Powers
One saintsblood pearl came with fifty charges of its healing magic and was able to cast a set of various spells. Each  spell consumed the different number of charges. Cure critical wounds could be cast at the expenditure of four charges, cure light wounds - one charge, cure moderate wounds - two charges, cure serious wounds - three, and restoration - five.

History
It was said that saintsblood pearls were droplets of blood that came from those pure of heart who were martyred for their worship. According to the stories, their spilled blood was as pure and white as fresh snow and hardened into these magical stones once exposed to the cold and cruel world. Saintsblood pearls were a way for the deceased saints to give their blessings to the fellow worshipers.

In 1372 DR, a saintsblood pearl could be purchased from the temple of Lathander, Moonglow Tower in Daggerford and another could be found among piles of curios in Torleth's Treasures in Gillian's Hill.

In 1374 DR, a wizard Vania, the unfortunate love interest of the man only known as the Collector from the Neverwinter's Blacklake District, was in possession of one of the saintsblood pearls.

## Sanrach Mountains

The Sanrach Mountains were the dominant geographical feature within Samarach on the Chultan Peninsula of southwest Faerûn.

Geography
The Sanrach Mountains isolated Samarach from Thindol in the east and Chult in the west. They fully encircled Samarach and the Sanrach Basin, stretching for hundreds of miles along the western, eastern, and northern border. Several passes, including Sorlmar Pass and Narubel Pass, allowed passage through the mountains. The mountains also served to halt the jungles of Chult from pressing further east.

Inhabitants
The heavily forested eastern slopes of the western arm of the Sanrach Mountains supported populations of blood apes and cloaked apes.

## Samargol

The Shining City of Samargol, also known as the City of Veils, was the capital city of Samarach. It guarded the entrance of the River Samar leading into the heart of the country.

Description
Samargol stood on the eastern bank of River Samar. It was a tropical city filled with lush greenery. The city's Samar Bay docks were the center of trade for the city and the entire country. Docks stood outside the city walls and had sharpshooter guards overlooking the district from atop the same walls. Samargol was home to proximately a quarter of Samarachan population.

Both males and females of Samargol often wore masks and veils in public. However, this was not the reason for the metropolis's name – the City of Veils. The name was in reference to numerous complex weaves of Nimbrese illusion magics that permeated Samargol. These spells made the city appear as nothing more than a hamlet with a humble keep in the middle. Once a visitor entered the city, the splendorous reality revealed itself – many called it the greatest city in Faerûn. However, that might also be an illusion.

A ring of fortified double walls encircled the city. The outer wall was built around the city's outer districts, while the inner wall protected a hilled area of Samargol – the home of nobility and the High Phantasmage's palace. The outer wall had three small gates that opened into the city. One gate was on the northern part of the wall and stood on the route that led directly to Taruin. The second gate faced the east and opened into the road towards Rassatan and Sorl. The third gate opened into the river allowing trade barges to enter and leave the city.

The inner wall had no gates, and only those who could fly by magical means were able to enter or leave the wealthiest district in all Samarach. Most nobles and aristocrats traveled via fly spell or rode pegasi. A series of underground passages were dug underneath the rich district, but its use was strictly forbidden and highly illegal, same as climbing the inner wall.

Geography

Samargol was a city located on the Wild Coast of the Chultan Peninsula,  to the immediate east from the Snout of Omgar at its base, and on the mouth of River Samar. Samargol's major neighboring cities were Taruin, 10 miles to the north along the River Samar and Rassatan further east along the Wild Coast.

Flora & Fauna
The city had a population of tucans and other colorful tropical birds.

Government
The seat of power in Samargol and the entirety of Samarach belonged to the High Phantasmage and the Council. The title of the High Phantasmage was rotational between the archmages of Nimbran. Each individual High Phantasmage reigned from just a few years to several decades. At the end of the High Phantasmage's rule, the tone along with the title were passed along to the next archmage. The ruler in the 1373 DR was Archmage Meleghost Zoaster who's reigned for many years and many rumors circulated in Samargol that he was not planning to return to Nimbran.

The Mercantile Affairs Committee controlled all trade activities in Samargol. Leaders of local trade companies, big and small, had to file paperwork with the Committee to legalize such events as an employee's dismissal.

Trade
The trade in Samargol was done via sea, river, and land. Each entering and leaving wagon or ship was searched physically by the guards and with the help of magics. Imported goods from other regions of Faerûn were priced at triple the average cost in Samargol due to trade restrictions and scarcity.

Culture

Like the entirety of Samarach, the city of Samargol was ruled by fear and mistrust of the dreaded yuan-ti. Society was consumed by the paranoia of the snake folk taint, corruption, and infiltration. Because of that mistrust was a cultural norm and was present even between friends or family members. The yuan-ti spies and even those who simply were accused of association with the serpents were punished swiftly and with extreme prejudice. The accused were taken to Samargol to stay trial, and the defendants rarely were acquitted.

The majority of Samargol's nobility were Nimbrese. They stood above Chultans and Tashlutans on the social ladder. This cultural divine sparked no unrest nor rebellion.

Outsiders were unwelcome in Samargol. The entirety of the city was closed off for anyone but the locals, while only one district – the Openpalm Bazaar, was open to visitors, merchants, and select individuals approved by the High Phantasmage.

Religion
Following the Time of Troubles, the Church of Leira established a hold in Samarach and its capital (in reality, Cyric was worshiped under Leira's guise). Big numbers of Leiran clerics settled in Samargol by the late 14 century DR, after Nimbral banned the concept of the state religion in the Seahaven. After Waukeen's return to the planes in 1370 DR, her church experienced renaissance. The clergy established solid hold in the cities of
Samarach and the capital of Samargol – a city of prevalent mercantile activity.

Defenses
Adult men and women were expected to take arms in defense of Samargol if the need arose. Spellcasters were well-regarded. Among them, those capable of weaving illusion were the most respected.

The city of Samargol and the country's trade routes were protected and patrolled by Samargol's Elite Guards. These men and women were highly trained warriors, dressed in ornate brass and leather chainmails and covered their faces with chainmail veils. The patrols often included silk-robed arcane spellcasters.

History
Sometime before the 14 century DR, a band of dreaded yuan-ti was unmasked among the citizens of Samarach. The revelation led to a widespread panic and paranoia. Despite the fact that most of the snakefolk in Samargol were believed to have been exterminated, the fear and mistrust remained, becoming part of the nation's everyday culture.

Following the magical cataclysm of the Spellplague, Samargol was presumed destroyed and most of Samarach, Thindol, Tashalar, and Lapaliiya were claimed by the sea. By the late-15 century DR, the devastation of the Spellpague was fixed by the return of Mystra, the Goddess of Magic. The drowned lands were returned to the surface. However, the fate of Samargol was shrouded in mystery as illusions covered the land of Samarach making the country truly "The Hidden Kingdom".

Notable Locations

Leira's Trick, the only inn and tavern in the city that allowed outsiders in as of the late 14 century DR.
Openpalm Bazaar, the only district open to visiting traders and merchants. It was the center of commerce, and an open-air market, surrounded by shops, temples, and other hives of mercantile activity.
A shrine of Waukeen could be found in the Openpalm Bazaar, it offered healing services to the visitors of the City of Veils.

Notable Inhabitants
Individuals
Dajos, a Captain of Samargol's Elite Guard circa 1373 DR.
Irim Mendar, a charismatic dealer of enchanted arms and armor in the late-14 cemetery DR.
Kureen, a Lady of Samargol who patronized a handful of merchants across the settlements of Samarach in the late-14 cemetery DR.
Sa'Sani, a prosperous merchant and secretly a yuan-ti pureblood whose merchant company operated out of Samargol and had trade affiliations with the Sword Coast North circa 1373 DR.

Organizations
Church of Waukeen maintained a shrine in the city's Openpalm Bazaar, staffed with several members of the Merchant's Friend clergy.
Crafter's Guild, the local gold that offered its halls and supplies for trade to visitors.

## Samarlogh

The Samarlogh was a dense jungle that covered the northern rim of Samarach on the Chultan Peninsula of southwest Faerûn.

Geography
Enclosed by the Sanrach Mountains in the north and west, and the River Samar to the east, the Samarlogh covered much of northeastern Samarach. The jungle reached all the way to the coast of the Great Sea in the west below the mountains. The Samarlogh was actually part of the larger jungle that covered much of Chult that was separated by the mountain range. A massive stone boulder could be found in the jungles of Samarlogh. It was known as the Heart of the Samarlogh by the locals. The Stone possessed magics tied to the Positive Energy plane. A chip off the Heart was turned into the enchanted weapon called Shard of the Samarlogh – the namesake of the Batiri Stone Knives tribe.

Inhabitants
Flocks of corollaxes and large carnivorous sloths, as well as many more dangerous creatures, could be found within the jungle. Ruins from House Sauringar strongholds littered the Samarlogh.

Rumors & Legends
Native Samarachans told of the legend of Squamata the Black, an embodiment of the encroaching jungle that swallowed everything in its path.

## Sadrach

Sadrach was a wizard who lived in Faerûn during the 14 and 15 centuries DR. He was a famous scholar of elemental magic, who dwelled within his own personal castle in south Faerûn.

Description
He venerated the four elemental lords; Akadi, Grumbar, Istishia, and Kossuth. He maintained four separate temples dedicated to each of them within his castle.

Personality
He was a confidant and learned man in life, who became filled with unrelenting rage while living in a cursed state of existence.

Relationships
His only known relative was his daughter Gingrid, a young necromancer who was granted unnaturally long longevity and youth.

History
When the Spellplague struck Toril, in the, Sadrach believed his magic was strong enough to protect the people within his castle. Unfortunately he was wrong. Those who were not killed outright became inflicted with spellscars, or worse yet, were transformed into plaguechanged.

Like many of his people, Sadrach was transformed into one of the "wallbound", unfortunate souls who became trapped within the structure of Sadrach Castle. He languished in this cursed state, as four stone faces and pairs of arms attached to the keep, filled with an uncontrollable fury for over a century.

During the events of the Second Sundering, in the final days of the, three of the gods' chosen and shades from Netheril came into conflict at Sadrach's Spire, causing the castle to crash into ruin. Sadrach and the unfortunate wallbound were freed from their stony prison, left to return to their loved ones. He aged wizard was last heard crying out for joy, thanking the divine beings of the Realms for his freedom.

## Salted fish

Salted fish was a common and affordable food item found throughout the Realms, usually produced in costal regions, salted fish was sometimes eaten raw, usually being soaked and cooked, or used as an ingredient in various dishes.

Description
Various species of fish were cleaned, gutted, and butchered and packed in salt. Smaller species were salted whole. With time, salt extracted moisture out of the fish and preserved the meat giving it longevity and ease of transportation as far as the sweltering lands of Zakhara. Before cooking, the salted fish was usually soaked in water overnight to wash off excessive salt.

Availability

Port Kir in Tethyr was a major supplier of fresh and salted fish, a lion share of the town's economy, being sold and traded in local markets and to caravans of traveling merchants. The entirety of Tethyr's costal area produced fish, it was consumed fresh or salted then traded inland.

Shadowdale, the Dalelands location of the Twisted Tower of Ashaba had salt fish commonly available and used in the kitchens of the tower.

Svardborg in Thrym was inhabited by the frost giant who fished and produced salt fish.

Ten Towns of Icewind Dale produced, sold, and exported salted fish, notably, Dougan's Hole was a major producer among the Ten and their fish was sold in Bryn Shander's markets.

Tharkar, in the Utter East was an unruly costal pirate city with salted and dried fish being a very common food items there. Donder's Dancing Masques tavern served it plane as a cheap bar snack.

Zakharan markets had salted fish available in abundance, often packed in a set of hundred fish and sold for prices from 2 gp to 7 sp, depending on haggling skills of the buyer.

Waterdeep's dock warehouses stored local salt fish. One such warehouse was turned into a shrine to Valkur, Sailor's Last Request.

In the Blade Kingdoms of the Vilhon Reach, salt fish was made out of carp, fished from Akanamere. The fish were salted, and dried in the sun, stretched on long strings.

Uses
Archenbridge tavern Bridge and Bow served a platter of salted fish, sausages, topped with melted cheese prepared with herbs, and brought to the table with fresh bread and parsley.

Juniril's tavern, the High Helm served a cold platter of salt fish, aggressively flavored cheese, and radishes. The joy of consuming that dish usually costed 3 cp.

Marsember's Platter of Plenty served Blentra's oysters and wild rice in mushroom soup, a delicious recipe, made popular by Volothamp Geddarm, used a cup of crushed salted fish as one of its many ingredients.

Ravens Bluff was the home of Skully's Bar and Bait, a seedy tavern that served fishbroth, a soup "dish" cooked with salted fish, old fishing bait that was too rotten to use, and various cheap and spoiled sauces and perfumes purchased for next to nothing.

Sabrae Tellendon was a drow High Priestess of Eilistraee who oversaw the descent to Undermountain in the mid–14 century DR.

Relationships
Kiyanda was Sabrae Tellendon's acolyte and while the High Priestess monitored drow activities in the North, Kiyanda traveled into Undermountain and Underdark too gather intel on the followers of Lolth.

In 1370 DR, Sabrae Tellendon could be found in the Yawning Portal healing adventurers brave enough to delve into the dangerous mega dungeon of Undermountain. ## Samuel Turgat

Captain Samuel Turgat was the captain of the Flyingfish ship from Daggerford, active circa 1372 DR.

Description
Samuel Turgat was a somewhat lanky middle-aged human male with dark medium-length hair and a fine mustache that framed his thin mouth.

Personality
Captain Turgat was considered the nicest marine vessel captain in Daggerford. Samuel spoke with a thick and put-on pirate dialect, rolling his r's and abusing the words of the trade. However, that was nothing but a show he seldom dropped, thinking that this dialect was expected from a man of his profession. When faced with non-sailors, Turgat felt comfortable enough to speak in his regular eloquent and educated manner.

Unlike many sea-faring folks, Turgat preferred reading to blackout drinking and could often be found doing just that in his cabin.

Possessions
Trulit & Greegon's claret‎‎ was one of the goods that Turgat's vessel traded in.

History
Samuel Turgat was an alias of a man who used to work at the Sorceller's Encapsulate, a mage library and guild of Daggerford. There the young man read through nearly every book, eventually coming across the book named Turgat's Revene, a work of fiction. That adventure novel told the story of Captain Samuel Turgat and his sidekick Doctor Algernon. Enamored with dreams of adventures on the high sea, "Samuel" purchased a boat he called Flyingfish using his sizable inheritance and legally changed his name to that of the fictional pirate. He hired a crew of "scurvy scallywags" and set to build his new life.

In 1372 DR, Captain Turgat was forced to dock in Daggerford after his ship suffered some damage in a fierce gale. The captain let his crew get drunk on their shore leave as he supervised the repairs. When Samuel Turgat met the Hero of Daggerford, he welcomed the opportunity to speak without the pirate jargon that tired him out. He informed the adventurer about a suspicious frigate docked next to the Flyingfish, the Barracuda. The ship had exclusively been loaded up under cover of darkness from suspicious barges that traveled up the Delimbiyr River.
