## Saddle

A saddle was a seat attached to a creature, either for riding or carrying objects.

Description
Types

Saddles were mainly split into two groups: riding saddles and pack saddles. Riding saddles were used by those who rode creatures, whilst pack saddles were frames used to carry equipment. (Riding saddles were sometimes used along with panniers for carrying small amounts of gear in addition to the  rider on the saddle.) Military saddles were also available, and were used by commanders and generals in battle. Exotic saddles were used to mount specific types of creatures, such as aquatic and aerial mounts.

These seats were sold all over Faerûn, Maztica, Kara-Tur, and Zakhara.

Saddles specifically made for camels had different names in Zakhara and were known as kharj, rahla, and terke. The rahla was small, comfortable and lightweight, whilst the terke was cage-like and enabled the rider to control the camel much more easily.

As well as animals, mechanical machines such as the Leaping Lizard of Raven's Bluff, were seated with saddles. Citizens of the city could mount the ride for 2 cp each.

Saddle frames were often made of wood or sheepskin. Saddles were sold by Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue, and each of them were made from black Ordulan leather.

Saddles manufactured in Calimshan had especially tall cantles.

Mounts

Such seats came in a multitude of varieties, each different depending on the rider's needs or the type of mount used. Types of mounts that used saddles were horses, mules, wolves, griffons, hippogriffs, steeders, pegasi, dragons, giant striders, and camels. Even dolphins could have saddles fitted to them, as well as owlbears, axebeaks, hippocampi, gelatinous cubes, purple worms, giant fireflies, giant lizards, veserabs, pteranadons, giant ravens, wyverns, stone flyers, nightmares, and carrion crawlers.

Usage

Traveling on saddles (particularly poor ones) for long periods could cause bad sores.

Instead of making saddles from scratch, arcane methods could be used. The mount spell could summon creatures with saddles already attached to them, or even howdahs, should the caster be powerful enough. Even the phantom steed spell summoned a ghostly mount with a saddle and bit and bridle to boot.

History
Saddles first became available in Netheril in .

1345 DR was known as the Year of the Saddle. The High Herald of Yartar was known as the Gold Saddle. In addition, the settlement of Lheshayl had an inn known as the Saddle of the Plains, Hill's Edge featured an inn called the Stone Saddle, and a body of water in Longsaddle was known as the Saddle Pond.

Notable Saddles

There were many notable saddles of the Realms, including those that could fit exotic beasts of all shapes and sizes. Some notable saddles were:

 The flying saddle of Thamos, a magic saddle from Cormyr.

 A saddle of the cavalier, a wondrous item favored by riders.

 A military saddle from Shadowdale had a huge black opal embedded into it. It was worth 1,000 gp.

Notable Owners
 Lord Charles Frederik LaVerne Blacktree IV owned a saddle that was modified with several attachments. His saddle held not only his bow and silver arrows, but also javelins in special scabbards.
 Chuqali Shilai's headquarters, the nom-satakon erughe, housed a number of saddles.

Notable Producers of Saddles

 Calishite-crafted saddles were masterfully made. Their design appeared rugged but was highly functional and comfortable for both the horse and the rider.
 Keltar was well known for its production of saddles. Their saddles had such a good reputation that they were desired by every Calishite horseman. One of the great crafters of Keltar claimed he could custom-make saddles for dragons.
 Mosstone was a notable producer and exporter of saddles.

Trivia
In the Roll of Years, the year 1345 DR bore the name "Year of the Saddle".

## Sanctorium

The Sanctorium was the grand library of Myth Drannor, located in the city during its glory days before the 8 century DR.

Location
It was located in the district of Sheshyrinnam, on the corner of Moonopal Way and Elbansyr's Ride.

Structure
The above-ground structure of the Sanctorium was a white marble building surrounded by wide-set stone stairs. Its outer walls were decorated with elaborate frescoes and sculptures that depicted the daily life found in Myth Drannor. The façade was altered every year by means of magic to show new scenes that occurred the year prior.

Beneath the library hall were five subterranean levels that housed its grand collection of documents.

Interior
Within the marble structure of the Sanctorium was a vast hall that featured statues that depicted elves, humans, dwarves and the other races that took up residence within Myth Drannor.

Services
A single figure sat at a lone desk within the hall to greet visitors and retrieve books and documents from within the Sanctorium's vaults. The true breadth of information found within the vaults was unknown to all. While petitioners were seldom denied a specific tome, no one was fully aware of everything it had to offer.

The only other people allowed within the vaults were the city's high mages and those spellcasters who had graduated from Windsong Tower.

## Salmon

Salmon, also known as sorn, was a common species of fish found throughout Realms. Acting as a food source for many beasts, humanoids, and other creatures.

Description
The various species of salmon had different hues to their scales, with silver being more common in the Moonshae Isles.

Behavior
Salmon were known for returning to their spawning grounds in cold mountain rivers every spring to procreate and lay roe, giving birth to future generations of the fish.

Ecology
Salmon was a major food source in the spring when they traveled upstream to breed. During that time, many settlements brought in big hauls of fish, feeding the communities of the Ten Towns on Maer Dualdon, as well as wild beasts, such as bears.

Salmon of the Lake Sember seasonally migrated down the Semberflow and River Ashaba towards the Sea of Fallen Stars. In the region, salmon and freshwater trout were the main food source for the elves, the Alu'Tel'Quessir. From the Sea of Fallen Stars, salmon seasonally ran upstream towards the Dagger Falls.

In Deepingdale in the Dalelands, salmon could be found in abundance in the waters of Glaemril and Wineflow, feeding the citizens of Highmoon and wild beasts. The salmon was a precious resource in the region and was under strict protection. Any passing traveler was considered a bandit if they caught more than one fish per person.

In Amn, salmon used the Comassur Creek as their annual spawning ground. The area was famous for being a great fishing spot.

Methmere in Chessenta and Unther had a large population of salmon, trout, and bass that sustained the communities living on the lake's shores.

Salmon could be found in great numbers between the islands of Gwynneth and Alaron of he Moonshae Isles, providing the local fisherfolk with a decent living.

The isle of Gundarlun in Trackless Sea made good coin exporting smoked salmon.

Salmon was among the species of fish that dwelt within the waters of the Lake of Mists, feeding the villages on its shores.

The island of Wa in Kara-Tur had a rich food source in fish like tuna, salmon, mackerel, and squid.

Beyond the Prime Material plane, salmon could be found on the Elemental Plane of Water where they swarmed the Citadel of Ten Thousand Pearls during certain seasons.

Lampreys were small parasitic fish that attached themselves to bigger-sized fish, sucking the blood out of their hosts. Salmon was one of the fish affected by these parasites.

Species
Shaengarne salmon: This species of salmon traveled to the Shaengarne River every spring to their sprawling ground.

Uses

Clothing: Korobokuru dwarves in Kara-Tur often weaved cloth out of hemp nettle, bark, fur, feathers, and salmon skin.
Deviled spice-and-salmon duck eggs: A famous delicacy from the Cold Duck eatery in Beluir.
Marsemban tarts: Delicious pastries topped with parsley, potato, diced salmon, crab, and smothered in almond milk, wine, leek, and persimmons.
Silky salmon and prawn tarts were one of the specialties of the Oak-Father's Boon inn in Mosstone of Tethyr.
Salmon cream: A traditional accompaniment for brassla made out of dried and crushed salmon.
Salmon en Croûte: A salmon dish from the Sword Coast that consisted of a fresh fish, wrapped in pastry crust and drizzled with a hearty buttter sauce.
Salmon, steamed with shallot and wine cream: A local specialty in Fireshear, Frozenfar.
Salted fish: Salmon was among the fish that were caught in the Lake of Mists by the people of the Endless Wastes. Damara was a major exporter of both salted and smoked salmon, sold via Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue.
 Smoked salmon: A popular delicacy exported from the island of Gundarlun. It could often be found in Waterdeep's fancy establishments and served as an appetizer at various social events there, or eaten for a fine breakfast in Cormyr. Smoking salmon was also a common preservation technique among the residents of the Endless Wastes.

History
In the days of ancient Netheril, salmon was commonly found in the Allegro River, Annagoth Bay, and Fria River.

In 1358 DR, during the Time of Troubles, the river of the Winding Water became corrupted, oily, and poisonous following the death of Bhaal. The river had been a habitat of salmon and, following the disaster, the population of the fish was decimated.

In 1364 DR, delectable imported smoked salmon was served at an event hosted by Lucia Thione-Hhune, held in the ballroom of her Sea Ward villa in Waterdeep.

In 1372 DR, a serving of bad salmon was blamed for the death of a nobleman from Daggerford, Elveronne Bricket. In reality, salmon was innocent and his murderous poisoner sister Muriel Bricket was to blame.

Trivia
Baraundarella was a voluptuous dancer who was unkindly called Salmon-Ella, for her fishnets that reminded some of fish in nets..
The green elves used images of various animals as tattoos, representing individual's spirit animal. Salmon were one among their designs.
The Silver Salmon was a hospitable inn in Corwell on the Moonshae Isles, named after the common salmon found throughout the isles.

## Sanatorium

The Sanatorium was an underground institution in Helm's Hold that catered to the care for spellscarred individuals who suffered the more severe ailments brought on by their condition.

Location
It was located beneath the Cathedral of Helm.

Interior
The labyrinthine Sanatorium was composed of long, twisting corridors and underground vaults.

Atmosphere
The dark and cold feeling of the Sanatorium lent to an atmosphere that more resembled a dungeon or prison, rather than a place of care and convalescence.

Services
For many years, the Sanatorium offered healing and housing for those spellscarred who suffered from any form of insanity or physical ailments brought upon by the lingering effects of the Spellplague. It was staffed by Helmite priests that dedicated themselves to the care of these unfortunate souls.

When the Prophet Rohini emerged as a leader in Helm's Hold, she championed the cause of offering care for the city's spellscarred. She was aided in her efforts by a trusted goliath named Torlgar, who served as the Sanatorim's warden.

Some patients that were considered relatively healthy aided in the upkeep and maintenance of Helm's cathedral. Every patient was allowed to leave their underground domain once each day and walk the undergrounds under close scrutiny by the priests.

History
During the 1470s DR, the Prophet Rohini began experimenting on the very same people she swore to care for within the Sanatorium, even transforming some into foulspawn abominations. Her atrocities continued when she allowed the Ashmadai to sacrifice the patients in the rituals they used to conjure forth devils from the Nine Hells.

During the Siege of Helm's Hold, circa the, Rohini secluded herself within the Cathedral of Helm and surrounded her Ashmadai cultists and devilish allies. Aided by the creature known as Hexabaal the Seer, the Hero of Sleeping Dragon Bridge ventured into the Sanatorium by means of a secret entrance and slew the warden Torlgar, before breaking through into the cathedral itself confronting Rohini herself.

## Samaj

Samaj was an ormyrr merchant of the Sharpstick clan who laired in the city of Myth Drannor circa 1369 DR.

History
In 1369 DR, the ormyrr of the Sharpstick clan were divided by the dark mind-affecting magics of the Cult of the Dragon. The usurper chief Pujik allied himself with the cultists after being driven mad by Kya Mordrayn's pool of radiance, while Retha, the old leader, was trying to stop the evil ormyrr. Samaj remained loyal to the tribe's true leader. Subsequently, a group of heroes, the Veiled Ones, confronted Pujik and killed the slave of the Cult. The tribe was freed from the Cult's corruption. In reward, Samaj offered the heroes the loyalty of the tribe as well as access to many magical treasures the ormyrr gathered in the ruins of the ancient city.

## Sandal

Sandals were a common form of footwear that could be found throughout the Realms.

Description

This form of footwear typically consisted of a leather sole that was held in place by a series of loops around the wearer's ankle, toes, or the top of their foot. When a sandal lacked an ankle strap the loose sole tended to slap against their wearer's heel or toes when they walked, which made walking quietly difficult without practice.

History
In the mid to late-14 century DR, one could purchase sandals from many of the bazaars in Zakhara for around 7 to 4 copper pieces depending upon how good they were at bargaining.

Around that same time, Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue was the sole exporter of Calimshan's unique sandals. They were made of fine grain leather in shades of black, brown, or white and had full brass buckles.

Varieties of Sandal
 Geta: a type of Kara-Turan sandal that was elevated by wooden blocks.
 Pampootie: leather sandals that were waterproofed by means of waxy plant fibers.
 Zori: Kara-Turan sandals that were made of either woven straw or wooden blocks. Of the two materials, woven straw was considered the cheapest and easiest to craft.

Notable Sandals
 Sandals of speed: a variety of enchanted sandals unique to Maztica that made the wearer faster. These were typically made and used by hishnashapers.
 Sandals of striding and leaping
 Winged sandals: a pair of enchanted sandals owned by the Olympian Hermes that enabled him to fly.
 Zin sandals: sandals made from the hide of a zin. These were said to enhance a wearer's ability to climb and move quietly.

Notable Users of Sandals

General
 Toe-covered sandals were a common form of footwear among both genders throughout the Realms.
Classes
 Sandals were a common article of clothing among monks.
 Wizards in the land of Amn were typically known to wear wooden sandals.
Groups
 In the land of Anauroch, the D'tarig often wore special widened sandals that were designed to make walking atop sand easier.
Individuals
 Akana Kiku
 Askoro Imo
 Cephas
 Deng Tse Chin
 Feena Archwood
 Hoshin
 Kane
 Kotaro Taro
 Moral
 Motoharu Kaijitsu
 Pikel Bouldershoulder
 Stoki
 Uldiznae Rockfall
 Zezen Washio
Nations & Settlements
 Due to the land's heat, sandals were the preferred footwear in the nation of Calimshan. Those made with supple leather were reserved for the middle and upper classes.
 In the land of Kara-Tur, sandals were the typical footwear for those who lived in Kozakura, the Malatran Plateau, the Plain of Horses, Shou Lung, T'u Lung, and Wa. The most common form of sandal in Kozakura and Wa was the gati. In Shou Lung and T'u Lung, sandals were only ever worn by commoners who couldn't afford to ruin their good footwear in the muddy fields.
 In the land of Maztica, sandals were one of the typical forms of footwear.
 In the land of Mulhorand, sandals were the typical form of footwear.
 In the land of Zakhara, all but the poorest of men could be found wearing sandals. The exception to this was the city of Huzuz, where sandals were only rarely seen worn.

Races & Intelligent Creatures
 Leather sandals were one of the preferred footwear among avariels.
 Bacchae commonly dressed in sandals.
 Cyclopskin commonly dressed in sandals.
 Halflings often wore sandals when weather was fair.
 When it came to humans, the ethnic groups that most often favored sandals included the Mulan.
 As part of their effort to imitate the clothing of local human populations, oni were sometimes known to wear sandals.
 Storm giants often wore sandals.

Religions

 Cultivators, the specialty priests of Chauntea, typically wore sandals as part of their priestly vestments.
 The clergy of Callarduran Smoothhands typically wore sandals made of steel as part of their priestly vestments.
 The interloper deity Hermes was typically portrayed as wearing a pair of winged sandals.
 Moralists typically wore sandals as part of their priestly vestments."
 The clergy of many deities in the Mulhorandi pantheon were known to feature sandals as part of their priestly vestments. These included Anhur, Horus-Re, Isis, Nephthys, Osiris, and Thoth. And the goddess Isis was often depicted as wearing sandals.
 Nung Chiang, one of the Nine Immortals, was said to always wear a pair of worn sandals.
 The clergy of Sehanine Moonbow typically wore simple sandals as part of their priestly vestments.
 The Maztican goddess Watil was typically portrayed as wearing sandals made of flowers.

## Sashenstar

The Sashenstars were a noble family of Baldur's Gate that had a long association with the city's Merchant's League.

Activities
They owed their considerable wealth to their interests in commercial shipping, mining operations, and the textiles industry.

History

Staring around the, and continuing on to the Iron crisis that afflicted the Sword Coast in the, the Merchant's League was to compete with the rising Iron Throne mercantile group. The noble family even allowed their Baldurian estate, overseen by Aldeth Sashenstar, to be infiltrated by a group of Throne-controlled doppelgangers. As a result, the city's Council of Four banned the Merchant's League from operating in Baldur's Gate entirely. While the Sashenstar family renounced the organization in public, they maintained a working relationship with the league in secret.

House Sashenstar experienced years of decline until the, when the family's prodigal son Dabron Sashenstar returned from an expedition to Sossal, took over leadership of the family's businesses, and brought down the Iron Throne with the blessing from the Baldurian Council of Four.

Notable Members
 Aldeth Sashenstar, family patriarch in the years leading up to 1372 DR.
 Bogo Sashenstar, a historic individual whose Bone Flotilla that plundered Aster Cove was not mentioned in school books in Baldur's Gate.
 Dabron Sashenstar, Aldeth's heir who was most famous for being the explorer that "discovered" the kingdom of Sossal.
 Silifrey Sashenstar, a member of the Parliament of Peers in the late 15 century DR.
 Yarrick Sashenstar, a ser patron of the arts and the father of triplets in the late 15 century DR.

## Saiyaddar

The Saiyaddar was the region of Anauroch considered to be the "hunting grounds" of the Bedine people.

Description
The lush grasslands were said to be about  across east to west, and about  north to south.

Geography
The Saiyaddar was centrally located in the region of Anauroch known as the Sword, south of the Wall of Fallen Djinn mountains and west of the ruins of Orofin.

Geographical Features
Alagh's Pass allowed for passageway between the Saiyaddar through the mountains to the Hills of Scent to the north.

Flora & Fauna
In contrast to much of the rest of the desert, the Saiyaddar was lined with natural springs that gave life to an abundance of plant life including tall grasses and many trees.

It was the one location in all of Anauroch that was home to the greatest number of wild gazelles. Other animals found within included hares, onagers, snakes, and a variety of birds.

Rumors & Legends
Knowledge of the Saiyaddar was kept secret by the people of the Bedine, and never claimed by any single tribe.

Inhabitants
Surviving tribes of armands roamed the Saiyadder after being chased out of Azirrhat by the Shadovar.

## Sapphire Spark

The Sapphire Spark was an enchanted necklace that was used to empower its wearer's magic missiles.

Description
The Sapphire Spark was a delicate and elaborate necklace with deep blue sapphire gems dangling off its chain. Each of the gemstone flickered with magic as if ready to burst into flames and warm to the touch.

Powers
This piece of magical jewelry allowed its wearer to cast the slightly more damaging version of the magic missile spell and drastically improved the spell's damaging potential if worn by a creature who had the spell in their spellbook. The spell's additional damage came in the form of a psychic energy.

History
In 1492 DR, the Sapphire Spark was in possession of a bugbear member of the Society of Brilliance, Blurg, who was operating out of a peaceful myconid colony in the Underdark, working to improve the living conditions of the subterranean realm.

## Sarkrith

Sarkriths were a species of reptilian humanoids that abhorred magic and waged a quiet war against all tainted by it. They were divided into two physically similar but quite different castes: spelleaters and thanes. They were counted as scalykind by other Scaled Ones.

Description
All sarkriths were at least  and weighed about . Spelleaters had light blue scales that dulled to grey as they aged. Both castes were muscular and generally lizard-like humanoids in appearance.

Personality
Sarkriths were cold, calculating, and entirely logical in their thinking; they were both patient and deliberate in their quiet war against magic. To the sarkrith, peace meant having control, and magic was far too unpredictable for them to accept, and magic in the hands of anyone meant that the sarkrith lacked control.

Spelleaters were obsessed with individual might; people were either stronger or weaker than one another, which was how the chain of command was established. They had a difficult time even grasping the concept of an exchange of equals and expected obedience from all other creatures. They saw all other peoples as being tainted by magic. Even in battle, they never seemed rushed or hurried.

Thanes were fierce, loyal, cruel, and hated magic just as much as the spelleaters. When dealing with less powerful creatures, they only spoke in the imperative; when dealing with spelleaters, they always responded with instant obedience.

Abilities
Sarkrith spelleaters and thanes were most divergent in their abilities and resulting tactics; the only abilities they had in common were their darkvision, keen sense of smell, and the ability to mask their own scent.

Spelleaters were capable of generating a ray that canceled any magical effects on whatever it touched. Additionally, any time a sarkrith spelleater successfully resisted a spell, they healed in proportion to the tier of the spell.

Thanes only took subdual damage from bludgeoning attacks, and any time they were hit with a spell that caused energy damage, they gained a measure of resistance to that energy for a day afterwards. At times, adrenaline releases in their body could boost them for a short time in combat. Thanes could also created an antimagic field with a  radius around themselves once each day.

Combat
When sarkriths made forays into the wider world, they fought in coordination with each other. Spelleaters commanded the thanes, who obeyed without question and always protected the spelleaters. The spelleaters would always direct their own attacks and those of the thanes against any foe obviously using magic. If there were mutliple spelleaters in a group, then half used their dispelling rays and they other half used the composite longbows they typically carried. Thanes always preferred to use their size and numbers to crush foes in melee combat; they were dangerous, but predictable, focusing on one foe (typically the closest unless directed otherwise) until the enemy went down, then going to the next. Thanes wore plate armor and wielded greatswords and mighty composite longbows; thanes typically wore chainmail shirts and carried shields, and were known to carry either composite longbows or longswords.

Society
Sarkriths had an orderly, regimented society that had room only for those other peoples that were tractable enough to be citizen-slaves; anyone else, especially anyone who used magic, the sarkrith aimed to kill. They typically lived in underground city-states. Sarkriths on most worlds lacked the numbers to have any real power.

Spelleaters were the heart of the sarkrith species and almost always traveled with a squad of thanes for protection. They also drove the sarkrith's hatred of magic. Thanes were bred to obey spelleaters and their superiors without question.

Since spelleaters were so obsessed with individual power and chain of command, very powerful creature such as dragons sometimes established dominion over groups of sarkriths. This was a risky undertaking, however, as sarkriths were never loyal to other species.

Sarkriths spoke the Common and Infernal languages; sarkrith spelleaters also spoke the Draconic language.

## Sabkhat

Sheikh Sabkhat was the head of the Mtair Dhafir tribe of Bedine circa the mid–14 century DR.

Description
The older sheikh was bone thin with eyes that were clouded over and only a wisp of a beard remaining on his chin.

Relationships
Sabkhat had a strained relationship with his daughter Ruha, who proved to have premonitions of the future at a young age. Because the Bedine people completely eschewed the use of magic, the sheikh sent Ruha to live with an exiled sorceress called Qoha'dar at the Sister of Rains oasis. After Qoha'dar died, Ruha spent a year searching for the Mtairi camp and eventually returned to her tribe. Families threatened to leave the Mtairi due to her return, and Sheikh Sabkhat was made the decision to marry her away to the son of a fellow tribe leader.

He had a formidable ally in Al'Aif, the most fearsome warrior of the entire tribe.

History
Ruha returned to the Mtair Dhafir and was sent away again by Sheikh Sabkhat in the . That same year Sheikh Sabkhat was approached by the charismatic warrior Zarud, who served as the spokesperson for the Zhentarim to the Bedine people. He was put under magical influence by the mage Yhekal and compelled to form an alliance with the Zhent outlanders. While Ruha and Al'Aif were able to talk some sense into Sheikh Sabkhat, he ultimately agreed to Zarud's conditions without entering into a formal alliance.

The death of Zarud the following night prevented the agreement between the Mtairi and Zhentarim from moving forward. According to Sheikh Sabkhat the tribe's only option was to "pay the blood price" and offer Zarud's killer over to the Zhentarim and hope they accept. Unfortunately for the sheikh, all the signs pointed to his daughter Ruha as being the murderer. While Al'Aif was able to let Ruha and her allies free, the rest of the Mtair Dhafir were not so fortunate. The tribespeople were slain by the Zhentilar army and their bodies fed to the Zhent's asabi mercenaries.

## Sa'ar

Sheikh Sa'ar was the leader of the Bait Mahwa tribe of the Bedine people for a time during the mid–14 century DR.

Description
He was a burly man with a hard face and eyes that hinted at his cunning and experience.

Personality
Among the members of numerous Bedine tribes, Sa'ar was known as a particularly intelligent leader. He was a boisterous and passionate man who quite proud of his people, with a dry sense of humor, but also someone that was dismissive of those he viewed as outsiders. At times he let tis pride get in the way of accepting outside aid.

Relationships
Sa'ar had a strong ally in Sheikh Utaiba, leader of the Raz'hadi tribe. During the times Sa'ar grew contentious with others, it was Utaiba who could calm his friend and help him listen to reason.

History
Leader of the Bedine
In the, Sheikh Sa'ar was approached by Yhekal, an agent of the Zhentarim that hoped to form an "alliance" between his tribe and the outlander merchant's group. The meeting was interrupted by the Qahtani sorceress Ruha and the Harpers agent Lander, who warned that the Zhentilar army slaughtered the Mtair Dhafir and Qahtan tribes. Sa'ar sided with Ruha and Lander, and had his people abandon their camps and ride off, barely avoiding a raid by the Zhents' asabi mercenaries. They traveled to the Well of the Chasm to regroup with tan allied tribe, routing the Zhent camp outside the oasis, and joining the Raz'hadi in another victory at the Battle of the Chasm.

Following the battles, Sa'ar and Utaiba brought together fifteen other sheikhs of the Bedine tribes to the sacred Elah'zad oasis to confer on what was to be done with the Zhentarim threat. They ultimately decided to fight as one united people, gathering all the Bedine within over a tenday's ride away from the oasis. Under the united leadership, the Bedine went on to claim victory against the asabis at the Battle of the Fissures, and finally turn away the Zhentarim from the Sword during the siege of Orofin.

Change of Life for the Mahwa

Over a decade later, in the, the Shadow Sea formed over the Shoal of Thirst, a result of the terraforming and weather manipulation caused by the Shadovar residents of the returned Netherese city of Thultanthar. Sa'ar and the Mahwa saw this new source of water as a blessing and they regularly camped upon its shore. In short time they forewent their nomadic ways and accepted the new leisurely lifestyle presented to them.

That same year Sa'ar reunited with Ruha for the first time since their shared victory over the Zhentarim. Ruha had been discreetly traveling across Anauroch with her new allies Caladnei and Hhormun of Cormyr, in search of Thultanthar's resting place. The two parties each used the others' strength to their advantage: supported by the Cormyrean mages, Sa'ar stole a herd of the Shadovar's flying veserab mounts, and the Cormyreans used the Bedine's raid to draw out the location of the Shadovar's forces, leading back to their city.

This type of fabric was thick and woven from high-quality threads of silk and golden thread.

## Sangin

Sangin was a human warlock who lived in Barovia during the 15 century of the Faerûnian calendar.

Description
The warlock wore simple robes of earthen hues but adorned himself with numerous rings. He wore particularly tiny glasses ad the end of his rather large nose.

Personality
He was a conniving man that cared primarily for his own wellbeing. He desperately sought a way to escape Barovia, seeing others as merely a means to that end.

Abilities
Sangin was at least somewhat knowledgeable about the workings of the arcane.

Relationships
Like all inhabitants of Barovia were in some way, Sangin was a servant of Count Strahd von Zarovich.

Sangin briefly worked with a pair of werewolves Dron and Utna, who laired with their pack in a cave network near Lake Baratok.

History
Sometime circa the 1480s DR, Sangin was dispatched along with Dron and Utna by Count Strahd to Toril on the Prime Material plane to search for a specific magical item that could allow travel away from Barovia. While away from the Domains of Dread the Sangin and the lycanthropes worked together to claim the item for themselves, as a means of escape from Strahd's ever-present and far-reaching control.

They tracked the item to the temple of Kelemvor in Baldur's Gate, and abducted the cleric Nerys Kathon to identify it from a group of other relics. After successfully identifying an enchanted locket that once belonged to a woman named Tatyana, Nerys withheld the information from her captors until she was rescued by the Heroes of Baldur's Gate. Unfortunately for everyone involved, Strahd tracked them down and used the mists of the Shadowfell to bring them all to his realm.
Once the group was returned to Strahd's domain, Sangin was promptly carried away by a cloud of bats to Castle Ravenloft. Marked a traitor by the count, Sangin was feasted upon by three of Strahd's vampires and turned into an undead monster himself.

After his transformation, the vampiric Sangin was dispatched alongside his his sires to exert Strahd's control over the werewolf den, forcing the pack's leader Kiril to order the death of Dron. Bringing the werewolves along as brutish enforcers, Sangin and the vampires assailed Baron Vargas for harboring the Heroes of Baldur's Gate, enemies of Barovia. In the ensuing battle, the undead Sangin was beheaded by Utna as revenge for Dron's death.

## Salve of senile serenity

Salve of senile serenity, also known as Teodgren's salve of senile serenity, was a magic potion that could temporarily elevate the mind-affecting symptoms of dementia in elderly sentient creatures.

Powers
The salve was pleasant-tasting and it immediately cleared the fog from the mind of a creature affected by dementia.

History
In 1369 DR, Teodgren's salve of senile serenity were mixed and sold by Bellowgulp Bluefingers and the Concocter during their time in Dennaton's gladiatorial pits of Thay. The salve, which apparently was also effective when ingested, was used by a drow wizard Gezzthemin on the rarely lucid elderly wizard Dulf Ebonbeard. ## Samblar

Samblar was the wizard forge master proprietor of Samblar's Swords in Myth Drannor, the smithy that dealt in enchanted arms and was in operation for hundreds of years prior to the Weeping War.

Abilities
Samblar was a master in both enchanting and metalwork, evident by the thick steel cables that anchored his workshop and store, Samblar's Swords, in place. The wizard personally ensorcelled the cables to remain phenomenally strong, even centuries past the fall of Myth Drannor.

History
Samblar opened his store sometime before the Opening of Myth Drannor, in the .

Before the fall of Myth Drannor, the wizard worked out of a small forge on the first floor of his tower located on the Dragoneyes Street, to the north from Castle Cormanthor. He created masterwork weapons and enchanted the ones he was especially proud of. His favorite enchantments were keen edge and magics that increased speed, durability, and accuracy. Samblar was a respected weaponsmith among the inhabitants of Myth Drannor, known for his fairness and impeccable skill.

Samblar died in the Weeping War of 714 DR, defending his home from the horde of demons and orcs of the Army of Darkness.

Rumors & Legends
Some of the adventurers who visited the ruins of the city after the War swore that Samblar's spirit returned to his anvil during the full moon and the clanking of his tools could be heard echoing through the night. Those who were brave and lucky enough to interact with the wizard's ghost claimed that he respected strength and bravery and could reward them with one of his special arms still hidden in the ruins of his tower or inside his magically sealed vault.

Circa 1372 DR, Sarraver had recently found his wayward daughter in Waterdeep. Coril was unwilling to leave the city to avoid his attentions, and so had to stay and reject his agents' growing numbers of attempts to take her back to be married off.

## Sarutobi's Stone Breakers

Sarutobi's Stone Breakers were unique enchanted bracers created in the distant land of Shou Lung.

Powers
Created to be worn only by law-abiding human monks, these gauntlets increased the wearer's strength and dealt greater damage when the monk used their fists in combat, especially when landing serious blows.

History
Sarutobi's Stone Breakers were commissioned by a wealthy and well-meaning merchant of Shou Lung for a legendarily skilled monk named Sarutobi for his upcoming battle with a fearsome stone giant. However, the legend stated that the martial artist declined the gift, declaring "I will crush this giant's skull with my bare hands, and I need no magic to help me do it!" thinking his talent was enough. Sarutobi was right. He won the duel and the Stone Breakers were locked away unused in the merchant's vault.

Later, the martial artist went missing after entering a gate that took him to planes unknown. Following Sarutobi's disappearance, the merchant gifted the Stone Breakers to the monk's son, who devoted the rest of his life to an unsuccessful search for his father.

Years later, by 1281 DR, Sarutobi's Stone Breakers had found their way to freezing Icewind Dale and into the hands of a halfling cleric of Waukeen, Quinn Silverfinger, of the Golden Lodge in Lonelywood. He offered these unique bracers for sale to adventurers in that year.

In the, Sarutobi's Stone Breakers ended up in the hands of the shady drow merchant Nym. They were available for purchase from his camp outside Bryn Shander's wall.

## Sarazh

Sarazh was the unofficial mayor of the town of Signpost in the noonday wilderness of Krigala on the plane of the Beastlands. She was a cleric of Deneir and a factor of the Sign of One faction.

Description
Sarazh was a tiefling woman with pale, wrinkled skin. She had blue eyes and wispy gray hair, which she wore in a messy topknot. Her dreamy expression and tiny build made her appear more delicate than she actually was.

Personality
Sarazh was very observant and had an incredible memory for details, which served her well in her role in her faction. She could recall pages that she had read, maps that she had studied, faces that she had met in childhood—all with astonishing accuracy. She was exceptionally intelligent and was able to piece together and correlate facts to solve mysteries.

Abilities
As a worshiper of the Faerûnian god of written knowledge, she had access to divine spells classified into the astral, charm, divination, and guardian spheres. She was especially skilled in divinatory magics such as detect evil, detect magic, and tongues, among others.

Possessions
Sarazh typically carried a quarterstaff with her.

Activities
Sarazh ran the Dreamhearth, a manor and estate for the Sign of One located in the frontier settlement of Signpost, but she effectively also served as that town's marshal and mayor. She assisted Factol Darius in searching the multiverse for clues toward their faction's purpose.

History
As a so-called "planar", Sarazh was born and raised in the Outer Planes.

## Sajah al-Munsif

Caliph Sajah al-Munsif, also called the Most Revered Mother or the Quiet Caliph, was the ruler of Hudid in the Pantheist League of interior Zakhara in 1367 DR. She was the only female ruler of a Pantheist city for a generation.

Personality
Sajah rarely spoke or acted directly, preferring to let her actions speak for her and leading by example. When she did speak she enjoyed using parables that were often confusing at first but ultimately related important truths leading to Pantheist enlightenment.

Activities
Sajah's resolve prevented the spread of Balanite heresy along the coast. She often donned her chador and walked freely among the people of Hudid, noting their needs so she could better lead them.

History
Sajah served the Pantheist League as a representative to the Grand Caliph in Huzuz. Impressed with both her wit and wisdom, the Grand Caliph appointed her caliph of Hudid in 1357 DR. The League Conclave approved of the Grand Caliph's decision.

Relationships
Sajah had twin daughters, Inara and Jinara, during her tenure in Huzuz.

His fondness for the horse was such that Vala Thorsdotter was familiar with Sable despite the horse having lived in the time of her great-grandfather, Bodvar. Melegaunt was so fond of the horse, in fact, that when Vala and her warriors arrived in Evereska to fulfill her clan's pact with him in the, he was saddened to see the horse was not with them, and had to be reminded that she had died more than 80 years prior. ## Sanctify crypt

Sanctify crypt was a powerful divine abjuration spell used only by specialty priestesses devoted to Nephthys called Goodwives.

Effects
Used by Goodwives to seal a burial chamber for all time, sanctify crypt lasted until the crypt was despoiled by looters who took any valuable items found within or destroyed the mummified remains of the deceased. The looters would incur the terrible major curse upon entering the crypt. Furthermore, a member of each looters family would experience the same curse for each generation until the crypt was restored to its previous state.

The curse could be lifted with the remove major curse spell, which only affected the direct looter, or the looter's descendants could perform a special quest for the Church of Nephthys to clear their family's name and remove the curse forever.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as a material component gold dust to cast. The gold dust needed to be valued at at least 1 gp per square foot of crypt floor to be sanctified. The gold dust was sprinkled on the floor during the casting.

References

## Sanctify spirit host

Sanctify spirit host was a powerful divine necromancy spell used only by specialty priests devoted to Osiris called Divine Arbitrators.

Effects
This spell was used during Mulhorandi burial rites to ensure the ba, or life force, had safe haven and ka, or spirit twin, traveled safely to the afterlife. The mummified body sanctified by this spell would rise by the power of Osiris if their crypt was ever disturbed, attacking anyone found within. The mummy was different from others of its kind in that it could only be turned by Divine Arbitrators, did not carry the typical mummy rot curse, was invulnerable to fire, and was only susceptible to holy water blessed by priests of Set. The mummy would return to its rest when the looters were killed or driven from its crypt.

Components
This spell required only verbal and somatic components to cast.

## Sarcophagus of death

Sarcophagus of death was a divine evocation spell used only by specialty priests devoted to Set called Nighthunters.

Effects
This spell created a cube of force in the shape of a coffin around a target chosen by the Nighthunter. The coffin was airtight and impervious to magic save for a dispel magic spell. The only way to escape the coffin from the inside was via a teleportation spell. Simply being inside the coffin caused physical harm as well as drained the target's overall constitution. Targets trapped within the sarcophagus of death stood a likely chance of death by asphyxiation.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as material components the priest's holy symbol and fragment of a sarcophagus consecrated by a priest of Osiris or Nephthys to cast.

## Sacred link

Sacred link was a powerful divine alteration and evocation spell used by priests devoted to Tempus, enabling them to create a magical bond between two objects.

Effects
Once the sacred link was established, what happened to one object would happen to the other regardless of the distance between them. This link would last until either one of the items was destroyed or the magic was successfully dispelled.

The items in question were held in the caster's hands when the spell was cast. The items needed to be made of the same material and be roughly the same size. The items could not be living creatures. This spell was most effective when cast upon identical, or nearly identical, items.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as material components the caster's holy symbol and strand of hair, fine wire or spider web to cast.

## Sandvoyagers Track

The Sandvoyagers Track, also known as the Sandvoyagers Trail, was a trail of footprints that cut across the Raurin. The Sandvoyagers Guild both created those tracks in their passage and used them to orient themselves.
Structure
Its sharp turns were announced by guideposts from which hung silver bangles stamped with the symbol of the Sandvoyagers Guild.
Path
The trail began on the south at Northknife Pass amongst the Dustwall Mountains; it continued northwest across the desert towards the Gates of Sule, then went north towards a guidepost pointing the way to Terbakar and Pazar before joining with the bed of the dry River Athis for some time, crossing through both the Oasis of Akhir and the Oasis of the White Palm. The track briefly split from the river past this point; it rejoined the dry riverbed closer to Medinat Muskawoon.

## Sash of spells

Sash of Spells was a powerful spell from Halruaa, knowledge of which was popularized in broader Faerûn by Volothamp Geddarm in his book, Volo's Guide to All Things Magical.

Effects
Upon casting the spell, the caster was surrounded by a diagonal band of energy for eight minutes. The band was capable of capturing up to eight spells: those spells were absorbed into the sash. If two identical spells were cast consecutively, the sash added a third identical spell, thus allowing the capacity of the sash to be extended to twelve spells.

This energy was not limited to the caster's spells, or even to spells that they would be able to cast. Divine spells were absorbable as normal by the magic. Once the time limit was up, the band's ends joined together, and all absorbed spells became ready to be unleashed freely through an act of will. This act of will had to be one of free will: the sash did not respond to charmed, psionically coerced, or otherwise controlled mind. The sash could be transferred by touch, but this caused severe damage to both the caster and receiver due to the wild surges involved. The release of a spell was swift enough that it could be used in the same round as an actual spell.

The magic had no maximum time span, and would hold for years without trouble. Only very powerful spells, such as limited wish or wish would deactivate a sash; dispel magic would not force so much as one spell from it. Trying to cast a new sash while already girded with one caused the second sash to fizzle out. If the wearer of a sash died, the sash discharged all of its spells around with random targets, usually with destructive results.

Finally, the sash of spells removed all need for sleep from the wearer.

Components
The spell called only for verbal and somatic components.

History
The spell was referenced by Volo in Volo's Guide to All Things Magical; the wizard claimed it had originated in Halruaa, and that it was a paltry modern equivalent of an old Netherese Mantle.

## Sand flea

Sand fleas were tiny parasitic insects that caused extreme discomfort to creatures they infested.

Ecology
Sand fleas inhabited sandy beaches. An infestation of these insects often was treated as a highly infectious disease. Their bites and movements caused host creatures extreme itching and discomfort. These tiny insects caused misery and discomfort, preventing the host from resting and healing normally, causing them to become irritable and distracted. Sand fleas could jump great distances, allowing the infestation to spread quickly from one host to another.

Sand flea infestation could be summoned via the infestation spell.

## Sauk

Sauk was a Malarite hunter, and one of the Blades of the Old Man. He was a part of the team that went to look for Kheil and secure the Erael'len in the Northern Shalhoond, conspiring to kill the Old Man of the Mountain.
Appearance
Sauk was a full head taller than Berun, and well muscled. In, he had cut one scar from the crown of his forehead to his cheek, a luzal unba; he cut two more during Tarsakh.
Personality
Sauk was violent and cunning, but loyal to a fault, tolerating otherwise impossible annoyances and resistance from Kheil.
History
When Sauk was fifteen, he killed his father's son. He joined the Blades of the Old Man at some point between then and, when he was a part of the raid on the Yuirwood where Kheil was captured; he considered that the blackest of his life.

After Talieth discovered Kheil was alive, Sauk had sent Taaki to lure him out of the steppes, looking for his aid in slaying the Old Man of the Mountain. After finding him and learning he went by Berun, he told him that Chereth was still alive, that the Old Man of the Mountain drained his druidic powers for his own use in between tortures. Though Berun offered to bring a force of servants of Silvanus, Sauk refused to allow that many outsiders to enter the hidden fortress. To get Berun to go with him, he attempted to threaten Lewan, his apprentice. After Berun's escape attempt ended in him being swallowed by a shambling mound, he took Lewan back to Sentinelspire anyway, expecting him to maybe be of some use.

During a conversation with Talieth, he realized Berun might not have died. He took the occassion of the vigil ordained by the Old Man of the Mountain to murder several blades loyal to the master of Sentinelspire. Later, when Lewan requested to be allowed to purify himself in the wild, it was Sauk that accompanied him; the half-orc offered the young man the chance to flee, if he so desired. He witnessed the exchange between Lewan and the Old Man of the Mountain, then questioned the pupil about it, finally relating this to Talieth.

On the night of Tarsakh 25, he fought several blades loyal to the Old Man, at least one killoren, and was instrumental in defeating the Old Man himself.
Relationships
Sauk considered Kheil to be closer than a brother, and even cut one luzal unba for him when he thought him dead. He considered only Talieth to be more powerful than he was. His arumwon, or animal companion, was a tiger named Taaki.

## Safety net

A safety net was a rope enchanted with Darsson Spellmaker's safe fall spell.

Description
These ropes were made from giant spider or ettercap silk and were available in lengths of, and .

Powers
Once tied into a loop, a safety net created a feather fall effect that operated both vertically, horizontally, and at any angle. The safety net would eliminate the perpendicular inertia of whatever approached it. Thus, a safety net placed horizontally would impart a feather fall for someone falling down on top of it.

The limit on the amount of weight that could be affected was  plus a number of pounds equal to twice the rope's length in feet. Multiple nets could be used to increase the area affected, but the weight limit was determined by the smaller net.

## Salmon Bay

Geography
Salmon Bay jutted south into the island of Alaron where the Sea of Moonshae and the Sea of Swords met. It cut southeast into the kingdom of Gnarhelm for approximately, nearly bisecting the kingdom into eastern and western halves. The southern end of Salmon Bay was connected to the Vasty Drink by a small unnamed river. The mountains of Hardangheight framed the bay's western shore.

Sailing up and down the Bay could be dangerous and time consuming, which forced the Northlanders of Gnarhelm to become more reliant on overland travel than most of their seafaring kin across the Moonshae Isles. Pirates could travel down the bay to pillage the land.

## Salana

Salana was a pahari sea mage inhabiting the waters of the Great Sea near Zarastro's Isle near the Corsair Domains in .

Description
In her natural form, Salana's lower body was that of a large fish, while her upper body resembled a human woman. Her scales were ruby red in color. Her red hair and striking green eyes made her quite attractive. While immersed in sea water she was capable of changing her form into that of a teenage girl who stood  tall. She was quite oblivious to her beauty and never wore clothes regardless of her form. If threatened while underwater she would change into the form of a small red fish in order to escape. She was 60 years old as of 1367 DR.

Personality
Though young and naive, Salana was intelligent and headstrong. She was never afraid to voice her opinion.

Abilities
Salana usually had the following spells memorized: charm person, friends, light, sea sight, fog cloud, mirror image, and converse with sea creatures.

A kiss from Salana would grant the recipient the ability to breathe underwater for a full day.

Possessions
Salana's spellbook contained spells inscribed on purple shells she wore as a belt. Her material components were kept in a pouch of woven kelp.

Activities
She was quite adventurous and enjoyed exploring shipwrecks and undersea ruins.

## Sahuagin language

The sahuagin language was the racial language of the sahuagin.

Speakers
Beyond sahuagin themselves, this was a common secondary language was among sea elves of the Inner Sea and Outer Sea,  even though the two races were typically mortal enemies.

Some of the individual specialty priests of aquatic deities were known to learn various other aquatic languages, such as Sahuagin. These included the sea druids of Deep Sashelas, the waveservants of Umberlee, and the waterwalkers of Istishia. It was also a common secondary language among jungle druids, specialty priests in the Church of Ubtao, who sought to learn the exotic languages of the creatures that lived around the Chultan Peninsula.

Phonology
The language of the sahuagins was difficult for most surface dwellers to understand without some magical aid, as it involved a combination of body language and sounds made through the gills.

Derivative Languages
Serusan, a pidgin language used by the various aquatic races of Serôs, featured elements of the sahuagin language.

Sahuagin Dictionary
K
Kuaumin
 "Noble Malenti". Translates literally as "traitor-kin of Sekolah".
Kurrimal
 "rip tide"; "rip current". Can be translated literally as "the danger waiting beneath the surf".
M
Malenti
 "ugly one".

Notable Speakers
 Ashakar Piscelene, a triton wizard, was capable of speaking and understanding this language.
 Zehira Deryan, a human privateer.

## Saint Dobla

She later was sanctified by the Church of Ilmater and became the namesake of St. Dobla's Alley. Due to having been a jhasina in life, some crudely believed that the members of Dobla's order engaged in wanton activities. This lead to the development of the insult, "as pure as Dobla's dancers," which was used against heroes and paladins to imply that they were lacking in decency and virtue.

## Sathra

Sathra was the master of the crime syndicate of Laothkund known as the Shadow Tongue and a necromancer until .

Description
Sathra wore an abundance of black iron jewelry, and she was rather portly in build. An aura of animated, suffering shadows surrounded her.

Personality
Sathra was unexpectedly tenacious for a crime lord, craving Angul so badly as to chase its wielder into the Yuirwood.

Abilities
In combat, Sathra could call upon the shadows that writhed around her to bring them to bear against many enemies. She was also capable of summoning demons. She also had the power to bring people from the grave as undead. When in combat with Kiril Duskmourn, she employed a number of spells, including one that seemed to temporarily overwhelm Angul'''s healing powers, possibly graymantle.

Possessions
Sathra's office doubled as the Shadow Tongue's treasury. As of, she had recently purchased a torture device known as a "Sembian Cradle".

Activities
As the head of the Shadow Tongue, Sathra was involved in a number of crimes, and in plans to corrupt the ruling council of the city.

Relationships
Sathra's personal guard was known as the Shadow Cadre. She knew the demon Demoriel by name.

History
In, the thief Gage broke into the Shadow Tongue lair looking for the enchanted elven sword Angul, which the Shadow Tongue had stolen.Gage says Sathra was after Angul. He found a way into the vault through sheer luck, after Sathra left the door open while leaving. She had been injured by trying to touch the blade herself. She caught the thief not long after, but wielding the blade, he managed to slay many of her bodyguards and even a summoned demon before she retreted.

She pursued Gage and the sword's wielder, Kiril Duskmourn, into the Yuirwood later. The star elf slew her in combat at that time.

Rumors & Legends
Sathra was rumoured to have a Red Room in the Shadow Tongue lair where she performed unknown tortures on her victims.

The thief Gage had told some lies to Kiril about Sathra having been hired by a certain Nangulis, per Telarian's indications, to manipulate her into heading for Stardeep. Kiril slew Sathra without first asking about such things.

## Saros

Saros was the Lord of Turmish who rose to power in 992 DR.

History
In the, the Merchant Princes lost control of Turmish. An ambitious warlord and charismatic leader, Lord Saros, convinced the military forces of Turmish to support him as a potential ruler of the nation. Lord Saros proclaimed that the leadership of Turmish needed to be in the hands of the man who "knows what this country needs." Lord Saros, of course, became that man and took the throne. Under his leadership, Turmish rapidly became a naval force to be reckoned with. He sponsored construction of ports in the Free Cities of the Vilhon Reach, namely in Hlath and Reth, and forged an alliance with the city of Hlondeth.

Lord Saros' legacy was Turmish becoming the guardian of the Vilhon's waterways and gaining security that allowed it to flourish. By the, Turmish's population grew more than twice its size.

## Salt dragon

Salt dragons, also called yellow dragons (which was, confusingly, a name shared with another species), were a species of chromatic dragon that dwelled in salt marshes and similar high-salinity environments.

Description
Salt dragons had small scales of pale yellow and whitish hues that helped them blend in with the salt-encrusted land of their preferred habitats. At hatching, however, their scales were noted to be slightly crystalline and bright, lemon-yellow in color; the color faded over time but the size of the scales, interestingly, never increased. Salt dragons had large claws in proportion to their size, which ranged from as little as  in length at hatching, to as much as  as great wyrms.

Personality
Salt dragons were orderly but cruel, delighting in tormenting other creatures, especially good ones.

Abilities
Salt dragons had various typically draconic abilities, as well as some peculiar to them. They were variously said to be resistant to metallic poisons and poisonous gases, or to be wholly immune to poison altogether. They were natural spellcasters, as well as having certain innate spell-like abilities: very young salt dragons could replicate pass without trace thrice each day, young salt dragons gained the ability to breathe underwater via magic, young adults could lower water twice each day, old salt dragons could control wind once each day, venerable salt dragons could cast protection from lightning once each day, and great wyrms could use hallucinatory terrain once each day.

Salt dragons took one of their names from their breath weapon: a cone of sodium chloride crystals some  long and  wide at the end. The salt caused great pain in those struck by it, and could choke or blind creatures; in severe cases after repeated hits, foes were known to end up permanently blinded. Immersion in fresh water could wash away the salt, but transmute rock to mud did not effect the crystals. Underwater, the breath weapon came out as a cloud of extremely salty water that had much the same effects but did not last quite so long.

Combat
Salt dragons typically used their breath weapon first in combat, before concentrating their attacks on any blinded foes. They preferred not to fight near fresh water, since that could be used to counteract the salt-blindness, and if they had to they would use lower water to reduce that weakness. After their breath weapon, they mostly relied on their claws and fangs in melee.

Salt dragons preferred to retreat from fights they were losing, often by swimming away from land-bound enemies and exiting the water to escape aquatic enemies.

Ecology
Salt dragons lived in salt marshes, rocky seashores, and salt flats. Younger salt dragons lived more inland, while older salt dragons had the power and abilities necessary to begin hunting at sea. They spoke their own dialect of Draconic and were known to live either alone or in small family units; in such groups the female acted as the hunter while the male guarded the nest and young/eggs.

Salt dragons were known to cooperate with lesser, lawful evil creatures: sahuagin if on the coast, humans and demihumans (those who could survive in salt marshes) if inland. The dragons provided protection in exchange for obedience and information.

Salt dragons were carnivorous and ate meat of all kinds; those who lived inland typically preyed on herd animals and cattle, while those on the shore preyed on fish and marine mammals. Their evil nature meant that they were known to seek out settlements of intelligent creatures that they raided, tormented, and preyed upon.

Salt dragons, interestingly, could and would salt excess meat to keep as later meals for both themselves and their servitors. They preferred to drink salt water, but were capable of drinking fresh water if necessary.

Coast-dwelling salt dragons came into conflict with bronze dragons, who were typically more powerful both physically and magically, as well as larger; consequently, salt dragons preferred to fight bronze dragons alongside their sahuagin allies. A venerable or older salt dragon might consider challenging a bronze dragon that was an adult or younger, though only after using protection from lightning.

Hatchling or very young salt dragons were sometimes preyed upon by bullywugs or yuan-ti. Although salt dragons were often aware of any black dragons that dwelled in swamps farther upriver, the two species rarely interacted.

In some theories of draconic hybridization, a yellow (salt) dragon might crossbreed with a red dragon to produce an orange dragon, or with a blue dragon to produce a green dragon.

## Sapra

Sapra was the only port city on the isle of Ilighôn of the Eyes of Silvanus, in the Sea of Fallen Stars, off the shores of the Vilhon Reach.

Description
Sapra was the only port on the entire island. The city was calm, quiet, and possibly, the cleanest settlement in all of Faerûn. The city was considered to be one of the most expensive places in the Vilhon Reach.

The city welcomed those who wanted to flee oppression by wizards. Sapra aided refugees from the lands ruled by spell-casting tyrants, and its inhabitants even included several "retired" Red Wizards who fled Thay. The island's magics shielded the ex-wizards from Thayan divinations.

Geography

Sapra stood on a small bay on the southeast of Ilighôn, directly in front of the cape passage gap in the reefs of Eldath's Ring that encircled the island. The Great Forest and Mielikki's Garden surrounded the city on land, while its harbors were awash with waters of the Sea of Fallen Stars through the Strait of Silvanus and the Farshore Straits. The Hierophant trail led from the city of Sapra towards the Oakenhall, or the House of Silvanus at the heart of the island. The Lake Bluesprings stood to the west from Sapra. The lake was a popular getaway for the city folk and indicated the unspoken border past which locals never ventured deeper into Ilighôn's wildwoods.

Government
The city of Sapra was ruled and guarded by the druids of the Emerald Enclave who venerated both Silvanus and Eldath. Not a single unwanted nor unwelcome visitor was allowed to enter the harbors of Sapra under the druids' watchful eye. Direct rulership of Sapra was in the hands of a council of six elders whose position on the council was a part-time job. The council met as the need arose, usually once a month. In the late-14 century DR, the leader of the council was the local blacksmith, Mayor Thomas Flagcairn.

The city of Sapra was mandated by the Emerald Enclave to control its size and population. In the late-14 century, Sapra's population was at 2,000 souls, pushing the Emerald Enclave's limits. Due to the number and growing size, the town's lodging and housing prices were on the rise as well. However, by the later parts of the 1370s DR, Sapra's population expanded dramatically to over 3,000 souls.

Trade
Sapra's export was humble and consisted mainly of foodstuff and various herbs, all produced by the island's lush forests. The slow trade only had a handful of merchant ships docking in Sapra every month. Because of that, most things not produced locally were valued highly. Locals spent a lot of time negotiating trades and bartering.

Defenses

Sapra's harbors and waters surrounding the isle of Ilighôn were defended by the Seven Sentinels of Silvanus, a group of water and air elementals who pummeled unwanted visitors and washed ashore allies whose vessels suffered damage or drowning innocents. The Seven Sentinels also guided friendly ships across the perilous reefs of Eldath's Ring towards Sapra. Additionally, Sapra, just like the entirety of Ilighôn, was shrouded in the faith-magic zone, a gift from Mystra, goddess of magic, to Silvanus. While on the island, all arcane magic was negated, permanent and long-lasting spells fizzled out, and wizardly magic items stopped functioning.

History
By the, Sapra had been peacefully existing under the control, protection, and policies of the Emerald Enclave for over 200 years. The city existed under strict growth and expansion limitations mandated by the Emerald Enclave. In the period of time from 1360 to 1370 DR, not a single new building permit was issued in Sapra, explaining the astronomical process of homes, land, and lodging. The borders of the city were laid out circa 1320 DR and were never again adjusted by the city's green masters until Sapra was finally allowed to expand by the .

Everything changed with the advent of the Spellplague that reshaped the Vilhon Reach and drained the Sea of Fallen Stars. The Eyes of Silvanus were no longer islands, instead becoming the new edge of Turmish and later its only port city. In the 100 years that followed, the city's population exploded to more than double its size—8,500 souls included the locals, druids, and Turamis. In the, the Second Sundering undid the changes and scars of the magical calamity, returning the Vilhon Reach to its former shape, making Sapra a secluded port of the isle of Ilighôn once more.

Notable Locations
 City Hall, the place for the council's gathering, center of the city's bureaucracy, and archives.
 The Dazzling Dolphin, an expensive and clean inn that charged ten times the average for lodgings.
 The Temple of Tempus, a large temple dedicated to the Lord of Battles.
 The Shrine to Eldath, one of the few small places of worship in the city.

## Saura Umakra

Saura Umakra was a Red Wizard, and apprentice of Nusair Darkul. In Hammer of, she recovered the  Scalamagdrion and used it to kill her master, inheriting his red robes.

Description
Saura bore a dragon's claw tattoo encircling her left eye, which glowed at the touch of her master, and an intrincate tattoo on her throat.

Personality
Saura was ambitious and ruthless, apprenticing herself to the repellent and lecherous Nusair Darkul in order to become a Red Wizard. She also was treacherous, eventually setting a trap for her master so as to inherit his robes with the blessing of his enemy.

Abilities
Saura could employ light. In contrast to her apparently meager magical ability, she was able to master the Scalamagdrion within the space of three days.

Possessions
Saura carried a serrated dirk. She also had her master's robes, and eventually, the Scalamagdrion.

Activities
Saura was her master's lover as well as apprentice, though she detested it.

Relationships
Saura Umakra was Nusair Darkul's apprentice and lover, knowing him best of all people in Thay.

History
In mid-Hammer of 1372, Nusair Darkul sent her to Skuld, where she was to retrieve the Scalamagdrion from a private collection. In return, he promised to sponsor her as a Red Wizard.

While in Skuld, she spent two days surveiling the bookstore where her target was kept, and then broke in at night. She was caught by the proprietor, Mephnit Nandem, who told her that he had summoned the Guard; she tried to negotiate, but after realizing it was not going to happen quickly enough, she simply slew him and took the book.

Three days later, she returned to Darkul Tower. She was, however, passed up for promotion. Unknown to him, however, before Nusair Darkul's final reneging on sponsoring her as a red wizard, Saura had learned how to control the Scalamagdrion''', and used it to kill the red wizard. She intended to reach out to Samas Kul afterwards for her sponsoring.The text is ambiguous on the timing of Samas Kul's involvement; the tense suggests that she may have already reached out, but it could also be worded as a desiderative (a hope or wishing out loud) on her part.

In the summer of the, pastorale parties became all the rage among Selgaunt's nobility. One such event was organized by Chenna Talendar of Selgaunt. Her simple pesant's garb was woven of the finest sarcanet and cambric.

Description
Santhro was a powerful cleric and warrior who served as the High Priest of Bezantur's Temple of Garagos.

Personality
Santhro was devoted to Garagos. In the face of doubts about his god's power, he would point to his powerful spellcasting abilities as evidence of Garagos's greatness. He was charismatic enough to dispel his few followers' doubts as well.

He was pragmatic enough to convert his temple into an inn when money became tight—in which capacity the temple was known as Santhro's Rooming House—although he was proud enough that he refused to cook for his guests unless they asked him nicely.

History
Santhro opened his Rooming House in the mid-to-late 14 century DR in order to make extra money in the face of increasing doubt about his deity in Bezantur.

## Saw

Saws were cutting tools used to carve through wood, stone, steel, limbs, and more.

Description
Saws had toothed metal blades, but otherwise varied in size, shape, and use. Types included:

 Amputation saws, a category of smaller saws used to remove limbs and prevent gangrene.
 Crosscut saw, the most common type of saw which sold for 3 gp from Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue.
 Folding saw, a portable saw large enough to fell trees
 Framed jigsaw (8gp), which was sometimes sold with diamond teeth for the purpose of cutting through stone (17 gp).
 Hacksaw (5 gp), used for cutting through metal with diamond-toothed versions (15 gp) able to cut through stone and even more superior versions (20 gp) able to cut through anything short of diamond itself.
 Two-person logging saw (15 gp), which could cut through wood five times faster than a common saw.
 Two-person ripsaw (7 gp).

Saws were ill-suited to be used as weapons.

## Sarah of Yellowcrest Manor

Sarah of Yellowcrest Manor was the diary of Sarah, a servant in the employ of the ill-fated Yellowcrest family of Waterdeep.

Description
Bound in leather and decorated with intricate brass corner fittings and a clasp in the same material, the cover of Sarah of Yellowcrest Manor was embossed with the crest of the Yellowcrest family: a rising sun behind a lemon tree. The book had clearly been blank originally; all its pages were handwritten, with only the very last pages remaining unscrawled. The writing steadily improved over the course of the book, as if the product of an author learning to write. The title Sarah of Yellowcrest Manor was written on its very first page.

Contents
The first few pages of Sarah of Yellowcrest Manor consisted of repetitions of the word "Sarah", as the author practiced her handwriting. This eventually gave way to lists of household chores, names, upcoming activities, and shopping items—these comprised roughly a third of the book. At some point, Sarah began a diary telling of her life in the service of the Yellowcrests. Most of the entries were undated and none were on consecutive pages.

In addition to chronicling her life as a servant in Yellowcrest Manor, Sarah wrote about her romantic relationship with the local squire Vecken. She also wrote at length about the noble Yellowcrest family, their business holdings, and their associates. The diary told of how Sarah had been taught how to write by Lady Maria Yellowcrest, who had taken the young servant girl under her wing—the diary had been a gift from her.

In the last few entries, dated to the, Sarah wrote of her growing concern for the Yellowcrest family. In particular, she found Lord Viallis Yellowcrest's obsession with ancient tomes and strange rituals disconcerting. Sarah sketched Viallis's ritual circle in her diary, as well as a strange book with a monstrous cover she once saw him chanting from; unbeknownst to her, this was Retribution of the Ancients, a deadly tome describing grim rituals.

History
Shortly after the diary's last entry, the Yellowcrest family was brutally massacred in their manor, their children and servants included. The only survivor was Lord Vallis, who had apparently been away on business when the gruesome murders occurred. In reality, Vallis had orchestrated the massacre himself as part of a ritual to contact the Great Old One Gaernoo, an aberrant being from the Far Realm. Viallis was never accused of having any connection to the murders, and left Waterdeep shortly after, claiming he suffered from a deep depression. In actuality, Vallis poured his efforts into establishing the Burned Tongue Cult to venerate Gaernoo.

Yellowcrest Manor was taken over by the Falgarst family, and the possessions of the late Yellowcrests and their servants were sold off at a street sale. It was here that the apprentice wizard Sirok bought Sarah of Yellowcrest Manor, later using it as payment to gain access to the fortress library of Candlekeep. The Avowed accepted the book, taking note of the grim story Sirok told of the unexplained murder of the Yellowcrests—the wizard had not actually read the book, instead merely relaying the gossip he had overheard. Finding nothing remarkable about the book, the Avowed cataloged it and promptly forgot all about it.

Unbeknownst to all, the grieving ghost of Sarah resided within the book, just waiting for someone to open it up so she could tell the truth of what happened to the Yellowcrest family. As of the, Sarah of Yellowcrest Manor remained unnoticed in the stacks of Candlekeep.

## Sanctified magic

Sancitified magic was a type of magic.

Sanctified magic required the caster to be completely and utterly devoted to good. Sanctified spells were incredibly powerful. However, they could only be cast with a significant sacrifice from the caster. Evil spellcasters were unable to cast any sanctified spell.

Sanctified spells were prepared just as normal spells. Unprepared sanctified spells could only be used from a scroll. Sanctified spells usually required verbal and somatic components but rarely a material component. Some also required a divine focus. All sanctified spells were temporary and expired.

All sanctified spells required a sacrifice from the caster. The sacrifice required varied, from a temporary draining of the caster's strength to their life itself. The sacrifice occured when the spell expired.

A sanctified spell could be made into an item, such as a potion, scroll or wand. Any individual of good-alignment could use said item, and the required sacrifice was taken from them instead of the individual who created the item.

Sanctified spells were unique in that they were neither an arcane nor a divine spell. Good-aligned druids, paladins, rangers, sorcerers and wizards could all cast a sanctified spell, but clerics can spontaneously cast any sanctified spell.

## Salted nuts

Salted nuts were a type of traditional tavern snack in the nations of Cormyr, the Dalelands, and several other places along the Sword Coast.

Availability
The Jade Dancer festhall in Waterdeep served bowls of salted nuts, hot garlic and butter breads, and roasted fowl skewers, as drink accompaniments. The similar snacks were available at the House of Good Spirits on Sorn Street.
In the Cormyrean caravan town of Waymoot, salted nuts were served at The Moon and Stars tavern as a free bar snack.
Daggerford's Lady Luck Tavern served salted nuts along with sugared breads and cheese-covered hardbreads.
Blackwater Stout of Blackfeather Bridge in Featherdale put out salted nuts, roasted almonds, cold shrimp, and twist cream puffs, for the drinking guests.

History
In the, War Wizards stationed in Waymoot enchanted salted nuts bowls in the Moon and Stars to spy on a newly formed adventuring company—the Swords of Eveningstar. Semoor Wolftooth of the troupe was bewildered by the nut bowl being taken away only to be returned back moments later, but noblewoman Narantha Crownsilver, used to Red Wizards' machination, informed the group of the magical spying.

## Saldee

Saldee was a sailor aboard the Vigilant circa 1374 DR.

Description
Saldee was a strong human woman with short-trimmed hair. She was aiming for a Captain's position and venerated both the Bitch Queen Umberlee and the Lady of Misfortune Beshaba. Saldee had a focused demeanor, strict personality, and she disliked jokes and levity.

History
Saldee was part of the Vigilant's crew in the  when Captain Lastri Kassireh's ship sailed from the Sword Coast to the nation of Samarach on the Chultan Peninsula. Onboard the ship were several passengers, including Volothamp Geddarm who traveled to meet with a wealthy yuan-ti merchant, Sa'Sani. The voyage was cursed with bad luck and numerous strange occurrences. Food supplies were mysteriously spoiled; freshwater barrels ruptured; one of the crew, a sailor, got drunk and drowned; Volo almost got poisoned as his poison soup took the life of Joquin; and a fellow sailor Rienur noticed new ropes becoming strangely frayed.

The string of suspicious occurrences – yuan-ti sabotage, culminated in a deadly storm. The Vigilant's shipwreck was washed ashore not far from the city of Samargol. Most sailors perished in the storm, while others were captured to be devoured by the local cannibalistic Batiri goblins. Saldee was in a group of survivors of the crash that included Volo and a handful of passengers who had to defend themselves from hungry Batiri. Eventually, the survivors were placed under arrest by xenophobic Samarachan Elite Guard and taken to Samargol.
