## Sabral Faryre

Sabral Faryre was the Magister for six days in, prior to which he was a courtier in Procalith, within Tashalar.

Description
He was tall, agile, and handsome.

Personality
He was a good actor, witty, and capable of pleasant speech. He was also bloody and ruthless in his means, though he claimed not to harbor intentions of being a tyrant.

Abilities
He had a spell he once used to set dead bodies afire, which allowed him to claim the mantle of the Magister.

History
Originally a courtier in Procalith, after the previous Magister and crown princess, Nasana Melnuthquel, died of heartstop as soon as she had slain the queen, Sabral set the body alight with a spell and proclaimed himself the Magister. Immediately, he slew the king, too, declaring himself king of Procalith.

As king, he quickly had all his rivals, and many powerful warriors, slain in front of him. Then he had a number of magelings rounded up; five were brought to him, while one committed suicide rather than be at his mercy. He quickly sought to ingratiate himself with the five, explaining that his bloody coup was necessary in order to gain the populace's obedience, and naming them his Council. He claimed he meant to become the Magister, and leave it to them to rule Procalith, concerning himself only with preventing them from acting against one other.

He was found dead days later, with a harpoon driven through both him and the throne, most likely through the use of a spell. Lady Mimré of the Council thought to claim the mantle of Magister from him, but nothing happened. His Council were all found dead within a tenday.

## Samous

Samous, whose self-proclaimed nickname was the Rapturious Songster, was a maestro inhabiting the city of Daggerford in the Western Heartlands during the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Activities
Samous was famous for buying a large plot of land in Daggerford and designing and erecting a monstrous building called the Grand Playhouse. The Grand Playhouse was located in central Daggerford along Duke's Way, the High Road, and the Farmer's Road. Samous' goal with the massive theater was to bring high culture to Daggerford by hiring skilled actors and singers from great cities such as Waterdeep. However, the citizenry of Daggerford showed little interested in such forms of entertainment and Samous was quickly forced to close the doors to the Grand Playhouse. He left the city a penniless man shortly thereafter.

## Sarndaen Dalabar

Sarndaen Dalabar was the Magister from  to . He became known as "The Opener", for his work on documenting portals.

Personality
Sarndaen Dalabar often played the part of a shy, bumbling apprentice with barely any ambition. His greatest passion was exploration.

Abilities
Besides being amongst the most puissant magisters, Sarndaen Dalabar was an excellent actor.

History
Before his challenge of Meldryn Jalensifer, Sarndaen Dalabar had been the apprentice to three wizardly masters in Athkatla, all of which eventually embroiled themselves in mercantile intrigues that became their undoing. After his challenge, he spent a few days in Meldryn Jalensifer's company, prior to finally slaying him with three meteor swarms.

Once the Magister, he worked to keep the most powerful mages opposed to each other, so they wouldn't turn their attention to him; he fulfilled his task to spread magic by giving magic to a few promising apprentices and calling on them to spread magic on his behalf. Then he, personally, devoted himself to exploring and making a map of all the gates he came across. Besides filling up books, he created the Opener's map, in a castle within the Ice Mountains; his work was cut short when he died in an avalanche after crossing through an unrecorded portal.

Rumors & Legends
Sources stress that though there was nothing left down about Sarndaen being responsible for the downfall of his three previous masters, there would be nothing recorded either way.

## Sap (spell)

Sap was a spell used by clerics of Rillifane Rallathil.

Effects
When cast, this spell covered an area,  per side, with tree sap. Any creature within the area of effect was subsequently covered in the sticky sap and their movement slowed considerably. The sap was thick enough to prevent physical attacks and the casting of spells that required movement. An individual could free themselves with a free action spell or similar effect. Pouring Copious amounts of wine onto an individual could also work.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required material components, the holy symbol of Rillifane Rallathil and a small amount of sap from a tree.

## Sauringar Tome

The Sauringar Tome was a prayer book that belonged to House Sauringar and used in worship of the Slitherer Supreme—the deity Sseth—before 1374 DR.

Powers
The ancient and well-worn prayer book possessed a minor divine enchantment that improved the owner's protection against poisons.

Contents
Most of the Sauringar Tome was written in the Yuan-ti language, and a lot of its contents were unreadable because of age. However, parts of it were scribed in an archaic dialect of Common. What was still readable included prayers and rituals dedicated to the worship of Sseth, the Slitherer Supreme, and sacrificial customs of the tsaa olo, that is, the church's clergy. The text indicated that worshipers of the deity willingly volunteered to de sacrificed by the clergy for the glory of the snake god. The tome also talked about the 'Sacred Way of Sseth' adopted by the yuan-ti people, these being the ways of manipulation and subterfuge to gain power and control.

History
The temple where the tome was kept in stood deep in the Samarlogh jungles of Samarach. In the late 14 century DR, the yuan-ti House Sauringar turned away from worshiping of Sseth in favor of the more aggressive snake deity Zehir. With it, temples dedicated to Sseth were abandoned. The Sauringar Tome's temple was guarded by Serpentguard D'shai‎ circa the .

## Safehold

A safehold or hideaway was a name used for extradimensional spaces in Faerûn, typically livable ones used for protection and privacy. Some sages called them "elf holds".

Characteristics
Practically all safeholds were accessible only from one location; most required a certain key as well, usually an item or a spoken word. A few keys could be more arcane and complex.

A safehold somehow weakened the planar fabric, making the location vulnerable to interplanar intrusion; this was said to have been a decisive factor in the damage that Myth Drannor took from the Army of Darkness.

True safeholds were extradimensional spaces; their entrance portal was often opaque and invisible. Safeholds were finite, and typically rather small, about the size of a room with enough room for four people or maybe twelve standing up. Being extradimensional, they kept off most forms of detection. They copied the traits from the plane to which they were adjunct, typically the Prime Material plane.

They had a single continuous wall encircling them, typically covered over with some construction, and some form of illumination. True safeholds always had some manner of refreshing air, through invisible links to the Material Plane.

Construction
Safeholds had been created in profusion by the elves of Faerûn, but they were not created anymore by the 14 century DR. Per the Knights of Myth Drannor, that ruined city had held hundreds of such safeholds in its heyday.

The air circulation of safeholds was made through special spells cast on stones that were then hurled high with slings; the highest point reached by such stones became a small portal for air absorption.

The construction of safeholds seemed to have been greatest during the centuries after the fall of Myth Drannor; the spells for the construction of safeholds had been just about lost by .
The construction of safeholds called not just for spells that cloaked and locked the entrance, but also to circulate air and keep the temperature. Some even had means of removing personal waste well away from the location. As it was not common knowledge, a would-be safehold creator needed to devise the process anew; it might have required the insides of transdimensional creatures, or special binding rites, akin to those of a weapon with a legacy. Candlekeep may have held sufficient knowledge to get a creator, had he already surpassed conventional magic, started.

The would-be creator needed first to create the extradimensional space, anchor it to a given location, and then shape it correctly. If they wanted to create portals afterwards or add new features, unique magic items would need to be devised, then linked to the walls. One of them would become the portal for the entrance; other, unknown spells would have to be chosen for the temperature and humidity desired, as well as for links that would furnish it with air, and the ultimate resilience and stability that the space needed.

These spells, known in the days of Myth Drannor, were unknown in . Without them, the space would be swept in a matter of days, twist itself into a cavity full of wild magic, or worse. The spells linking the safehold to external air sources without allowing anything inside were also lost; modern safeholds used smaller portals, which could let in other things as well. The knowledge of the spells necessary to create these magically hidden lairs and safehouses remained a secret shared between extremely powerful mages and other arcane spellcasters, such as liches, who existed for a number of lifetimes, by that year.

The Sword Heralds created a number of safeholds starting in 620 DR. In particular, they created in 640 DR a labyrinth full of foul beasts, for the amusement of House Auantiver's youth. This was the site of the foundational event for the Men of the Basilisk in 1247 DR; they were looking for more safeholds into the late 14 century DR.

Safehold Collapse
A safehold whose walls were struck with disintegrate, or subject to other magical effects, might expel all of its contents through a portal, with the force of an energy vortex.

Notable Safeholds
Cormyr
The phantom skull in the Elfskull Inn was said to be the marker for the entrance of a safehold belonging to the Sword Heralds.
A ring once given by Vangerdahast Aeiulvana to Azoun Obarskyr IV transported him and Queen Filfaeril Obarskyr to a safehold, amongst other unfortunate locations.
Suzail's Palace of the Purple Dragon was known to have at least one. The latter was rumored to be festooned with the bones of would-be usurpers.

The Dalelands
An elven safehold crammed with weapons was said to exist in the Sharburg, within Tegal's Mark, summonable with the right words.
Either Volothamp Geddarm or Elminster claimed to know of a safehold accessible in the Dales by leaping off of the Standing Stone at a certain time of day, which led to a labyrinth guarded by a dozen beings resembling liches. Depending on the tales, the labyrinth's center concealed a spell library, a stair to the Underdark, a portal to the Outer Planes, or a Cult of the Dragon cache.

Cormanthor
Many mages had attested to a safehold accessible in Myth Drannor from an once magnificent building, which held a number of amenities but was guarded by a death tyrant whose eyes emerged from the walls that attacked anyone who attempted to remove the contents. They also attested to a sensation of being surveilled, perhaps even mentally, while resting there. They attested to a decanter of endless water and Murlynd's spoon being available for their use.
The Speculum, in Myth Drannor, was the site of a safehold, accessible by placing a magical key in a hole in the flagstone floor of its central hall. It was believed to have been established by Alaghlar of the Horns.
A hidden safehold in the western, older part of Myth Drannor was known to hold a deepspawn.
Castle Cormanthor had enough safeholds that, with their full use, it could stand to hold up to eight thousand people.

Amn
A safehold dubbed "The Nighthole" was said to exist in Athkatla, in the use of an unscrupulous, secret merchant society, and to be accessed only at night.
The Cowled Wizards kept 16 safeholds across Amn.
Emperor Shoon VII had a number of safeholds.

Moonsea
The mage Perendra Raslemtar of Voonlar had a safehold on the path to her root cellar, attached to a portal; the words "Orntharm Felderenslor" altered the portal's destination to be a safehold of hers. Anyone casting a spell there was ejected from the space.

Sword Coast
Elves had built safeholds into the walls of Belkram's Fall in Undermountain.
The Tel'Teukiira had their base of operations in the Silver Safehold of the old Sword Heralds of Cormyr. The place was accessible through a Teukiir badge. Khelben Arunsun built the Silver Safehold around, with the aid of the Sword Heralds. It was connected to Blackstaff Tower by a gate in the cellars.

Other
Chessenta and Turmish were said to be riddled with safeholds.
Both Castle Waterdeep and Piergeiron's Palace were rumored to have such safeholds.
The Deep Imaskari who didn't flee into the Plains of Purple Dust after their defeat to the Mulhorandi led by Nezram were said to have fled to extraplanar safeholds.
Brymmyn Thorl had created unique safeholds, accessible from existing portals with a separate key. He added such safeholds to many a gate.
Aralagath Tarsil had discovered a safehold accessible from anywhere in Faerûn, the key to which involved a word, a gesture, and water or stone in the palm of his hand.
The Brotherhood of the Secret Quill had a mammooth vault known as the Vault of the Bound, for storing copies of their most important texts; this vault was also known as a safehold.
Priests of Mystryl were expected to keep magic safe into the future; amongst other locations, they were known to cache it in safeholds too.
The Knights of the Shield met in safeholds regularly.

Trivia
Elminster's evasion, when used by Elminster, took him to an undisclosed location which he called "Safehold". That location wasn't really a safehold, but a satellite orbiting H'Catha. Per some of the most obscure sages, it had been a gift from Azuth, after the original owner for whatever reason went missing.So Saith Ed Part 10. Gotta find the Candlekeep post. Other sages included the fact it had only been ever visited by Ao.

Some people spoke of "safeholds" where to hide money with no one knowing. Such a safehold could refer to a location or a service.

## Sacrosanct

Sacrosanct was a powerful divine abjuration and alteration spell from the wards sphere that kept intruders from entering temples.

Effects
The area to be protected was outlined with a radiant glow during the spell's casting. This area was spherical and centered on the caster. Its size depended on the skill of the caster. Once activated, the barrier intercepted intruders using magic or psionics to travel into the temple via the Astral Plane, ethereally, by teleportation, and so on. Once intercepted, intruders were transported to a random location in Faerûn.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as material components a piece of rubber, a small mirror or other reflective surface, and the eye of an owl or other creature that could see well in the dark.

History
This was an incredibly rare spell according to Volothamp Geddarm.

## Sanctified marker

Sanctified marker was a divine invocation spell from the creation sphere that created a permanent stone obelisk that served several purposes.

Effects
The sanctified marker was any size, shape, and color the caster desired, so long as it did not exceed twice their body length. Multiple sanctified markers could be stacked upon each other, and the marker would stand wherever it was set during casting, floating in midair if need be if there was no solid ground. A marker could be blank, bear a single symbol, or bear a single symbol and an inscription. None of the symbols or inscriptions could be magical in nature. A sanctified marker could be designed to explode the moment it was attacked with magic or a magic item.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as material components three drops of holy water and a stone the size of the caster's palm to cast.

History
Sanctified marker was granted to priests of Deneir, the Lord of All Glyphs.

## Sambranna Highstar

Sambranna Highstar, titled Seer of the Revealing Light, was a sage and seer of the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Activities

Sambranna undertook an extensive study of many of the great divine spellbooks of the Faerûnian pantheon, both cataloguing their spells and compiling their histories and known powers, as well writing of the nature of divine magic. Sambranna did this to inspire faith and prove the true might of the priests of the Realms, and covered all the major faiths equally to avoid weakening any one by sharing its secrets. The seer worked long and tirelessly, searched even in Myth Drannor, and presented queries even to the gods themselves. In this endeavor, Sambranna claimed to have the favor and protection of no less than three deities—Lathander, Mystra, and Oghma—and was confident of their protection should any there be any reprisals for the work.

History
Sambranna conducted research in the ruins of Myth Drannor in the, and wrote the preface to the work in a place called the Well of the Fallen there on Ches 12.

Appendix
Background
Sambranna Highstar is presented as the in-universe author of the real-world sourcebook Prayers from the Faithful, though the in-universe version of this work is not known.

References

## Sa'ib al-Banu

Imam Sa'ib al-Banu was a high priest and wererat inhabiting the ruined city of Moradask on the Sea of Salt in the Haunted Lands of interior Zakhara in 1367 DR.

Description
He stood  tall and was heavily muscled. He habitually shaved all of the hair from his body.

Personality
Sa'ib was quiet but spoke with wisdom and authority.

History
He was originally from Talab in the Cities of the Pantheon and received his training as a mystic there.

Activities
He experienced a strong vision once while adventuring within the Cities of the Ancients where the Pantheist gods revealed that his hair was a filthy trait and should be shorn from his body if he wished to attain spiritual perfection. Despite being openly ridiculed for his new belief system, Sa'ib gained a strong following in Talab until he and his followers were exiled for smuggling weapons. Fate guided Sa'ib and his followers to Moradask where they could prosper without harassment. It was here that Sa'ib discovered a shrine to Ishistu, a savage albino rat god. The idol of Ishistu lacked any body hair and Sa'ib took this as a sign to begin worship the rat god.

Abilities
Sa'ib usually had the following spells memorized: bless, command, cure light wounds, detect evil, endure heat, light, aid, augury, continual light, hold person, know alignment, silence, 15' radius, withdraw, wyvern watch, create food and water, dispel magic, glyph of warding, prayer, protection from fire, starshine, cure serious wounds, divination, protection from evil, flame strike, raise dead, blade barrier, heal.

Possessions
He carried several magic items including armor of command, a shield of the holy, a magic scimitar giant slayer, a ring of spell turning, and a potion of extra-healing. Sa'ib also armed himself with a mace, jambiya, a sling, and a razor.

## Sark

Sark was a crudely named stew served at the Copper Coronet tavern of Athkatla in the 14 century DR.

Description
The word "sark" was a crude expletive and an apt name for the dish. Sark was a stew cooked with Athkatlan harbor water and various unsavory leavings of the River District, like fish guts, catfish carcasses, cheap mussels, dog cadavers, rats, and dew-worms. The concoction was flavored with various herbs picked in the Grave District and served in hollowed-out stale moldy bread bowls.

History
Sarks were a popular but foul creation of Old Skratha, the cook of the Copper Coronet in the mid-to-late 14 century DR. These dishes were the only food available at the tavern and the only food its clientele could afford. Skratha was known to slip poison into a serving of the stew if compensated accordingly.

## Sasha (Council of Ten)

Sasha was a Council Clerk and, later, the youngest Councilmember of the Council of Ten from the city of Phlan in the mid 14 century DR.

History
By the, Sasha joined the Council of Ten as its youngest member in recognition of her prolific successes in dealing with the Silver Blades that enriched Phlan's coffers, as well as aiding in purging the city of corruption a decade prior. Just before the city of Phlan and other settlements on the shores of the Moonsea were spirited away by joined forces of the evil deity Bane, Councilmember Sasha left the city to inspect Phlan's outpost in the neighboring land of Thar. She was accompanied by bodyguards, yet they could not protect the young Councilwoman from falling victim to the new master of Taydome's Keep – mage Kimarr.

Kimarr was a one-time ally of Phlan but revealed himself as a traitor and sought to use Sasha as a bargaining chip allying with Bane's forces. He found a way to magically enslave ogres and giants of Taydome's Keep. His forces captured Sasha, Bostell – one of her bodyguards, and a fellow mage and ally of Phlan – Quil. Subsequently, Sasha was freed by the heroes of Phlan, while Kimarr was slain after he transformed himself into a fierce blue dragon.

## Saru

Saru were a species of intelligent gorilla-like apes that dwelt in the jungles of the Malatran Plateau of Kara-Tur.

Description
Saru's similarity to gorillas came from their strong large upper arms with the span of, and short, stout legs. Some saru were known to resemble orangutans. Saru, on average were between  and . Their entire bodies were covered in black or copper-red fur, leaving faces, palms, and soles of their feet bare. Saru had slight sexual dimorphism, with the females usually being slightly shorter than males.

Personality
Saru were kind, curious, and peaceful creatures that felt no animosity towards other sentient species of the jungle. They were friendly towards the korobokuru folk who, for some reason, felt strong antipathy to saru. The only creatures saru truly hated were the dreaded leopard katanga who prayed on and consumed saru children. They also felt a special kinship with animals, whom saru considered to be "persons." Saru delighted in communicating with animals of the Living Jungle and loved being responded to by those creatures who understood them, such as apes, monkeys, humans, and demihumans. This affection for all living things often led to saru approaching hippos, lions, sloths in search of a conversation, and just as often, sent them fleeing for dear life from predators less inclined to converse.

Most saru took on the roles of warriors, thieves, or combinations of thereof. On rare occasions, saru were known for taking religious roles of shamans, exclusively female occupation.

As a species, saru often displayed fright of magic, especially its visual displays, that could leave them paralyzed in fear.

Saru spoke their own complex langue that consisted of vocal sounds like grunts, hoots, and screams, as well as rich use of hand motions and facial expressions. They were capable of learning other languages, and many adventuring saru did. They also knew the ape language spoken by apes and monkeys, and well as monkey katanga.

Abilities
Because of saru's natural affinity to animals, they were treated as friends by domesticated and wild beasts alike, as if they were under the effects of the friends spell. This ability to commune with animals could be triggered once per day by all saru.

All saru were great climbers but could not swim.

Combat

In combat, saru relied on the use of weapons, learned from the Nubari and shu neighbors. They relied on such arms as clubs and spears of their own creation, but they were perfectly capable of wielding more complex weapons acquired from other communities of the jungle. However, saru were never truly disarmed as their great arms were powerful weapons in their own right. When entering unarmed combat, saru pummeled their opponents with both hands dealing significant damage in the process.

Society
Saru valued family above all else. Their clans often consisted of individuals related by blood. Mates were considered to be as close to a family as those of the same blood. Saru society did not know the concept of marriage, and saru couples were simply called mates. Saru clans did not hesitate to adopt strays of orphans into the community and the family. An average clan consisted of three or four adult male apes, four to six females, and up to four children. Saru reached adulthood at the age of 16, while females traditionally were caregivers for the young. Males typically were hunters and fighters. Some saru communities consisted of four or more clans forming villages, while other clans preferred solitude, only gathering together in times of strife or dire seasonal weather. Traditional saru communities did not have settlements. They lived nomadic lives, moving from one part of the Living Jungle to another. Saru villages were generally small, just several handfuls of simple huts of dried grass and packed mud.

It was a common assurance for saru youth to leave their homes in search of adventure and thrill.

Saru worshipped a demigoddess named Chee'ah, who walked the Malatran Plateau. Those females who received a call from the deity embarked on a pilgrimage to find her and take on the role of Chee'ah's "clerics."

Following their first contact with tribes of the Living Jungle, saru began adopting the cultural aspects of their new neighbors. From them, saru adopted the custom of wearing simple clothing like loincloths, belts to hold weapons, and rough-hewn jewelry. Saru were fascinated by other intelligent creatures and liked to imitate their activities.

Ecology

Saru were omnivores. Their diet consisted of vegetables, nuts, roots, insects, and small animals. Despite having a small amount of meat in their diet, saru equated eating flesh to cannibalism, the belief that made them bad dinner guests among other tribes of the Malatran Plateau.

History
Saru were believed to had evolved from the gorillas of the living jungle. However, the truth of the seeming accelerated evolutionary rate remained a mystery.

As of the 14 century DR, saru were still relatively new members of the Malatran tribes. Saru's first contact with the Nubari and shu happened only a century prior.

In the late 14 century DR, The Year of Discovery on the Malatran calendar, a small unnamed saru village on the shores of the Sleepy Lake fell ill with a wasting sickness. A vein earth spirit named Ligela cursed the saru after one of the village's youths broke a taboo and accidentally fell on a patch of sacred flowers. A group of heroes of the Living Jungle sent from the Tribe of Big Chief Bagoomba helped the village's survivors to appease the spirit, but the damage done was irreversible. The village was devastated, and the survivors had no other choice but to join another saru tribe.

Notable Saru
Ban-Nut and Choc-Choc, a duo of saru young orphaned after their mother was consumed by ravenous valley ants in The Year of Gathering Darkness.
Crash, a dim-witted saru adventurer and companion of equally intelligent adventurers named Rowr, and Budd, in the late 14 century DR.
Magilla, a saru shaman leader of the Sleepy Lake village circa The Year of Discovery.
Mokka, the chieftain on a non-nomadic and unnamed saru village, inspired by the stationary life of the Tribe of Big Chief Bagoomba circa The Year of Gathering Darkness.

## Sail at Sunset

The Sail at Sunset was a ramshackle dockside tavern in Tulmene of Tethyr, frequented by old salts and rowdy sailors in the late 14 century DR.

Interior
The taproom of the Sail at Sunset was quieter than most establishments of similar repute. The main feature of the taproom was a large painting hung over the bar, depicting a beautiful sunset and a storm-battered ship entering the harbor.

Atmosphere
The tavern was known for its servers—a surprisingly unattractive, warty bunch of farm lasses with tough bodies, more muscular than most warriors. These women were specifically hired for their looks by Kralbert Dightmaer, who ran the establishment in the late 14 century DR, to avoid harassment of his employees by rowdy sailors.

The tavern was seldomly cleaned, and when it was cleaned it was by pushing drunkards across the floors and out of the door.

Not surprisingly, bar fights were a regular occurrence, but they were always broken apart by a hose of saltwater pumped through from behind the tavern. The presence of three giant, muscled, thuggish bouncers armed with clubs and whips helped to persuade others against starting trouble. Smacking a troublemaker with a chair was another convincing argument.

No bard or minstrel performers ever showed up at the Sail at Sunset; however, hired table-dancers entertained the clientele after sundown.

Services
The Sail's beers were watered down, and the only food on the menu were biscuits, fried in butter. Occasionally, the tavern offered salted eels or squid when the proprietor was able to purchase a barrel-full for cheap. Each salted fish was sold as-is for two coppers "per corpse," as Kralbert Dightmaer referred to the grub.

Appendix
References

---

## Sasrelintar Myrtel

Sasrelintar Myrtel was the proprietor of Myrtel's Stargirt Wonders, the so-called "Shop of Wonders," in the Gem District of Athkatla in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Sasrelintar was an attractive dark-skinned and black-haired man of . His reserved manner of speech and delicate child-like features put people at ease. His body moved silently with elegance.

Personality
Sasrelintar enjoyed maintaining a certain mystique about himself. He claimed to have arrived in Amn from Lantan, despite having an uncharacteristic skin color for a Lantanna and lacking any interest in mechanical gadgets. His trade in magical miscellany was in high demand among Amnian lovers of all things showy and flamboyant.

When asked about his past or any of the numerous rumors about his allegiances, Sasrelintar answered with a polite smile.

Abilities
The shop's proprietor's true powers were unknown. He might as well have been nothing but a wizardly meddler with an interest in enchanted items.

Possessions
Sasrelintar's shop was a sizable building on Coront Street of the Gem District. People claimed that his fanciful rings were none other than rings of spell storing of undisclosed properties.

History
Despite his own claims of hailing from Lantan, rumors claimed that Sasrelintar Myrtel was once a toymaker from Calimport until causing someone's wrath and fleeing the country. Since he opened his Stargirt Wonders shop, along with his assistant Drestan, Sasrelintar had taken the lives of several thieves, assassins, and kidnappers who were reckless enough to target the man. He also was known or rumored to have some connections to prominent and wealthy Amnian families. Some thought him to be the infamous "small, dark, and silent man" who procured the rarest of all magical items, such as Halruaan skyships for his clients, while others claimed Sasrelintar Myrtel was on the Council of Six's payroll. More outlandish rumors aligned him with Shadow Thieves, some secretive wizardly organization, and even Red Wizards of Thay. The truth could be that he worked for all of these organizations.

Another well-known rumor was that Sasrelintar had dug out a cavern underneath the Shop of Wonders, where he tested the magics of his goods. The cavern initially was three cellars that became one after a magical explosion years before the late 14 century DR.

Appendix
References

## Satyrdance Pool

Satyrdance Pool was a pond in Myth Drannor, a possession of the Tornglara family, and a socializing spot for the matriarchs of several houses as of the mid-to-early 3 century DR.

Location
The pool was located in a private garden, behind House Tornglara.

Structure
Satyrdance was the "wannest" of several pools. It had a wooden path leading up to it. An oval mirror hung from a shadowtop tree close to the pool, and many branches were low enough to hang clothes from.

Activities
Every fourth day after highsunfeast, the ladies of several houses met there as of 241 DR. The pool was spiced with rosewater for such occasions, and refreshments were served.

History
In the, a meeting hosted by Alaglossa Tornglara, and attended by Ithrythra Mornmist, Duilya Evendusk, and Cilivren Doedance resulted in the four ladies agreeing they expected Elminster, a human, being given the honor of being named armathor would result in the eventual assassination of the Coronal Eltargrim Irithyl, with the ensuing succession fight causing extreme damage. In light of this eventuality, they made a vague resolution to try and prevent Cormanthor from coming apart in spite of the human being named an armathor.

Rumors & Legends
It was said that the Tornglara ladies used a mirror by the pool to scry into human cities for fashions they might want to copy.

## Sacred fist

Sacred fists were specialty priests devoted to Tyr, the Even-Handed.

Culture
The principles and techniques used by sacred fists were developed by a man named Hlam during years of living alone in a cave on Mount Waterdeep. A sacred fist combined the divine powers of a cleric with the martial prowess of a monk. Hlam organized his followers into the Order of the Even-Handed. Admittance into the order required a vow of devotion to Tyr and a vow of impartiality.

Activities
Most younger sacred fists desired to best a monk of Helm in ritual combat.

Abilities
In addition to the normal abilities of a cleric of Tyr, sacred fists excelled in unnarmed combat.

Possessions
As sacred fists practiced the art of unnarmed combat, they rarely carried weapons.

## Salyndra Shaern

Elder Reader Salyndra Shaern was a half-elf cleric of Oghma and of Corellon Larethian based at the House of Knowledge in Neverwinter in the late mid-to-late 14 century DR.

History
The House of Knowledge was already being run by Chief Priest Watger Brighthair but, due to his youth, Salyndra was installed by the Church of Oghma to guide him. They continued to lead the temple together through the 1360s DR to at least 1370 DR.

Activities
The two priests offered education for free, with teaching sessions doubling as services to Oghma the Binder.

As well as guiding Watger, Salyndra's role was also to promote good relations between humans and elves in Neverwinter and to study human wisdom for the elves. In all three areas, she did well.

Description
A petite old woman, Salyndra had hair that was gray, long, and untidy and spoke in a dry tone. She wore plain dark robes.

Personality
Although Salyndra had a cynical attitude and her advice was sharply incisive, at her core she was genuinely kind and a fast and loyal friend to travelers in need. Adventurers she'd aided often returned to the House of Knowledge to update her on their triumphs and to pay her back via donations to the temple.

## Sailor's Last Request

The Sailor's Last Request was a small chapel to Valkur located in Waterdeep during the 14 century DR.

Location
The temple was located in front on the docks, in an old and small warehouse building nested on the corner of Dock Street and Odd Street.

Atmosphere
The small chapel was long since missing a front door, and served as a refuge to impoverished sailors, dove and seagulls. The floor was full of bird droppings, and the whole place smelt of fish and brine.

The place didn't have dedicated clergy members watching over it—just an old retired sailor named Olroy acting as its guard. The altar was an old broken-down table in a corner, full of offerings left over time by worshipers such as seashells, locks of hair, and mementos of strong sentimental value.

The temple to Valkur was often a loud and busy place, used by seafarers as a place to socialize and find new crew members. The deity didn't mind as it valued strength and actions over prayers.

History
Before being a temple, the place was a small warehouse used to store salted fish.

## Sabrast Windriver

Sabrast Windriver was a Cormyrean noble in the mid-14 century DR.

Personality
He was quite acerbic and cynical.

Possessions
His holdings were comically extensive as of, including a villa on Turnhelm Street, stables on Sarangar Lane, a city manor on Ambel Row, business offices on Waervar Street, a hideaway of sorts on Westchapel Way, cottages on Brightstar Street, Waymoot and Undelmring Street, a country state on Gray Oaks, and a hunting lodge in Mouth o' Gargoyles. He owed 36,000 golden lions in back taxes as of that year solely in land taxes for those properties, but also owned around two score other houses and about a hundred farms upstate.

He also owned a number of ships, including a yacht which, at the time, was moored on Moonever. Per the port rolls of Suzail, his ships had departed from the port sixteen times but arrived at the port twenty times as of that season, implying he had at least five ships. However, at least two different ships used the same charter and name.

He personally attested, as well, to more properties, such as the Olde Lace and Glitterswash chain of shops in Sembia, as well as four more mistresses, implying he had each of them in yet another property.

Relationships
He did not send his family any money, including his wife, his daughter, and his three sons, Falorian Windriver, Arastor Windriver, and Bralzaer Windriver. Elminster Aumar owed him for some great services, and Andrast knew how to see through his disguises.

History
During a revel in Ches 14 of, Sabrast Windriver was joined by Andemel Graeven and Raurild Sarpath. After they discussed some of Andemel's schemes and joked with Raurild over his wife, he was accosted by the precept Immult Murauvyn, who was looking to extract back taxes from the man, and brought both Vangerdahast and several Purple Dragons to bring him for forced labor if he continued to withhold payment. He was bailed out of the matter by Elminster, who teleported in a wealth of money for him, and warned that he would release three or four small dragons if his intercession was rejected.

Legacy
Mystra later counterfeited Sabrast's own memories of the event and diverted them to Elminster's mind, until in the, they were misappropriated by the archdevil Nergal.

## Sarlin

Sarlin, also known as Sarlin the Supreme, was a malevolent wizard in the mid-14 century DR.

Personality
Sarlin knew to keep to an agreement in the face of temptation. He was, nonetheless, an evil sorcerer.

Possessions
Sarlin's hut was at most three days away from Elminster's Tower. In, he acquired the Ring of Murbrand.As Tarth Hornwood asks for a few days, he takes at most a tenday. Since four days are needed to duplicate the staff, the trips to and from Elminster's tower may have taken at most three days each.

History
In late Mirtul of, Sarlin had fallen on hard times when he was visited by Tarth Hornwood, on whom he had played some trick at a prior time. He agreed to create a false duplicate of Tarth Hornwood's staff in exchange for the Ring of Murbrand, a task he believed would take him four days. It was completed successfully, and he acquired the ring.

She trusted Vangerdahast thoroughly, and was in turn trusted by him.

## Sasha Flint

Sasha Flint was a dwarven resident of Phandalin in the late 15 century DR.

She was friends with fellow teenagers Alana Hallwinter, Nedd Angelite, and Grace Hardstone.

History
When she was a teenager in the 1490s DR, she and two dwarven friends—Nedd and Grace—became skeptical of some of the tall tales written by Volothamp Geddarm in the newly-published Volo's Guide to Monsters. The three teens decided to travel to the Crooked Forest to investigate, where they captured by gnolls. Soon, their friend Alana arrived in the woods to search for them, but was captured as well. The harrowing ordeal left Sasha and her friends in shock and barely able to speak.

## Salusk

Salusk was a species of human head-sized melons that grew on creeping spindly vines all over Faerûn.

Description
The fruit was oblong in shape with two bumps or "horns" on its opposite sides. Salusk fruit's sweet and succulent flesh was beige and tasted reminiscent of a pear when it reached ripeness. As they matured, their skin turned from pale to emerald green. Unripe salusk had thick and oily skin with a tough and bitter-acidic taste. If left unharvested, the fruit eventually turned brown, oversoft, and sour. Its skin turned maroon and ripped, attracting swarms of hungry wasps and hornets.

Usages
Unripened salusk could be cooked in oil. The fruit's flesh absorbed the flavor of anything it was fried in, often making it a substitute or addition to meat products if fried in animal fat. The fruit's tough leathery rind was rich in fiber and edible, yet held no flavor.

Availability
Salusk grew abundantly throughout Faerûn, reaching as far south as Calimshan. Cultivated salusk was planted along long fences the vines could cling to and it was fertilized with cattle dung. Because of the fruit's tough rind and resistance to bruising, salusk was easy to transport, keep, and trade. Even unrefrigerated and exposed to sun, salusk fruit could keep for two tendays.

Appendix
References

## Salt

Salt was a substance that was widely produced and used in the Realms for a variety of culinary, industrial, alchemical, and magical purposes.

Description
Salt was a naturally occurring mineral that formed translucent white crystals. When mined from the ground, it was called rock salt, also known as halite.

It was not to be confused with other substances referred to as salts, such as saltpeter, smelling salts, natron, alum, potash, sal ammoniac, bromine salt, or alkaline salt that were often inadvisably edible and were more readily applied in laboratories or other non-culinary situations.

Availability

Salt was a common trade good on many routes, with most large caravans containing at least one dedicated salt wagon. It was rarely the mainstay of entire economies, however, as it was broadly naturally abundant, most regions having at least some local production. Its value as a spice and a preservative was also somewhat lessened by the wide selection of other methods available in Faerûn and beyond. This included preservative substances such as maxoris, seal berries, halamda, and nitre, the spell preservation, or careful use of brown mold. Other flavor-enhancers including the wide array of spices in the Realms and spells such as flavor or prestidigitation.

Purveyors
Many people and organizations used the harvesting, trading, and application of salt as a source of income:
 The Saltbaron clan of Duergar in Gracklstugh practised salt mining as their main trade.
 Gnomish families often made a living by mining small salt deposits.
 The Salters' and Packers' Guild of Port Kir in Tethyr packed salted fish for inland trade.
 The Fellowship of Salters, Packers, and Joiners in Waterdeep preserved food for oceanic trade, including between Faerûn and Maztica.
 Aurora of Aurora's Emporium sold salt for in bulk for 1 sp per pound, in 1 lb blocks or 5 lb of granules, or smaller seasoning packets for 1 cp.
 The markets of Zakhara would typically sell salt at a price of 1 sp per pound.
 The island of Tharsult was a center of trade for spices and salts.

Locations

Salt could be obtained from salt marshes, the ocean, or other bodies of salty water by evaporating the water. A common method was pouring the water across a flat surface and letting the water evaporate. It was harvested from a wide variety of sources in the Realms:
 The Flooded Forest between the Vast and Moonsea regions.
 The Adder Swamp in Chessenta was difficult to access, but gave Chessenta a sizable salt export.
 The Spider Swamp in Calimshan.
 The Great Swamp of Rethild in the Shining South.
 A tributary of the Deeping Stream in Featherdale infused with mineral salt was harvested by boiling the water.
 The water from bogs beneath the salt pans of the the Sword could be harvested for salt, though it was difficult and risky.
 The lakes fed by the Anai River in the Plain of Horses were harvested for salt, especially Lake Gusang, with each lakebed's minerals producing a different color.
 The Dwyvach Salt Flats in the Moonshae Isles.
 The Moonsea provided a unique salt.

Salt could also be found as naturally forming chunks on the shores of extremely salty bodies of water.
 Azulduth, the Lake of Salt, near Mulhorand.
 The Lake of Steam in southern Faerûn.
 Lake Lhespen in the Shaar had several communities that harvested from it.
 Volcanic hot springs could produce special kinds of salt.

Salt pans on the site of dried-up lakes and inland seas were also a source of salt:
 The Anauroch desert had many salt pans that were mined by the Bedine, especially in the Sword region. The Shoal of Thirst was an especially massive salt pan, formed from the basin of Hidden Lake.
 The Raurin desert.
 The Lake of Salt contracted in summer, revealing expansive salt flats. They were mined by locals from the towns of Azun and Buldamar.
 A kavir was a salt flat in Zakhara which was actually a crust of salt over thick black mud, making a dangerous hazard for travelers. Similar formations existed in Anauroch covering saltwater bogs.

Exploratory salt mines were major producers of rock salt. Underground deposits could be located by the presence of a salty spring, as groundwater combined with the salt vein.
 The mountains around Amn, including around Keczulla.
 The mountains of Calimshan.
 The mountains of Chult.
 The Korinn Archipelago in the Moonshae Isles.
 The northern islands of Mintarn in the Sea of Swords.
 The Orsraun Mountains.
 The Whiteshell Mine near Blingdenstone in the Underdark.
 The mountains of Samarach in the Chultan Peninsula, including Torich's Mines of Selgol.
 The Serpent Hills in the Western Heartlands. Below the head of the Great Petrified Serpent, ancient gnomes dug salt mines.
 The Valley of Umbricci near Colletro in the Blade Kingdoms.
 The valleys near Marrauk in Laerakond produced very pure, very fine salt.
 Small veins of salt formed in rock crevices in the Sword of Anauroch.
 The arid plains of Ma' Yuan and Ch'ing Tung in Shou Lung provided great amounts of salt for the empire.

Other large deposits of salt could be found elsewhere in the Realms, but were not necessarily accessible:
 The Underdark contained many large deposits of salt. These included the Buried Realms near Tethyamar, the caves of Deep Raurin, the Darklands near Traaskl Thorog, and the deposits near Lake Drooud that were searingly-hot thanks to nearby underground volcanism.
 Lake Luo was surrounded by salt flats.
 The Sea of Salt near Moradask in Zakhara was a large salt pan, but was too inhospitable to harvest.
 The alkaline lake in the Pit of the Ghuls in Zakhara was an untapped source of many types of salt thanks to its remote location and extreme danger.
 The T'hai Salt Flats in Wai were a salt lake that drained away, but were inaccessible thanks to the necromancer Utwa So.

Usage
Food

 Salt was often used to preserve meat such as pork and fish including cod, salmon, and herring (turning them into salted fish), prolonging their shelf life. Wemics favored salt to preserve their foods.
 Salt was a primary ingredient in the creation of certain condiments and sauces such as mustard, garum, and soya sauce.
 Salt was a common seasoning in countless dishes, ranging from subtle to overwhelming. Some salty meals and snacks such as salted nuts were served in taverns in order to make patrons thirstier, or served alongside dishes such as farmer's cheese and caprese to enhance the taste.
 Salt was sometimes applied to food via a small device called a salt shaker, or stored in another called a salt cellar.
 Sauerkraut was made by fermenting cabbage in salt.
 The Bedine salted their coffee.
 Colored salts were a part of Chultan cuisine, and salt was used in recipes such as kinche and kita.
 The people of the Plain of Horses, harvesting around the Anai River, favored white salt for the upper classes, green for the rest, and red salt for animals.
 Dwarves were fond of eating heavily salted food that others found unpalatable, and often carried chunks of rock salt to lick. They would even lick salt residue from the walls of natural caverns they traveled through.
 Gnomes generally over-salted their food by other's standards, and ate rock salt like others would eat candy. Deep gnomes rarely cooked, instead preserving their food with salt to the extent it was unpalatable to others.
 Greater sea hags derived much of their sustenance from sea salt and consequently spent much of their time grinding salt through the use of magical rock-crushing devices.
 The Tuigans had their tea with salt and milk.
 Qilué Veladorn was fond of eating glow worms dipped in rock salt.
 In the sage Elminster's opinion, only barbarians salted their butter.

Other
 Salt was used in the production of alchemical potions, especially explosive ones.
 Salt was involved in processes to refine and purify gold ore.
 Saltsteel was a form of magically-hardened salt which could then be used in crafting.
 Salt was a key ingredient in some medicinal balms.
 The priests of Loviatar rubbed salt in their wounds to accentuate the pain.
 Arable land was sometimes salted to poison the land for generations, such as in Sessrendale. However, this was considered a grievous affront to the harvest goddess Chauntea.
 Bamsmacks were filled with common salt.
 The salt bond was a Zakharan bond of hospitality formed by the consumption of salt from a host's table.
 Marids used exotic salts to flavor the water they breathed.

Spell Components
Salt was used as a material component in many spells:
 Agitate wounds used a pinch of salt.
 Clone could be performed using a vessel filled with salt water to contain the grown body.
 Comprehend languages used a pinch of salt and soot.
 Gentle repose used a pinch of salt along with two copper coins placed on the corpse's eyes.
 Melt used a pinch of salt along with a pinch of soot.
 Metal to rust used a vial of salt water.
 Obar's lesser purification used a pinch of salt along with a pinch of limestone dust and three drops of potion of sweet water.
 Preservation used a pinch of salt.
 Proof from teleportation could use 10 lb. of salt stuffed into a 5-lb. sack.
 Protection from the elements could use salt to protect against drowning and depth pressure.
 Spellweb could use a grain of salt.

Bedine magical traditions used salt or another elemental aspect of the desert as a component. This included At'ar's fire, cleanse water, conjure sand lion, create water, death smoke, find water, fist of sand, flesh mirage, flying jambiya, life water, pillar of sand, sand gems, sand healing, sand jambiya, sand shadow, sand shroud, sand whisper, sand worm, sandswallow, sun stone, whispering sand, wind blade, wind compass, and wind shadow.

Creatures

 Salt was a vital part of most creatures' biology, with too much or too little causing illness and death.
 Sea-native aquatic beings such as sea elves and sahuagin usually required salt water to remain healthy.
 Nishruu were heavily damaged by contact with even a handful of salt.
 Salt dragons lived near the sea or saltwater lakes. They breathed a cone of salt crystals, potentially blinding the target or incapacitating them with pain.
 Salt mephits were minor elementals made of salt.
 Salt mummies were created when a person who led a horror-filled life was buried near veins of salt, often becoming a threat to miners.
 Snails, slugs, and scum creepers were highly vulnerable to salt.

Magic
 A wand of salt could be used to induce pain and dehydration on a target, or salt a large area of soil.
 The salt cantrip could create a small amount of fine salt.
 Purify food and drink and purify water could be used to remove the salt from water, making it potable.
 A decanter of endless water or alchemy jug could be used to make salt water.

Other
 The Green Sword River was extremely salty, but was purified of salt at Sekras by an mysterious device operated by the priesthood of Thoth.
 Crystal flowers were highly-prized natural halite or gypsum formations from the Underdark that resembled flowers.
 The wastelands of Avernus in the Nine Hells were dotted with salt flats made from the tears of the damned.
 The Quasi-Elemental Plane of Salt was an entire plane made of salt, a rare instance of too much salt.
 Hamdh was a desert-growing shrub in Zakhara which was extremely rich in salt, making it ideal for camel grazing.
 In T'u Lung tradition, salt was one of the five tastes.
 Salt was used colloquially to refer to old sailors ("old salts").

## Safe Haven

The Safe Haven was the inn of Darkenshield, a Cormyrean village in the Stonelands. It was destroyed along with the rest of the village in 1352 DR.

Structure
The inn was originally two stories, though by 1369 DR the upper floor had collapsed in on itself. The nearby barn also remained mostly intact, though both structures were barely habitable. The internal walls of the inn were also largely destroyed, as the resident ogres desired more open living space.

History
In the, Darkenshield was attacked by one of Gondegal's units, the Twilight Brigade. The Safe Haven was not spared, and during the raid the twelve-year-old stableboy died trying to protect the horses stabled in the barn.

By 1369 DR, the ruins were inhabited by a gang of eight ogres who found it a convenient camping spot. They redecorated the interior and defended the area from invaders, killing and eating one of Lord Vorik Aris's agents when he attempted to scout out the ruins. They had amassed a small hoard of treasure in their time, including thousands of coins, a ring of water elemental command, a Murlynd's spoon, a periapt of health, and a Quaal's feather token.

## Sand

Sand was a substance composed of small grains of finely ground up stone and other minerals.

Natural Occurrence

Vast amounts of sand could be found in the natural world, such as the deserts of Anauroch, Zakhara, and Raurin.

Wind could drive piles of sand into piles or hills called dunes, which could cover hundreds of square feet. The strongest of winds could blow dunes large distances in only several days. Dunes always had a steeper slope on the leeward side, where collapses were common. On occasion, a change of wind mid-formation caused a blowout, a hidden hollow cavity that collapsed under weight and sucked in people and animals. A sand crust could form from mud or salt residue, creating a thin, hard surface over a patch of deep, soft sand.

Sandstorms were harmful to most creatures, as the driving particles would reduce visibility and chafe skin. Powerful enough storms could bury buildings, scour the flesh, blind the eyes, and choke the lungs. The shifting build-up of sand could even bury creatures alive, crushing and suffocating them, especially when an unstable dune collapsed in a sandslide (or was moved en-masse by a spell).

Devil dunes were massive, malevolent forms of sand that resented trespassers, animated by magical power.

Quicksand was an area of treacherous ground, usually found in marshes or deserts. In marshes it was a waterlogged, near-liquid soil or sand, while in deserts it was formed from a fine, powdery sand that flowed like water. It was very dangerous to unprepared explorers, who could easily drown in it.

Availability
 Fine sand could be bought from Aurora's Emporium for 1 sp per 10 lb.
 Fine sand was harvested from near Kirinwood for the glassmaking industry there.

Usages

 Silex was a fine, pure white sand, and was widely used for all manner of industrial purposes, including the production of potions, glass and enamel.
 Titania was a white sand used to make brilliant white enamels, but had to be processed from ilmenite.
 Sand was mixed with lime to make mortar, used in construction.
 Exact measures of sand was used to fill hourglasses and minuteglasses for timekeeping.
 Hardsand was a dark, metallic sand derived from adamant, used to surface harden metal for masterwork projects, or manufacture extremely strong putty.
 Sand could bear high concentrations of metals, such as copper, gold, iron, silver, electrum, quicksilver, and even adamant or mithral. This sand could be used to make specialized potions, dyes, and inks, or processed in large quantities to extract the metals in bulk.
 Sand was a potential material for golem construction, in particular the sand golem.
 Sand was one of many materials used by the Watchful Order of Magists and Protectors to fight fires in Waterdeep.
 Zakharan artifice led to the creation of magical sands used for various tasks. These included crystallizing, thirsty, caging, rasping, mirage, obscuring, sinking, restoring, fiery, slowing, solidifying, and scintillating varieties. A similar but unrelated substance used in Zakhara was sand of truth.

Spell Components
Sand was used as a material component in many spells, many of which required no more than a pinch or handful of sand, though some demanded more complicated reagents. These included blast of sand, cleanse water, color spray, conjure sand lion, cool strength, dispel mirage, dune, dust curtain, flowstone, Leomund's secure shelter, maskstone, mirage wall, move earth, move dune, move sand, persistent image, river of sand, sandcone, sand form, sand jambiya, sand quiet, sand slumber, sand sword, sand shadow, sandstorm, sleep, statue, timewarp, traceless travel, transmute rock, and watchware.

Bedine magical traditions used sand or another elemental aspect of the desert as a component, and was known as "wind and sand magic". This included At'ar's fire, cleanse water, conjure sand lion, create water, death smoke, find water, fist of sand, flesh mirage, flying jambiya, life water, pillar of sand, sand gems, sand healing, sand jambiya, sand shadow, sand shroud, sand whisper, sand worm, sandswallow, sun stone, whispering sand, wind blade, wind compass, and wind shadow.

Creatures

 Blue, brass, yellow, sand, and brown dragons preferred to make their homes in sandy deserts and wastelands, and were adept at burrowing through the material.
 Sandlings and sandmen were elementals made of sand. Other earth elementals could be made of sand, but were somewhat weaker than those of soil or clay.
 Skriaxit were air elementals also called living sandstorms, and used sandstorms to tear apart their foes.
 Creatures such as sand fleas, sand cats, sand giants and Sand were not actually made of sand, nor was sand stew, fortunately.

Magic
In the Zakharan view of magic, many spells related to the element of earth were grouped into the province of sand. Elemental mages who specialized in sand were called sand mages.

Many spells existed that manipulated or affected sand in some way, especially from Bedine and Zakharan traditions.
 Awaken sand gave an amount of sand animus and intelligence.
 Black sand infused an area of sand with shadowstuff and negative energy.
 Blast of sand sprayed a lethal blast of sand.
 Choking sands filled a target's lungs with sand.
 Control sand raised or lowered an area of sand.
 Desert fist animated an area of sand into a crushing fist.
 Dust curtain could create a small sandstorm.
 Hissing sand caused sand to flow as directed.
 Move dune moved a huge volume of sand.
 Move sand moved a large volume of sand.
 Part sand could form a passage through a mass of sand.
 Pillar of sand raised a pillar of sand up.
 Protection from normal missiles also extended protection against the effects of being caught in a sandstorm.
 River of sand created a destructive river of sand.
 Sandcone excavated a conical area of sand.
 Sand form created a sandy simulacrum of the target.
 Sand shroud buried an object or non-living creature in sand.
 Sandswallow caused a large area of sand or other soft ground to collapse inward, swallowing foes.
 Soften earth and stone could be used to turn dirt into sand.
 Traceless travel allowed the caster and their mount to pass across sand without leaving tracks.
 Unleash monolith could summon a huge, rampaging, sandy elemental.
 Wall of sand created a large, temporary wall of sand.
 Whispering sand imbued sand with the ability to store and relay a message or trigger a verbal spell.

Other
 Memnon's presence in the Calim Desert caused the ground to break apart into fine sand, spontaneously generating dry quicksand.
 The Lands of Intrigue were also called the "Empires of the Sands", but this was a misnomer, as only Calimshan had deserts and neither Amn nor Tethyr had any deserts to speak of.
 The Elemental Plane of Earth contained a great deal of sand and other similar materials.

## Saprophytic plague

The saprophytic plague was a supernatural, fungal disease that plagued the Sword Coast in 1492 DR.

Acquisition
It was spread by means of the spores that grew off of an infected individual. Anyone within  of that person or creature had a chance of being infected as well, though hardier individuals had a higher chance of resisting infection.

Any foodstuffs exposed to saprophytic spores would spoil within a matter of hours, and also transform into ooze.

Effects
The symptoms of the saprophytic plague came on in stages. At first, an individual would present a fever, and oozing lesions infested with fungus. Over time, they lost their individuality, and became mindless, zombie-like creatures. Eventually, their body dissolved into an intelligent, magenta-colored ooze. During the entirety of the infection, the victim released more spores that would infect other animals and sentient humanoids.

Interestingly enough, fungi and fungal creatures were entirely immune to the disease.

Cure
There was no known means to cure individuals afflicted with the plague, even by means of powerful magic like the wish spell.  Foodstuffs however could be cleansed by means of purifying spells.

The only cure of the saprophytic plague was the destruction of its creator, the lichen lich Xanthoria.

History
In the, a group of adventurers arrived at the library-fortress of Candlekeep, investigating the nature of the saprophytic plague. Folks in towns across the Sword Coast were succumbing to the plague, herds livestock fell, and tons of foodstuffs went to complete waste. Fortunately for the people of the region, those adventurers managed to delve into the lair of Xanthoria in the Lykortha Expanse, defeated the lichen lich, and destroyed its phylactery.

## Sarsson

Sarsson was a type of plant native to wet tropical regions and warm volcanic subterranean areas.

Description
Sarsson was a common but little-known herb. It grew in fine carpets close to the ground, and its leaves were broad, short, and yellow. It was most commonly found in hot jungles, tropical riverbanks, warm bogs, and underground, nearby hot volcanic vents underground. It had a distinct peppery-lemony taste.

Usage
The strong taste of sarsson could be used to make foul water better tasting. However, it only affected its palatability but not food safety. As a seasoning, sarsson was used to complement meats. Certain species of carrion birds and related creatures, like giant striders and gorcraws found sarsson irresistible when mixed with fresh blood as it brought forth a vivid, strong taste beloved by them. because of that, sarsson was used in fire newt society as part of execution for murder. Fire newts found guilty were tortured for three days, their body parts removed, and treated with sarsson. The guilty individual was then fed alive to their own mount.

Sandyrina the Sensual was a dancer and fence in Tilverton in the 14 century DR.

Sandyrina was beautiful and often went scantily dressed as part of her work as a dancer.

Personality
She was a fair dealer and kept her client's secrets well.

Relationships
She was protected by the pseudodragons Tannath and Slip, who she found abandoned as babies and raised herself, gaining devotedly loyal companions.

Activities
Around 1358 DR, Sandyrina was a dancer at the Winking Moon tavern, but her true vocation was as a fence. She pretended to be a prostitute and met traveling merchants and adventurers in their inn lodgings, taking stolen and dangerous goods off their hands.

## Sapphire dragonfly

A sapphire dragonfly was a large, blue dragonfly that could be found in northwest Faerûn, including Icewind Dale, the Sword Coast North, and the Savage Frontier.

History
In the late 15 century DR, a sapphire dragonfly found its way into the home of Edgin and Zia Darvis in Targos. While Edgin moved to kill or capture the insect, Zia opened a window to "let it go". After Zia's death at the hands of a Red Wizard, a sapphire dragonfly appeared above her pyre, and henceforth the insect would remind Edgin of his lost wife. Eleven years later, the sight of such a dragonfly would ultimately help him to move on from his grief when he realized that he had to let his wife go just as she had once let the dragonfly go.

Appendix
Background
According to The Art and Making of Dungeons & Dragons: Honor Among Thieves, the sapphire dragonfly was the only creature in the Honor Among Thieves film that was not taken from existing Dungeons & Dragons sources. The sapphire dragonfly was used to represent the concept of letting go of the past. In the Realms, it is possible this is a deity's manifestation such as a favored animal.

## Safiya's robes

Safiya's robes were bright red, proudly announcing her affiliation with the Red Wizards of Thay. The garb had +3 protection enchantment weaved into it, as well as magics that provided improved universal protections from magical and physical effects and hazards. Safiya's robes also dramatically improved the wearer's skills to concentrate. Because of its enchantments, the robe could only be worn by human Red Wizards and none other.

Safiya appeared wearing her magic Red Wizard robes in the, when she found the western hero Kalach-Cha deep within The Bear God's Barrow in Rashemen. She continued wearing the robes while traveling with the new companion, however, Safiya insisted on changing out of the red garb when entering Mulsantir and other Rashemi settlements due to the tense relationship between the Red Wizard and the inhabitants of the land of witches and berserkers.

## Sailor's Crossing

Sailor's Crossing was a harborside cobblestone plaza decorated with a fountain at its center, located in the city of Baldur's Gate.

Location
The plaza stood on the crossing of several streets at the edge of Eastway, a single city block northwest from the Low Lantern and south of Fisher's Warf. The street called Admiral's Way began on the next block to the southwest.

The plaza smelled of salty sea breeze and spayed water mist from its tiered fountain with several copper coins in its waters.

History

In the summer of the, on Kythorn 19, the Sailor's Crossing plaza became a scene of a grizzly murder. A dead elf minstrel wearing a glued-on beard was found dead at the fountain's base. A large anchor was embedded in the man's chest. The adventurer and detective named Tav begin the investigation of the murder on Kythorn 20, later returning to the crime scene on Kythorn 22. By that time, the body had been removed by the Flaming Fists and sent to the Harborside Hospital with only a couple of poleaxe-armed guards keeping an eye on the site.

## Sashelan blessing

Sashelan blessing was a spell used by elvess of Myth Drannor and Semberholme.

Effects
When cast on a targeted creature, they entered into a state of deep sleep. The spell granted the targeted creature the ability to breathe underwater, endure the pressures of the depths, and remain dry, along with any possessions held at the time of casting, until the spell expired.

The spell left a subtle coral-pink radiance upon the targeted creature, visible only within the upper  of water. Once submerged beyond that threshold, the color shifted, renderering the targeted creature invisible, detectable only through magic-detection spells.

Sashelan blessing could not be cast on individuals who already possessed active water-breathing magic or an innate ability within them. It was also unable to be cast on large creatures.

Similar to sleep, the targeted creature's slumber could be interrupted by damage or loud noises. However, the ambient hum of the deep sea, the constant background noise of the ocean and its inhabitants, or non-damaging interactions, such as those with dolphins or similar creatures, were insufficient to break the spell. If the targeted creature was awakened, the water-breathing ability remained in effect for a very short time.

Use
In an ideal scenario, people would be aware of the spell's purpose and willingly submit to its effects, knowing that it spared them the potentially harrowing descent through the depths to the haven of Myth Nantar. Nowadays, the few elves and humans who were acquainted with the spell consider it a rescue enchantment for sailors who have fallen overboard, allowing them to endure until they ccould be brought ashore. Many sailors in the Inner Sea owed their lives to this spell, yet the slumber it induced often fosters skepticism among them regarding the existence of tritons, merfolk, or sea elves in significant numbers.

Sashelan blessing was used by the Shipwreck and Salvagers' Guild, headquartered in Marsember. Access to this spell was contingent upon guild membership and payment of dues, amounting to five percent of all salvaged treasures or a minimum of fifty gp per year. Unbeknownst to guild members, the guild's founder also shared this spell with various allies. Therefore, with some inquiries and a generous sum of gold, spellcasters across the Dragon Coast, Cormyr, Sembia, the Vast, and Impiltur could be found who are capable of casting this spell or teaching it in exchange for a significantly higher fee.

History
Originating during the days of Aryselmalyr and the ascendance of Cormanthyr, this spell was crafted with the purpose of fostering better relations between surface-dwelling elves and the sea elves of Serôs. The spell enabled sea elves to immobilize struggling individuals at risk of drowning and granted them the means to survive the journey to safety, be it the shore or other sanctuaries.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required material components.

## Sarturl's Study of Magical Fields

Sarturl's Study of Magical Fields was an academic magic textbook written by the Netherese arcanist Sarturl.

Contents
The book contained meticulously complete examinations and comparisons of magical effects that took up a physical volume of space. It was exhaustively detailed and precise, but Sarturl did not craft engaging prose and the book was terrifically dry to read. As an example of the depth of knowledge that Sarturl provided, an entire page was devoted to describing the smell of an active antimagic field.

History
Sarturl's Study of Magical Fields was considered a classic and foundational piece of academia by the arcanists of Netheril during the Age of Discovery ( onwards). Any respectable arcane library would be expected to contain a copy of it, along with other works such as Ivrynxra's Exploration of Enchantments, Haerro's Veltanabula, Oloam's on Serpentine Matters, and Skouloond's Spell Studies.

## Sarkoarvhan Dhurr

Sarkoarvhan Dhurr was a clerk and author who lived in Tethyr in the 14 century DR.

Personality
Sarkoarvhan was a mild-mannered and shy man.

History
Sarkoarvhan worked as a clerk in Tethyr, enduring the treatment of cruel and pompous masters and clients. Unable to contain himself any longer, he poured his feelings and experiences into writing the novel Arauntril: Lady Death, Lady Flame. The biting and lurid prose proved extremely popular across the Heartlands, leading to an anonymous author creating the even more extreme sequel Arauntril Scourged. Although he was initially greatly upset by the imitation, once Dhurr had the chance to read it he found it quite delightful.

## Sarevok's horned helmet

Sarevok's horned helmet was the unique helm worn by the infamous Bhaalspawn Sarevok Anchev.

Description
It resembled the skull of some fiendish creature, the maw of which opened up to reveal the face of the wearer.

Powers
The helm granted the wearer several boons. It bolstered their physical fortitude and protected them from all manner of spells or effects that attempted to manipulate their emotions, most notably fear or terror.

It also granted the wearer the 'Deathbringer's sight'. This power both allowed them to see in darkness from  away, and also allowed them to land more vicious and deadly blows in battle.

History
Sarevok wore his helm in his fateful battle with his half-brother Abdel Adrian in the Bhaalist temple beneath Baldur's Gate. That battle became famous among the followers of Sarevok's divine father Bhaal.

After Sarevok's death, he was resurrected by his brother and compelled to play a role in Abdel's confrontation with other mortal Bhaalspawn. At some point during the following century, Sarevok came into possession of his horned helm once again, and wore it as he presided over the Murder Tribunal in Bhaal's temple in the Undercity of Baldur's Gate.

In Marpenoth of the, the Leyland–Nightstalker wedding took place. Dame Jenilynn Leyland, a Knight of the Right Hand of Tyr, and Sir Sarren Nightstalker, a Knight of the Griffon, were united in a social gala that was reported in the pages of the Ravens Bluff Trumpeter. Samovars were vessels filled with boiling water intended for serving tea. They were important in Rashemaar culture, where they were used in tea ceremonies.

A samovar appeared as an urn-shaped vessel with a spigot. A tube of burning charcoal kept water stored within at boiling temperature.

While typically crafted out of metals such as tin, some samovars were instead made of porcelain.

Some of these vessels were crafted to accommodate a separate teapot on top, which would be filled with particularly strong tea meant to be diluted with the samovar's boiling water. In Zakhara, samovars of this kind were sometimes also used to serve coffee.

## Sabran

Sabran was a Zhentarim priest of Bane in the mid-14 century DR.

Personality
Sabran could find humor in Zhentarim hierarchy and its workings. He got quite bitter at nosy people.

Relationships
He bickered much with Mhegras during their assignment to the capture of Shandril Shessair. Drauthtar Inskirl and Eirhaun Sooundaeril both saw him as a rival worth slaying. Hlael Toraunt and Korthauvar Hammantle counted him as the most formidable Zhent in the caravan.

Abilities
One of his spells allowed him to cheat death in exchange for a live sacrifice. He was likely the most formidable Zhentarim in the caravan.

Possessions
Some of the cargo he concealed included horse barding.

History
Sabran joined the caravan from Scornubel to Waterdeep in which Shandril Shessair sought passage on late . He presented himself as a weaver instead of a priest, and concealed some horse armor as contraband, despite the fact he was undercover. He remained undercover through the ambush at Haelhollow, not taking part in the battle.

In the aftermath of the fight, they tried to capture Shandril while she was out of spellfire and knocked out, guarded only by Narm Tamaraith, but decided against it when she woke up too soon. However, they were then ambushed by the Harpers Beldimarr and Arauntar, who were posing as caravan guards, and got their necks broken. This left them unseen to the Zhentarim scriers Hlael Toraunt and Korthauvar Hammantle. for much of a day. Unknown to the Harpers, he survived thanks to a contingent spell.

He was slain during the caravan's next ambush, by Marlel.

## Samril's Fine Blades

Samril's Fine Blades was a weaponcrafting workshop in Secomber during the 15 century DR.

Services
Samril's Fine Blades was owned and operated by the Samril halfling family, and performed a wide range of tasks related to the crafting and assembly of weapons. It was linked by merchants' map to Arthane Furnace in Beliard who supplied raw blades, then sent them on to the Arthane Armory in Waterdeep for sale.

At Samril's, naked blades arrived and were sharpened with House of Yarthin whetstones and polished with mineral powders from Anastur's Abrasives. Duskwood and blueleaf wood was obtained from Maraszymo Darluth and other local woodcutters, dried in kilns by the Samrils, and then carved and fitted as weapon grips. Leather for wrapping the grips was sourced from Falthryn and Daughters Tannery and Sorkryn's Soft Hides of Scornubel, usually ox-hide or rothé-hide, with horse-hide and donkey-hide used if nothing else was available. The leather grips were secured with braided copper wire from Gleskur's Finemetals of Secomber. The pommels of their swords were usually round knobs affixed to the end of the tang, variously ivory, bone, or brass. Ivory was sourced from trustworthy passing merchants as available or special-ordered from Waterdeep, while a regular supply of hard-wearing peryton, owlbear, dire wolf, and fire lizard bone was obtained from Galdegut's Boneyard. Brass and other alloys used in the construction was supplied by local bulk merchants and worked on-site by the Samrils, while ivory and bone was carved into shape by young Samrils overseen by their elders. The finished blades were coated in vine oil, wrapped in canvas from local suppliers, and sealed in gum that the Samrils made from local lalannas vine and bitterthorn roots. They were then packed in crates and sealed with pitch before being sent to Waterdeep and the Arthane Armory.

## Sarafin

Sarafin was a roaming adventurer who traveled the Sword Coast North during the late 15 century DR.

Description
Sarafin was a slender High elf woman with olive skin, short brown hair, and grey eyes. Her adventuring garb consisted of handsome light-colored cloth armor and simple leather pauldrons.

History
In the, Sarafin was a member of an adventuring band that included the gnome Yonks, the half-orc Zog, and the tiefling Quinara. The group traveled the Sword Coast in search of steady adventuring work. They traveled from Waterdeep to Baldur's Gate during the Absolute crisis and, unable to find work, planned to move out to Neverwinter as the city was searching for adventures. During her stay in Baldur's Gate, Sarafin took a swim in the River Chionthar but emerged dirtier than before the plunge. The river was polluted at the time by Lord Gortash's machinery.
