## Sandwich

Sandwiches were a type of handfood made from a variety of ingredients, such as spiced chicken or ham spread, all stacked and affixed on or between two (or more) slices of bread. They were consumed by simple sailors to the rich and powerful.

Variations
A Waterdhavian establishment called Mother Teshla's Turret Club for Adventurers served hot dripping butter-and-bacon sandwiches to its guests.

In the late 14 century DR in Cormyr, prawn-and-cress sandwiches were all the rage in the king's court. Another variation, and admittedly much simpler, sandwich from Cormyr was made with bread and goat cheese. In the mid–14 century DR, sandwich platters could be found served in taverns and inns, such as The Hidden Lady of Suzail. Some of these sandwiches were stacks of folded meat and millet. In Immersea, cheese sandwiches were a common snack.

The Elfsong Tavern in the city of Baldur's Gate served plain or spiced melted cheese sandwiches among its many foods in its taproom.

The Happy Hippocampus in the town of Hill's Edge, the Sunset Vale, served a distinct variation of open-faced sandwiches called hot bites.

In the metropolis of Ravens Bluff, the Dancing Bear tavern served gypsy dishes made by the Stravinsky family. Gyro sandwiches were among the unique delicacies served. Another establishment that served sandwiches was a gnomish out-of-the-way café named Le PoTeete. Its gnomish dishes included Rosto, a sandwich of roast rothé on rye bread, served with gnomish pale ale. Frieda's Fish Emporium of Ylraphon in the Vast was an eatery that specialized in fresh and fried fish. One of the items on offer was a fish sandwich.

Sandwiches were made as far south as the Border Kingdoms and the settlement of Gallard, where fried-fish sandwiches were a common and fragrant occurrence.

Platters of cold-meats-and-cheese sandwiches accompanied by jugs of ale were served in taverns of the planar metropolis of Sigil.

History
Sandwiches were eaten as far back and the ancient Empire of Netheril, where meats were smoked to be made into sandwiches.

## Sarsae

Sarsae was a hardy savory fruit grown and cultivated in north Faerûn.

Description
Sarsae fruits varied in size, depending on the levels of sunlight. They had tough, spotted green skin that changed color from yellow to white as they ripened. The fruit's hard flesh was yellow-white in color, similar to that of apples. It had a tart, almost cheese-like flavor.

Usages
Sarsae fruits were used in cooking in similar ways to tomatos.

## Sabkhat el Talab

Sabkhat el Talab was a sage and diviner of the Bedine.

Description
He wore distinct clothing.

Relationships
For whatever reason, Sabkhat el Talab was the subject of divinations on the part of Cormyr.

History
In, Sabkhat el Talab sought out a path across the Scimitar Peaks that was shorter than the one commonly used. He went missing during the search, prompting the Crown of Cormyr to send agents to look for him. The last person to see him was a serpent lord who saw him go down a crevasse along the road.

Actually, Sabkhat el Talab had died near the center of the lifedrain effect created by a phaerimm spell within the crevasse.

Appendix
Notes

Background
Sabkhat el Talab may be intended to be the same as Sabkhat; his involvement with the Ja'ur Dai, similar to the Ju'ur Dai, would seem to imply so. However, it is possible he may be simply a homage to The Parched Sea, as he is a diviner himself instead of having a sorcerous daughter, and furthermore, dies by the end of the adventure, making it bizarre at best that he would die in 1360 DR as well.

## Sauvrurn

Sauvrurn was a nobleman from Marsember during the late 14 century DR.

Personality
He was not enthusiastic about the prospect of bedding Alusair Nacacia Obarskyr, and treason, for all his antipathy regarding the Obarskyrs, was still a prospect he was jittery about.

Appearance
He sounded cultured, and used a strong, musky perfume.

Relationships
He conspired together with Thandro as a member of the Rightful Conspiracy.

Possessions
He'd been able to gather together a comb that slew lice, a head for a walking stick that always knew north, and three more baubles from his family.

History
Sauvrurn and Thandro discussed their involvement in a future assassination attempt on Filfaeril Obarskyr and Alusair, respectively queen and princess regent of Cormyr, in . He was reticent to do much, but Thandro reassured him enough to get him to participate in the planned assassination. He had gathered five minor baubles in advance, and could muster twelve swordsmen; he was instructed to head for a lamppost near Thelvarspike Lane in advance of their attack on Thundaerlyn Hall. Their discussion happened in a warehouse full of Selgauntan glowstones, which prevented them from being scried on, but unknown to them, two merchants and fugitives, Malakar Surth and Bezrar were eavesdropping on them.

## Samanthiss

Samanthiss was a medusa, or greater medusa by some accounts, who practiced wizardry and ran the Morden Winery in the Vast, alongside Vorlon, her maedar mate, in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
Like other medusae, Samanthiss had a monstrous appearance and a head full of living snake hair.

Personality
Samanthiss was a highly intelligent medusa and was considered an oddity among her kin. She lacked the vicious malice common among the medusae and preferred a studious lifestyle. Samanthiss happily stayed in seclusion from others and dedicated her time to magic and winemaking.

Abilities
As a medusa, Samanthiss possessed a deadly petrifying gaze, and her serpentine snake hair had a venomous bite. She also used the same medusa poison to coat her arrows. As a mage, Samanthiss was a competent spellcaster and had access to a number of spells collected in the spellbooks she stored in the winery cellar. All of Samanthiss's books were penned in an arcane language, unknown to those unfamiliar with such magics. The medusa knew how to sling spells from the fourth circle of magic and had such spells as feeblemind, phantasmal killer, vampiric touch, lightning bolt, and mirror image memorized, among others.

Together with her mate, Samanthiss could work magics that grew plants, trees, and henges and could summon and entrap various guardian plant creatures and beasts. Some of the creatures under the medusa's command were local crows and her huge crow familiar called Stoner, who kept its attentive eye on the winery from high up in the air. The duo also kept two stray dogs who found a home at the winery, Merlot and Wander, as well as a collection of bloodthorns, thornslingers, and serpent vine snakes.

Possessions
Samanthiss's most prized possessions were her spellbooks, priced at 4,000 gp if sold to such interested parties as Ravens Bluff's Wizards Guild.

Activities
Alongside her husband, Vorlon, Samanthiss committed her life to studying and improving the art of winemaking—something the couple found a passion in. Arcane magic was another passion of the medusa. She spent her days in the cellars underneath the winery, where she established a study, researching magic and creating variations of existing spells. At night, the medusa walked the land and tended to the vines. She also left her seclusion when her petrifying gaze was needed to defend the winery from marauders, bandits, and roaming monstrous humanoids.

History
Samanthiss and Vorlon hailed from the Cold Lands and the Bloodstone Lands. Vorlon suffered a loss of family at the hands of some adventurers early in his life, sending him onto a nomadic traveling life. Eventually, he came across Samanthiss, and the two became mates. Vorlon felt a strong familial affection and protectiveness for his mate because of the trauma and loss he suffered. Keeping them both alive was his goal in life.

A couple of years after the, Samanthiss and Vorlon came across a newly rebuilt winery just outside of Dragon Falls in the Vast. The winery was run by a priest of Chauntea named Larn. Vorlon, a follower of the Earthmother himself, and Samanthiss were both impressed with the grapevines and the winery's well-maintained condition. Samanthiss took an opportunity to petrify Larn, allowing Vorlon to take over the priest's identity. The couple continued running the winery for the following decade. Vorlon showed remorse over Larn's fate but came to love winemaking. Larn's stone body was kept in good condition for if the medusas ever needed food.

Vorlon and Samanthiss discovered a set of Books of the Master Winemaker that taught them everything they needed to know to produce quality wine. Samanthiss found the winery's extensive cellars and used them to house her study, where the medusa could hone her skill by day while hiding her monstrous appearance and caring for the grapevine by night. The new owners quickly mastered winemaking and began producing a variety of good-tasting wine, much more than they could ever hope to consume themselves. There were many dangers in the Vast: bandits, monstrous humanoids, wild beasts. All were known to target the Morden Winery occasionally. Over the next decade, the medusa and her maedar mate defended their new home and used the slain intruders for food. To further protect themselves, Samanthiss and Vorlon used various magics to grow a thorny thicket around the vineyards, further segregating it with a row of trees. They also summoned creatures to act as eyes in the sky, guards, and muscle.

However, the winery's rightful owner, Shauna Morden of Ravens Bluff, discovered its existence after her father Charles Morden's death circa the . She renovated and reopened the Tavern of the Garden and was interested in using a family-owned winery. The young woman hired a group of capable adventurers of the Living City to investigate the presumably abandoned winery and its condition and establish a dialogue or partnership with its keepers, if there were any.

When the adventurers reached Dragon Falls, they met the proprietor of the local Tavern of the Flagon Dragon and found out that the winery was supplying the establishment with cheap but quality wine and that the hermits who ran the winery had few other interactions with the locals. The group also met a local hero and druid defender, Keldar of the Forest, who knew of Samanthiss's nature but did not consider the medusa and maedar to be disruptive to the natural balance and let them be. Subsequently, adventurers established a dialogue with the medusae, despite Vorlon's misguided attempt to let the adventurers perish in a harpy encounter. Subsequently, the Morden Winery was returned to the Morden family, and, by some accounts, the maedar vintner was allowed to run the business; by others, the medusas were slain. Larn was believed to have been released from the decade of being a statue. In one account of the events, Samanthiss and her mate were so grateful for the adventurers' understanding they eagerly met with Shauna Morden and, when they were satisfied with the agreement that would let them work the winery in peace, they gifted the adventurers with copies of the Books of the Master Winemaker, as well as several other precious enchanted items and exotic precious wines.

## Saeraethra's Stream

Saeraethra's Stream was a minor waterway in the Delimbiyr Vale that merged into the Delimbiyr River on the western border of Daggerford's farmlands.

Description
Saeraethra's Stream began in the northern parts of the Misty Forest and trickled west, parallel Delimbiyr, down the Sword Hills, through the deep crags of the Lonely Barrens, crossing the Trade Way just south of the hamlet of Gillian's Hill. From there, Saeraethra's Stream curved northeast, passing through the crag at the bottom of the Wild Hills and merged into the River Delimbiyr in a small delta, west of Daggerford's farmlands and east of the Lizard Marsh.

History
The stream gained its name in the . It was named after the adventuring bard Saeraethra, who inhabited its shores, was killed defending the land against a band of hobgoblin raiders.

After spring began in the, Harnrim Starangh made a quick raid near Mouth o' Gargoyles on the sanctum of Vangerdahast, then retired from his post of Royal Mage. After he slew three War Wizards and escaped, the War Wizard Huldyl Rauthur ordered Darthym, who'd just reported the losses, to wake Sarmeir and have the two of them stand guard in the armory, where two people had been slain.

## Saer Fleiner

Saer Fleiner was a middle class inhabitant of the city of Baldur's Gate in late 15 century DR.

Description
Saer Fleiner was a dwarvish woman with a clean-shaven face and dark brown hair, gathered in a braid and formed into a tight hairstyle. She had a light freckled skin and green eyes. Fleiner wore embroidered finery of green and golden colors.

History
In the, as the Cult of the Absolute menaced the city of Baldur's Gate, the gates between the Lower City of Baldur's Gate and its more affluent Upper City were closed, monitored by the Flaming Fist mercenaries and allowing only the wealthy and current residents of the Upper City through. Saer Fleiner was terrified of the cultist army that loomed over over city and was outraged by Baldur's Gate shutting the gates. The dwarf believed that the Upper City had more than enough space for everyone.

## Saer Frunilla

Saer Frunilla was an inhabitant of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
She was a human woman with a fair bronze skin tone, blue eyes, and a full head of red curly hair.

History
In the, Saer Frunilla was among those citizens of the Gate who were not convinced by the broadsheets and propaganda coming from the office of the new Grand Duke. The rulers promised safety from the encroaching army of the Cult of the Absolute, but Frunilla thought that they were as vulnerable as if they were outside the city walls. However, she was hopeful that when the time came, Lord Enver Gortash would fulfill his promises to the city.

## Sarutobi

Sarutobi was a renowned martial artist from Kara-Tur who lived long before the late 13 century DR.

History
Sarutobi was already a famed fighter by the time he was to fight a duel with a stone giant. To aid the martial artist, a wealthy merchant of Shou Lung commissioned the creation of enchanted bracers, later to be known as Sarutobi's Stone Breakers. According to the story, Sarutobi passionately declined the gift, claiming that he needed no help in crushing the giant's skull. The martial artist survived the duel and won.

One day, Sarutobi stepped through a gate and disappeared into the planes, leaving a son behind. The merchant gifted the magic gauntlets to Sarutobi's son, who later went on a life-long quest to find his missing father. He never did.

## Sacrast Handragon

Sacrast Handragon was a Cormyrean noble in the late 15 century DR.

Description
He had a face that betrayed nothing. His voice was soft, close to being lazy.

Personality
When it suited him he could be ruthless and swift. He had very strong self-control.

Relationships
Marlin Stormserpent believed he merited watching, not full trust.

Arclath Delcastle knew him to be associated with Marlin Stormserpent, Mellast Ormblade, and Irlin Stonestable, as well as three lords around Arclath's own age, Broryn Windstag, Kathkote Dawntard, and Delasko Sornstern.

History
To Marlin Stormserpent's knowledge, the fortunes of the Handragon family had fared the worst.

Sacrast was involved in the intrigues weaved by Marlin Stormserpent around his blueflame items in the . When Marlin Stormserpent summoned his co-conspirators to tell them about an axe being a third such item, Sacrast arrived with great haste; in the ensuing conversation, he had some minor input.

When Marlin tried to release the ghost from the enchanted axe, Sacrast Handragon was amongst the people who were absent when Mirt came free instead. After Marlin related the events to him, Sacrast confirmed he'd be attending the Council of the Dragon to represent his family.

After the calamitous Council, Sacrast Handragon turned up at Staghaven House, dropping in on Broryn Windstag, who'd been discussing the outcome with Stonestable and Ormblade. He mentioned that, besides the butchery at Castle Obarskyr, where many older nobles had been cut down, he had seen many of the servants of the Stormserpent family running in the streets, claiming that Marlin and everyone at Stormserpent Towers had been slain. While there, they also received the visit of Traevyn Illance, who wanted to recruit them into his own faction should civil war rise, Marlin Stormserpent, who was worried about the mental invasions of the vampire archimage Manshoon, and Osbur, a servant of the late Olgarth Oldbridle.

## Sash of vitality ceaseless

A sash of vitality ceaseless was a magic sash worn about the waist that granted extra vitality to the wearer when it was most needed.

Description
The sash had a red and white color pattern, with red representing "harm" and white representing "healing".

Powers
While wearing the sash of vitality ceaseless, individuals recovered more rapidly, but only if they were badly injured.

Notable Owners
The Cult of the Dragon located at the Well of Dragons possessed a sash of vitality ceaseless.

Rumors & Legends
It was said that the true power of the sash of vitality ceaseless could only be tapped by a deva. For instance, if a deva wore the sash in conjunction with certain other items crafted by their ancestors, the objects were said to grant further powers such as a resistance to fire. Devas who came into contact with such items often came to feel that they had possessed the same item in a prior life.

## Sallyanne Corks

Sallyanne Corks was a female drow inhabitant of the city of Baldur's Gate in late 15 century DR.

History
In the, Sallyanne Corks and five other Lolthsword drow women were targeted and eventually murdered by Orin the Red, a Bhaalspawn member of the Murder Tribunal that hid its ugly carcass under the streets of Baldur's Gate. In her depraved commitment to Bhaal, the deity of murder, Orin hunted down individuals who came to represent other Bhaalspawn, who pressed a century earlier in the Bhaalspawn crisis, in this case – the drow Bhaalspawn Sendai. Sallyanne and the other drow were turned into gory dark effigies to the Lord of Murder in the cave basement of the groundkeeper of the Bloomridge Park in the Lower City.

## Satyr language

The Satyr language, sometimes referred to as the Faun language, was the racial language of satyrs.

Speakers
Beyond satyrs themselves, Satyr was a common secondary language among many of Faerûn's demihuman subraces. These included forest gnomes, lightfoot halflings, lythari, moon elves, rock gnomes, sun elves, tallfellow halflings, and wood elves. Beyond demihumans, Satyr was also commonly spoken by other fey creatures, such as hamadryads, korreds, oreads, and the fey-associating bacchae.

Satyr was also fairly common as a secondary language among most druids, including those devoted to certain nature deities. These included the druids of Chauntea, druids of Eldath, druids and druid/rangers of Mielikki, the druids of Silvanus, the druids of Sheela Peryroyl, and the druids of Shiallia. Other forms of specialty priests in the Realms for whom Satyr was a commonly known secondary language included the Eldathyn peacemen and the Shiallian woodwives.

Notable Speakers
 Holly Graham, a human druid that operated Holly's Mill near Ravens Bluff.
 Lanseril Snowmantle, a half-elf druid of Silvanus and one of the original Knights of Myth Drannor.
 Prismal the Outrageous.
 Rairun Blackbrow, a human druid of Silvanus.
 Veda Silas, a Ravenian half-elf wizard.

## Sanjos Irridan

The Sanjos Irridan was a vampire necromancer and surgeon who sold undead grafts at the Corpse Market in the Shadowfell city of Evernight as of the late 15 century DR.

Personality
He was evil and insane.

Activities
He sold body parts, including those which were magically animated and could be grafted onto a living person. These included everything from limbs to organs. Such specimens were known to be hard to procure as they had to be removed from (often unwilling) undead creatures without being damaged. He would also perform the actual grafting of the body part onto a client for an extra fee. This required a complicated and vile ritual that no sane or reasonable person would generally wish for.

History
As of, Sanjos Irridan was the most famous provider of grafts at the Corpse Market. A large banner over his shop proudly displayed the slogan: "an arm and a leg for less than an arm and a leg."

## Sangora

Sangora was a vampire merchant in the Shadowfell city of Evernight.

Description
She was very ancient, with messy, long white hair, sunken eyes, and sharp fangs.

Personality
Sangora loved to gossip and to ask and answer questions of new acquaintances, especially visitors to Evernight who had arrived from the Material Plane. She was quick to spot a potential customer, and would spread her cloak wide and call out to offer them her wares.

Activities
She was the proprietor of Sangora Sanguinaries at the Corpse Market where she sold warm blood. As a side business, she also sold information about Evernight for a hefty price (or for an answer to a probing question). She was very knowledgeable about things like Evernight history and phenomena such as the Crevices of Dusk.

Relationships
She had four vampire spawn assistants. While she evidently trusted them, they were prone to act against her wishes when they believed that she would not learn of their disobedience.

History
As of the 1490s DR, Sangora was active in Evernight and had not been to Neverwinter in over ten years (not since before Dagult Neverember had earned the respect of that city's people).

There was a sunken road between Salopar's Field and the neighboring Aerhiot's Field. The hedges outside both fields had overgrown into the ditch-level path to such a degree that they formed a gloomy tunnel-like space underneath their intertwined branches.

Birds and squirrels darted along the path every time someone trod upon it.

## Samrith Vess

Samrith Vess was an accomplished scribe and researcher from Baldur's Gate who was active in the late 15 century DR. She was an expert on Chultan history.

Personality
Samrith was empathetic and had a good and sincere heart. She sought knowledge for the sake of enlightening herself and others, and tales of ancient Chult made her giddy.

While she was very well read, she believed that true knowledge could only be gained through experience. She dreamed of visiting the ruins of Mezro upon learning that the city had been built by a god.

Possessions
She traveled with her father's old spellbooks despite not being a wizard like he had been.

Relationships
She was very close with her bodyguard, Zara, and would grip her arm tightly when excited. Zara, in turn, was permissive and protective.

History
Samrith spent most of her life in libraries and places of learning on the Sword Coast. Through her extensive reading, she came to be enamored with stories about Chult, particularly the ruined city of Mezro. She became an expert in Mezroan history, including everything from the construction of the Maze of Life to the special tea once enjoyed by the city's royalty. Eventually, she decided that she had to visit Chult for herself in order to gain the kind of experience and expertise that books could not provide.

Around the time that the death curse struck Toril, Samrith spent her savings to book a trip from Baldur's Gate to Port Nyanzaru. Once there, she arranged for the guide Qawasha to take her and Zara to the ruins of Mezro.

She learned a great deal about Mezro from the knowledgeable Qawasha during the journey to Mezro, most notably that he believed that the city had not actually been destroyed but rather removed from Chult and placed into a "paradise." Once they neared the ruins, Samrith was approached by a trusting chwinga, who traded her a magic charm in exchange for a seashell from the Moonshae Isles. This very charm would later grant Qawasha a vision of a terrible device located deep in the jungle that was preventing Mezro from being restored. Samrith promised to join him in a quest to destroy the device, and proposed to return to Port Nyanzaru and finally study to become a wizard like her father.

## Sahha

Sahha was an athletic game or sport derived from handball that first emerged in the late 15 century DR among the prestigious and wealthy of Amn before spreading to wider society.

Rules

A sahha match was played on a large square pitch by two teams of five players. Players could set, spike, check, or volley the ball across the pitch, with points scored by moving the ball into the opposing team's goal. The two traditional teams were the shark and the nautilus.

Sahha balls were often very well decorated, and included designs such as beholders, red dragons, and miniature giant space hamsters.

History
Sometime around the 1470s DR, House Bessail of Amn was in its death-throes after years of mismanagement by its financially challenged patriarch. Upon his death, the heir Valenti took drastic and risky steps to save the family business, inspired by his sister Tche's favorite game, handball. Valenti tasked his most trusted sailmaker Rhaal Voleau with creating a tough yet beautifully decorated game ball using canvas sailcloth, lighter than the traditional heavy leather balls inflated with bladders used in handball.

The summer after the creation of the new balls, Valenti and Tche went to a family friend's estate on Lake Esmel, and showed them off with a variation of handball gameplay while all the other richest families of the Merchant's Domain took their Midsummer holidays there on the lake. The ploy worked, and the upper classes of Amn became obsessed with the light, ornate, and above all novel balls. The fashionable and avant-garde trend-chasers couldn't resist investing in his product, and practically every wealthy family in Amn competed to obtain their own sets of lavishly decorated game balls. Within a few years, the new game, now dubbed sahha, was a mainstay of summer fun among the vacationers of Lake Esmel, with merchant houses sponsoring professional teams and high-stakes bets being placed on the outcomes. Casual play also spread amongst the whole society of Amn, partially due to imitation of the upper classes, but also due to Valenti and his sisters advertising it heavily as they sold their sahha balls.

A few years later, Tche traveled up the Sword Coast with Rhaal to spread their merchandise, eventually coming to Neverwinter. She advertised the game there with the approval of the Sunite Midsummer Festival coordinator Yina Morradi, including handing out and selling pamphlets describing the rules and techniques of the game. These one-page pamphlets were written by her younger sister and given oddly gravitas-heavy names like Codex of the Check, Compendium of the Set, Enchiridion of the Volley, and Fundamentals of the Spike. The game proved quite popular in Neverwinter as well, with even uncivilized Sword Coasters appreciating a good ball game.

The game also spread to Chult through Amn's colony of Port Nyanzaru, with confused explorers finding sahha balls left lying in the jungle.

## Salty Dogs: Curse of the Rum Golem

History
In the, a copy of Salty Dogs: Curse of the Rum Golem managed to captivate and exhilarate a stuck-up sage from Candlekeep, teacher of Nansi Gretta, the proprietor of The Bibliophile in the city of Baldur's Gate. During Nansi's time studying at the fortress library, she was a proponent of preserving copies of adventure novels at Candlekeep, to her teacher's opposition. Years later, a copy of Salty Dogs ended up the only thing to read on the teacher's long carriage drive, and he found himself absorbed by the adventure so much that he invited Nansi Gretta to Candlekeep to have dinner there and discuss a possible scholarly paper on the topic of pulpy adventure novels.

## Saeraede Lyonora

Saeraede Lyonora, better known as the Slayer, was a Netherese human sorceress turned into a unique undead creature that awakened in the mid–8 century and went on a slaying rampage following Elminster Aumar.

Personality
When not slaying mindlessly, Saeraede was charming, but duplicitous and depraved, with utter scorn for anyone's life save her own, and very deliberate about its depravity. Also, despite it issuing from her, the twinkling sound she made while moving really got on her nerves.

Description
Most of the time, the Slayer was just a faintly visible mist, which made a sound like bells as it moved. It could turn into a whirlwind, or take other forms, becoming a morass of claws and jaws.

When aping the form she had in life, Saeraede Lyonora had green eyes, glossy black hair to her slim waist, broad shoulders, and tufty eyebrows, though she made a point of projecting a figure that was taller, slimmer, and more buxom than she remembered herself to be.

Abilities
The Slayer possessed a startling ability to devour living flesh in less than a heartbeat, leaving little but bones behind; those bones sometimes crumbled into dust to be carried away by a stiff breeze. As a formless thing, she was able to flow through small cracks, even shutter chinks. It also had the ability to turn into a ghostly mass of jaws, with barbed tentacles and long claws protruding from it. It was able to thwart many wards easily, though upon taking damage in that shape, it returned to its mist form. The Slayer was registered by wards that detected uncontrolled magic; such wards made it out to be among the strongest things they were designed to detect. It could also draw power from items, somehow. When it had drawn enough power, the Slayer could even thwart the magic of gods. It also showed an uncanny ability to track, and even perceive past actions, showing agitation when it touched spots where strong aggressive magic had been used.

Saeraede Lyonora was also a sorceress, and could cast at least one offensive, acidic spell, capable of reducing a man to sludge in moments. Her ghostly form was susceptible to spells such as shatterstar, which targeted incorporeal entities. She could also project images, and scry with spellspheres, even towards distant mages. Given time, she could also warp existing magic, no matter how powerful, to ensure it responded only to her touch. While she could coalesce enough to move physical objects, that required her to expend much energy that she could only recover by stealing lifeforce.

Possessions
Saeraede collected a number of feminine dresses in particular from passing caravans: one of them was blue and ruffled, one was black with slits, and another was red, with gem-highlighted dragons along the hips.

Relationships
In her undeath, Saeraede had taken two lovers, both of which she drained to death: Brandagaeris and Sardon.

History
Saeraede Lyonora considered that in life, she had been a feared sorceress. At some point, she became a phantasmal entity and was entombed in a titanic stone casket at some location, which was surrounded by runes of some form. Lettering outside the chamber was Netherese, indicating she had been imprisoned by people of that nationality.

She was later disturbed, first by the Chosen of Mystra Elminster Aumar, and then, years later, by a nameless band of adventurers that included Iyriklaunavan, an elf wizard, and Nuressa, a warrior, on Mirtul of the . Elminster Aumar had become trapped in the tomb somehow and, after being disturbed by the adventurers, escaped. Shortly after, the runes went haywire and a strange mist was released from the giant coffin; the Slayer perceived that the creatures that had disturbed the tomb still lived.

Later, in mid-spring of the, the mist followed Elminster Aumar, who had first disturbed its grave, to Castle Felmorel; it followed him inside, and when he left in the middle of the night, it followed him outside. Along the way, it took a detour, passing through Torntlar's Farm, where the farmer Habaertus Ilynker swung his hoe into the misty body out of curiosity. The mist struck him, leaving only a wind-scoured skeleton. The misty entity continued to pursue Elminster, lingering longer in places where he'd been, like the hill near Tresset's Ringyl, where it showed signs of distraction and hunger. Afterwards, it headed for the path between Aerhiot's Field and Salopar's Field, where it consumed the farmer Nuglar and his three oxen.

The entity then resumed its tracking, heading for Moonshorn Tower, where Elminster had been. There, it somehow forced the doors open, and flowed up into the repository for Netherese texts and trinkets. There, it was interrupted by the tower guardian, Mardasper Oblyndrin, who struck it with a shatterstar spell; though it was effective, she endured it and retaliated, killing him with a spell.

In the, the entity assaulted the tower of Yintras Bedelmrin, devouring all the deer that grazed around it one night before squeezing in through a window that was ajar. It was, however, repelled by the mage, who was wielding a blessing from Azuth himself. At some point during or after the crowning of the sorceress Dasumia as queen of Galadorna, the mysterious being assaulted Glymril Gard, slaying the mage Klandaerlas Glymril, the Master of Wyverns in that isolated castle. Late into the year, it remained close to Elminster and followed him into Galadorna when he became the Court Mage, staying near the spires of the House of the Unicorn; the entity fled into the streets after the goddess Mystra revealed herself.

Later, it had taken residence in a ruined keep between Starmantle and Westgate, which belonged to Karsus of Netheril, whom Saeraede Lyonora had been aware of. This structure was called Karsus' Keep.

In the early spring of the, the monster had gained the nickname of "the Slayer" after settling in a section of the forest near Starmantle between Oggle's Stream and Rairdrun Hill, and completely depopulating it of any animal life larger than a mouse, giving the area the name the Dead Place. It also eliminated merchants that passed by, livestock that got too close, farmers, woodsmen, a patrol of Duke Horostos's men, and even a large force led by a priest of Tempus. The thing was not spotted by griffon riders; the Duke sent a band of adventurers after it, the Frostfire Banner, which it eliminated without encountering any difficulties. Afterwards, it schemed to lure mages to Karsus's Keep. However, while it neared Westgate to seek mages, it learned that Elminster was approaching the location, and decided to target him specifically.

Eventually, no one in Reth or Westgate would take on the assignment of slaying the monster, having heard of the fate of all who faced the Slayer. By the time the Slayer had devoured the entire population of Ayken's Stump, a woodcutting village, the Harpers took notice of the situation and promised Duke Horostos a report on the matter by the end of the season. In the meantime, the Slayer, now planning on using its name in life, Saeraede Lyonora, continued to scheme on how to capture Elminster and to devour adventurers all the while. She managed to draw Elminster to the Keep, where he was attacked by adventurers; she killed the adventurers and then lured him with her own twinkling down a well, leading him into a hidden throne room. Elminster followed Saeraede's phantom into the throne room, neither of them aware of Ilbryn Starym, who was hidden there already.

Saeraede Lyonora cast a spell to shut the well entrance, to trap Elminster there without him noticing, and then pretended to require a ritual to be freed. Upon being freed, she promised Elminster the use of those runes and their secrets. She then tried to trick him into a different casting, which he fell into hook, line, and sinker. At that moment, she was only briefly cut off by the sudden arrival of Azuth, accompanied by the mages Caladaster Daermree, Tabarast, and Beldrune, but quickly resumed her attack on Elminster, Azuth's spells passing cleanly through her to attack the Chosen. At that precise time, the six dreadspells of Shar, led by the Sharran priest Elryn, discharged a fusillade of staves and rods upon the chamber. Saeraede protected Elminster in those last few moments, to ensure she could feed on him herself. However, just as she was on the cusp of regaining full form and life, she was stabbed through the heart by Ilbryn Starym. When he pulled his sword out to try and strike at Elminster, Saeraede's bulk exploded furiously, reducing Ilbryn to ash in return.

The blade had a curved point and many barbs angled backwards, ensuring that it would cause massive tearing when pulled from a wound. It bore a sickly yellow glow from the virulent enchantments upon it.

The dagger had lethal and gruesome enchantments, including a vampiric negative energy bite that drained the life force of the victim and transferred it to the wielder. The Priestess Vicala had the blade during the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar during the Darkening, when she attacked the Midler farmhouse and took the Midler family as sacrificial prisoners. During her time around Eveningstar, she used the blade to sacrifice many innocent farmers.

## Sand mephit

A sand mephit was a kind of mephit tied to the Elemental Plane of Earth.

Description
Sand mephits had small, winged humanoid forms, colored in earthy browns. They shed a small amount of sand as they moved.

Personality
These mephits were generally mischievous and mean-spirited, seeking to cause trouble whatever the situation.

Combat
Sand mephits fought by clawing at their enemies, and could unleash a breath of searing heat to burn everything in front of them. Although they weren't very strong, they were immune to fire and life-destroying effects.

Ecology
Sand mephits were short-lived creatures linked to the Elemental Plane of Earth, and were closer related to salt and dust mephits than other kinds. They preferred dry, arid environments, and could be found in the Stonelands of Faerûn, as well as in the desert of Har'Akir in the Domains of Dread. They could also be sent by the powers of the Lower planes as agents against their enemies.

## Salamander War

The Salamander War, sometimes called the Salamander Wars, was a disasterous yearlong conflict waged by Thay in the, first alongside and then against an army of salamanders that they themselves had summoned.

Location
The war took place along the North Coast of the Wizards' Reach and in the south of Thay across what would become the Tharch of the Priador.

History
Causes
The Salamander War began as one of a long line of ambitious schemes by the Red Wizards and the rulers of Thay to conquer neighboring lands. In this case, a triumvirate consisting of Tharchion Hargrid Tenslayer of Lapendrar and the Zulkirs of Evocation and of Conjuration plotted to conquer the cities of the North Coast of the Wizards' Reach. To achieve this, the Zulkir of Conjuration obtained an alliance with Fyzzar, the Lord of Salamanders, and Marrake, the Sultan of the Efreet, to lend their elemental forces to an invasion of the region. The initial agreement was for the salamanders and efreet to eliminate all of Thay's competitors along the North Coast, and in exchange, the Zulkir would establish a permanent gate to the Plane of Fire and the area would be set eternally ablaze so as to establish a foothold for the elementals on Toril.
 Critically, the agreement was made without the involvement or knowledge of Kossuth, the god of elemental fire and the superior of both Fyzzar and Marrake. It was speculated that these lesser lords hoped to increase their standing at Kossuth's expense with this endeavor. The other major issue with this arrangement was that neither side intended to honor it: the Thayans intended to banish the elementals once their war goals were achieved while the Fire Lords intended to continue expanding their influence across Faerûn.

In 1357 DR, the invasion began with the Zulkir of Conjuration opening a temporary gate in the village of First Falls to allow the salamanders and efreet onto the Material Plane. The invading army comprised two or three Thayan legions supporting about 100 salamanders—led by their own commanders—and 100 efreet—who had been pledged to follow the Zulkir himself. Perhaps intentionally, the invasion was launched during one of the Simbul's absences from Aglarond.

Battles
The salamanders and efreet achieved their war goals with startling speed, razing the cities of Lasdur, Taskaunt, Murbant, and
Thasselen before forcing the immediate surrender of Hilbrand, capturing Laothkund, and laying siege to Escalant. This quickly led to conflict with their Thayvian allies, who had not expected such quick results and were unprepared to renege on the alliance, which they had planned to make look like an accidental failure to uphold their end of the bargain. When the elementals demanded that they be provided with the promised permanent gate, the Red Wizards instead attempted to banish them from the Material Plane. The Zulkir of Conjuration's gate in First Falls had been designed such that destroying or dispelling it would draw the salamanders and efreet back into their home plane as their ability to persist on the Material Plane was tied to its magic. However, while the Thayans succeeded in sending the efreet away, they were unable to banish the salamanders, who turned their full fury against their former allies. Faced with certain defeat, the Thayan triumvirate called for aid from their fellow tharchions and zulkirs. The threat posed by the rampaging salamanders forced the mightiest of the zulkirs, Szass Tam, to pull back from his invasion of Rashemen and send his undead forces southward.

For over a year, Thayvian undead, human, gnoll, and goblin forces battled the salamanders, who burned everything in their path. Hargrid Tenslayer was killed in the fighting, and the Thayans were forced to abandoned their new holdings in Lasdur, Taskaunt, and (to a lesser extent) Hilbrand. The Thayan naval facilities on Alaor were heavily damaged, and much of the island's vegetation and farmland were destroyed. The forces of Thay were soon worn down.

The tide only began to turn in Thay's favor when an up-and-coming Red Wizard named Aznar Thrul pleaded for aid from Kossuth, and with the help of the Kossuthan clergy, ultimately convinced the god to help. Kossuth saw his underlings' scheme as a power play against him and feared that it could spark conflict with other elemental gods, and so he sent his own army of fire elementals to combat the salamanders. This army wrecked havoc on the land, burning parts of the Yuirwood and killing many seelie sprites, but it successfully drove back the salamanders. In conjunction with the Kossuthan priests at the Temple of Kossuth in Escalant, Thrul arranged to create a one-way portal to the Elemental Plane of Fire through which many of the salamanders were made to flee. Thus, Thrul ironically defeated the salamanders by creating a permanent connection to their home plane—the very thing that the triumvirate had indented to avoid—and great pains were taken to keep the existence of this portal a secret in the following decades.

The war ended in the .

Aftermath
The war was considered a disaster for Thay despite their newly acquired territory. To make matters worse, the nation had to deal with the arrival of the Grand Army of the Tuigan almost immediately after the resolution of the war.

The territories gained by Thay during the war—notably the cities of Escalant, Laothkund, and Thasselen—were at first nominally considered to be part of the Tharch of Lapendrar, but they were instead merged with the Tharch of Bezantur to form a new tharch, the Priador. Aznar Thrul quickly eliminated the Tharchioness of Bezantur, Mari Agneh, to become the Tharchion of this new tharch. Meanwhile, the Tharch of Lapendrar expanded only slightly to control the territory west of the River Lapendrar and to nominally rule over Tilbrand.

During or shortly after the war, the Zulkir of Conjuration who had triggered the whole affair was slain.While his death is not stated explicitly, Zulkirs are only replaced once they have been killed. He was replaced by Sabass, a Red Wizard who had opposed the plan from the very beginning and had even been willing to hire foreign adventurers to stop the initial plot. Meanwhile, Aznar Thrul would end up becoming the Zulkir of Evocation.

Kossuth's intervention in the war skyrocketed his already high popularity within Thay, and his faith was elevated to the status of the official religion of the Priador. The Red Wizards also worked to maintain a close relationship with the god and his clergy in hopes that he would support their future military endeavors, which led the Kossuthans to begin making demands, such as when high priest Mythalanir bid Aznar Thrul to acquire Yrix's ring of fire for him.

In the wake of the war, salamanders were despised all across Thay. This led them to be expelled from the Flaming Brazier, the grandest Thayan temple of Kossuth. The salamanders who actually participated in the war remained bitter, and some would watch the exit of Thrul's Ecalant portal and attempt to slay any mortals who dared cross its threshold. Others were punished by Kossuth for daring to rebel against him, with some being sentenced to serve the Red Wizards for a thousand years, such as by acting as guardians within the Citadel.

A tshala known as Flamedancer swore revenge on both the Thayans and Kossuthans in the wake of the war, during which Kossuth's fire elementals destroyed its home.

## Sally Flymm

Sally Flymm was a merchant and cobbler who, alongside her husband, Dravo Flymm, ran Flymm's Cobblers in the city of Baldur's Gate in the late 14 century DR. Sally was also the mother of the infamous Lord Gortash, one of the leaders of the Cult of the Absolute.

Description
Sally was a human woman who, in the late 15 century DR, was an aged matron. She had a light skin tone and hair silvered with age and dark eyes. While running the shop, Sally wore a simple black dress with red frills and a leather corset over it. Her wrinkled neck was decorated with a simple silvered necklace.

Possessions
Sally and Dravo's shop was located in the Lower City of Baldur's Gate, near Beehive Goods and Wares.

History
Sally and her Husband were poor cobblers who struggled with having their ends meet whilst running the cobbligng business. At some point, the married couple ran into serious debt, owing money to multiple parties, including some vicious loan sharks who threatened to torch the store. Out of desperation, the Flymms sold their young son Enver's services to a warlock, earning a hefty sum that secured the shop and their couple's safety. Despite a cruel and desperate act, Sally believed that they did what was best for Enver and the parents. The boy was smart, too smart, according to Sally, so the mother was at peace with the difficult decision to give Enver up and all three surviving versus all three being murdered by the loan sharks.

Years later, Enver Flymm, now using the name of Enver Gortash, became a warlock in his own right and made a fortune by smuggling weapons. His ambition led Enver to become one of the three leaders of the Cult of the Absolute, assembling an army of brainwashed servants, many of whom were infected with altered mind flayer tadpoles. While unsavory activities were hidden, Enver rose in prominence in Baldur's Gate, becoming a lord. Still bitter over his parents' actions, Gortash stormed into Flymm's Cobblers like a fist of justice and infected his parents with mind-altering parasites. These tadpoles, however, were different; instead of brainwashing them, the creatures dominated the Flymms. Sally was happy and eager, running the business and praising her beloved son for becoming a Lord, all while her true self was locked inside her mind, unable to do much but watch her body being puppeteered by the parasite. Sally's husband, Dravo, is faced with an even darker fate. The parasite completely destroyed his mind, leaving nothing but a smiling puppet behind. By the time the Cult of the Absolute's army attacked the Gate, Sally and Dravo had been controlled by the parasites for several months. It was unknown if the two survived the ensuing battle for the city.

## Sayan

Sayan was a city in Laothan in Malatra and the biggest seaport in the realm.

Geography
It was situated on Laothan's northern coast, on the shore of the Eastern Sea southeast of the Hanchi Shoals. A trail ran west along the coast to Thiam and another ran south through the jungle to Pursai.

Government
The city was the home of the Nuyen, a powerful clan in Laothan. As of 1357 DR, it was ruled by Prince Nuyen Phi. The Nuyen and their allies and supporters together maintained the largest army in Laothan and dominated the north. Prince Nuyen Phi had also grown deaf to the edicts of the monarch of Laothan, Thok Lian, and even rebellious.

Economy
The port received a great deal of overseas trade from the northern realms of Kara-Tur in the mid–14 century DR.

Relations
A number of spies and assassins of Shou heritage from T'u Lung had established themselves in Sayan and worked  for anyone with the coin to hire them. Prime among them was the Sung ninja clan. Thus, the city was rife with spies serving the Thok clan, rivals of the Nuyen. There were even a few spies serving Priest-King Vishnan VII of the Kuong Kingdom. In the eyes of some conservative Kuong, the city was "another center of decadence" in Laothan.

The process of smoking took several days. The fish was cooked in enclosed smokehouses. The result was a savory food. Sarkul was never used as a filling for Tantran baeranth pies.

## Sangehirn

Sangehirn was an unusual specialty of psionics that dealt with focusing the Invisible Art towards curative and healing ends.

Culture
These psionics used focused their efforts on their bodies, keeping them physically fit. They typically viewed bodies as patterns of muscles, bones, and blood flow. It was not uncommon for sangehirns to develop a selfless attitude, caring more for the wellbeing of others than for their own body.

In the late 14 century DR, the city of Gildenglade in Turmish was home to a group of sangehirn and aspiring sangehirns known as the Hands of the Glade.

Abilities
All sangehirns in time came to master the following psionic powers: adapt body, affinity field, biofeedback, body adjustment, body purification, empathic feedback, empathic transfer, energy adaptation, energy conversion, forced share pain, inertial barrier, leech field, oak body, psionic iron body, psionic restoration, psionic revivify, psychic chirurgery, psychofeedback, restore extremity, specified energy adaptation, suspend life, sustenance, thicken skin, timeless body, true metabolism, and vigor.

Through their mastery of healing powers a sangehirn gradually learned how to alter the targeting and distance of the psionic abilities that came naturally to them, allowing them to use on others powers that normally could only target the psion themselves. The powers that could altered in this way were biofeedback, body adjustment, body purification, suspend life, sustenance, and vigor. However, suspend life could only be manifested upon another target if they were willing.

As they continued to advance their psionic mastery, sangehirns obtained a supernatural healing factor, allowing them to recover from harm more quickly. However, this healing factor could not restore damaged caused by starvation or thirst, not could it regrow or reattach a limb.

## Sarltan

Sarltan was the name used by a drow during his infiltration of Scornubel in .

Description
When disguised by magic, Sarltan resembled a fat, unlovely man.

Personality
Sarltan was a businesslike, very decisive man, who was willing to trade, but turned to killing at the first sign of treachery.

Activities
Sarltan was busy around the clock, even at night, acting the part of a trader, which involved a fair amount of genuine trading.

Relationships
All the other drow impersonating locals in Scornubel preferred to defer trading offers related to Underdark valuables to Sarltan. There was also a guard snake in his lair.

History
In, Dove Falconhand investigated Scornubel looking for signs of drow replacing citizens with cloaking magicks; she took the disguise of a noble drow woman named Iylinvyx, and began peddling glowcap mushrooms and deep wines openly throughout the night. At Cata's Pump, she was oriented by the barkeep to speak to Sarltan; without anyone telling her where Sarltan was, she tried instead asking at Chasper's Trading Tower, where she was told to ask Daeraude in Blackmanacles by another drow impersonator, who identified herself as Yamaerthe. After Daeraude, she was told to seek a corner lantern and candle seller, a lock storage keeper, and a highly discreet hiresword sub-contractor. She was then led to Delsart's Drive, and then to Pelmuth's Draw; shortly after, she found the door, and after leaving all her steel weaponry there with a guard, she was finally allowed to see him.

He spoke with her briefly; while ascertaining her intentions, Sarltan asked what her true shape was. When Dove claimed she was a drow, he immediately made a gesture that brought two crossbowmen to shoot at her. During the ensuing fight, Dove slew many of his men, and nailed his hands to the desk at which he was sitting with a dagger; however, when combat finished, she noticed the man's throat had been cut during the commotion, and his thigh was transfixed with a dead-magic-bladed knife that made it impossible for her to interrogate his dead body. She resolved to cut her losses and flee.

## Sacrifice soul

Sacrifice soul was a unique empowering spell from the necromancy school of magic that was granted to the most devoted servants of the githyanki Lich Queen Vlaakith CLVII.

Effects
The sacrifice soul spell was cast upon the corpse of a recently killed humanoid within a hundred feet away from the caster. The spell offered the dead being's soul to Vlaakith, and in exchange, she temporarily made the caster Vlaakith's undying aspect, greatly bolstering their protection as well as all physical and mental abilities. Lastly, the spell allowed the undying aspect to discharge six powerful beams of magic, similar in effect to the eldritch blast spell. When the spell was cast upon a still-living humanoid, they became afflicted by bad luck and weakened, while the opposite was the boon granted to the caster. Once the target creature was killed, the caster temporarily became Vlaakith's undying aspect.

History
In the, Tska'an, a gith assassin, was granted that spell by Vlaakith to hunt down and slay individuals marked for death for displeasing the Lich Queen. The group known as the Absolut Adversaries may have had been such targets during their travels across the blighted town of Reithwin.

## Sandiraksiva

Sandiraksiva was a great black courser, and vessel of the soul of one of Gaumahavis cubs.

Description
A mighty black horse, Sandiraksiva was exceptionally strong, able to pull as much as two normal horses, exceptionally fast, and had cloven hooves with which to climb the rocky lands of the Katakoro Shan.Standing  tall and weighing more than, his hooves was as cut by polished black stone, and his eye near shining with yellow light.

Personality
In his young years he preferred to graze in the high meadows and tundras of the Katakoro Shan, but after his imprisonment by the Raja of Solon and later the Dalai Lama he embraced the wild steppes of the Hordelands. He tolerated no other stallions in his herd.

Abilities
He was able to get energy from the very light of the moon, and it was moonlight that empowered him to be exceptionally strong and fast. Should he miss moonlight for an entire night, his unnatural powers would wane until he once more could bathe in moonlight.From his nose he could launch fireballs against target, and with his hooves he could fly, riding the wind in gallop.

Relationships

History
Born as a leopard cub of Gaumahavi, he developed a soul along his mother while Surtava found enlightenment. Several reincarnations later his soul was of the black courser Sandiraksiva. While roaming the high meadows of the Katakoro Shan he was ambushed and captured by the Raja of Solon, Ambuchar Devayam, who used him to coerce his mother Gaumahavi, at that point inhabiting the body of a mighty dragon, to help with the war against Ra-Khati. During the war however the Dalai Lama managed to capture him, though he knew not what the great horse actually was. He was put in a magical prison from where he was unable to escape, and while Hubadais part of Yamun Khahan great horde passed near, king Ambuchar Devayam used the opportunity to threaten Ra-Khati into giving over Tsenya Bhrokiti and Sandiraksiva in return for continued peace. After a long and eventful journey down the Rainbow Falls, he was freed by a group of Tuigan spies who was seeking a way through Ra-Khati and onto Khazari’s southern flank. The tuigan could not stand to see such a magnificent horse in a cage and were willing to die to free him.

The leader of the local Tuigan force, Hubadai presented gifts to the mighty horse, twenty white mares was given from the horse-riding horde to the great stallion, a mark of respect that Sandiraksiva had not received in more than 50 years of imprisonment, first in Solon and then in Ra-Khati. Hubadai's respect and gifts to Sandiraksiva, led to an understanding between them, and it was not long before Sandiraksiva willingly became the mount of Hubadai.As a respected mount and ally of Hubadai, Sandiraksiva was gifted a further 80 white mares, and ruled over the 100 strong herd, being quite protective of them.

For all that, he was simply the service-mage at an outlet of Aurora's Emporium, usually only transporting goods via teleport spells from the central warehouse.

Instead, for his protection, he relied on a couple of enchanted rings: a ring of spell turning and a ring of spell storing, which contained Evard's black tentacles, feeblemind, fly, invisibility, and wizard eye. He wore many other shiny rings, all with gems, on all his fingers.

## Saisha

Saisha, or hammerlock, was a paralyzing ingestion poison used by Zhentarim in the 14 century DR.

Effects
The effects of saisha were such that soon after ingestion, the target became incapable of moving, with their joints tightly locked up; only the lungs and eyes could move, while the jaw hung slack. The joint locks were so effective that they gave the drug the name of "hammerlock", referencing the fact that joints had to be broken with a hammer afterwards. It lasted for about three hours.

Production
It was made out of herbs from Tashalar. The recipe was a secret.

History

Saisha was available to the Zhentarim in the mid-to-late 14 century DR, and in use by Calivar Murpeth on, who employed it on the cook Gustal Sorold, the harlot Talantha, and attempted to use it on the scrivener Maervidal Iloster, all of which he was to kill afterwards.

## Sarifal feywardens

The Sarifal feywardens were highly respected guardians and spies who served the fey realm of Sarifal and its leader, High Lady Ordalf, as of the 15 century DR. They were renowned as champions of the natural world.

Activities
Feywardens kept watch over Sarifal and all of its inhabitants, and pledged to do so for as long as the realm remained on the Material Plane. They were expected to follow and uphold the decrees of High Lady Ordalf, and worked constantly to combat corruption of their realm and any despoiling of the natural world. Their vigil focused mainly on countering threats from denizens of the Feydark, humans from the neighboring realms of the Moonshae Isles, and the Unseelie fey. They preferred to operate in the wilderness, often traveling in small, loosely organized parties led by their eldest members.

A few feywardens served as spies and adventurers beyond the borders of Sarifal. Their duty was not only to protect their realm from without but also to gather intelligence about its enemies.

Within Sarifal, the mightiest feywardens were seen as not only protectors but as representatives of the realm and as champions of the Feywild and of sacred forests across Toril. Experienced members were often sought out for their wisdom and knowledge, and had an extensive understanding of the workings of nature. They taught the importance of living in harmony with nature, and strove to instill this belief into future generations.

Abilities
Attuned to the natural world and blessed by the bizarre and life-giving energies of the Feywild, the feywardens wielded arcane and primal powers that helped them to traverse difficult terrain with ease and to defend themselves and their homeland. Initiates gained Sarifal's blessing—allowing them to call upon a protective boon that briefly shielded them and their nearby allies from a chosen element—and mastered the ritual of Spirit Fetch by the time they completed their training. As they grew in knowledge and experience, they learned to use primal forces to enhance their natural fortitude.

Depending on where their roles took them, feywardens trained for different contingencies and mastered different magics. They could call upon the powers of both nature and the Feywild to alter their physical and spiritual abilities or to summon powerful and useful fey spirits. For example, they could pop into existence a pixie-like spirit "advisor" who could offer insight, help them speak with animals, and let them see through its senses. Other feywardens could call upon the light of Sarifal, causing them to glow with a Feywild power that distracted and weakened their foes. The most powerful feywardens developed a talent for arcane and fey trickery, allowing them to mimic spells like blur, mirror image, spirits of the shadowed moon, and warlock's leap.

Members
Any fey or elf could join the feywardens' ranks (save for drow), although few high elves aside from the Llewyrr were welcome enough in Sarifal to be counted among their number. Wild and wood elves were most common.

There were no particular skills or abilities that new recruits were required to have, although many wielded primal power or had lived in the Feywild. Junior members operated under the oversight of their mentors, and upon passing a number of initiation tests to become fully independent and trusted feywardens, they were expected to teach and mentor new pupils. The most experienced and renowned feywardens were seen as not only the primary defenders of Sarifal, but as stewards and leaders of the realm, its culture, and its nature.

Feywardens were held to high expectations of discipline, especially by the standards of the fey. Most were well-meaning, and strove to be recognized for their deeds by the fey of Sarifal and especially by their High Lady. Members tended to dislike spending time outside of the wilderness, and saw efforts to tame the wilds as wasteful and untrustworthy. They had a deep reverence for the Earthmother and other primal spirits of the Moonshae Isles. Leaders among the feywardens attracted the direct attention of such spirits, who would visit them and encourage them to support strong and sustainable connections between civilization and nature.

Outsiders often perceived feywardens as fanatical, introverted, and suspicious. Unseelie fey were quick to try to tempt them toward ruin and betrayal.

## Sagrais Bigelow

Sagrais Bigelow was a halfling thief and one of the retired adventurers inhabiting the Nellie Thursday Home for Experienced Adventurers sometime in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
Sagrais had become quite rotund in his old age, reaching . He stood  tall and his hair, once brown, had become entirely white and fled to the back of his head leaving only a sharp widow's peak.

Personality
He would insist he was as capable a thief in his 150[ year as he was when he was 25, but the extra weight he gained after retiring made him much less dexterous than he thought. His hearing was also slowly slipping, and oftentimes people would need to talk quite loudly to make themselves understood by him.

Abilities
Sagrais was fluent in several languages: Common, Thieves' cant, Elvish, Dwarvish, Gnomish and Orcish. As a rogue, he was proficient in handling slings, daggers, knives, and darts. He was also knowledgeable in the arts of herbalism, rope use, and fire building, and was a capable pony rider.

Possessions
He still owned a longtooth +2 dagger and a pair of bracers of defense, and often wore his ring of protection +2. He also owned a very useful pouch of accessibility and an enchanted dinner plate that would summon a meat-and-potatoes-type dinner whenever it was warmed over an open fire up to four times a day. When forced to go adventuring, he would carry a set of five scroll with the spells invisibility, hold person, wall of ice, mending, and magic missile.

Relationships
His friend of many long years, Henry the Shadow Master, also resided at the Nellie Thursday Home for Experienced Adventurers and they would often sneak out to smoke their favorite pipeweed and have a drink without Henry's wife discovering anything.

History
In his younger days, Sagrais worked with a human illusionist named Henry, who performed spells and tricks as a distraction while Sagrais emptied the pockets of the audience.

At the age of one hundred and fifty years, Sagrais accompanied several other geriatric adventurers, including his good friend Henry, on a quest to find Wemic the Wise's old missing roommate. Calling themselves the Company of the Swan they would be dragged across several worlds, including Olympus, where they met the deity Hermes; the jungles of Quorsit; and the demi-plane of Kansas, where Sagrais would face Hedgeheart a dwarven thief in a duel.

## Sardamon Thorland

## Samnur

Samnur was a small coastal village in Chessenta during the 15 century DR.

Geography
Samnur lay on the eastern coast of the Bay of Chessenta, between Heptios and Mordulkin.

History
Not long after the Wailing Years, early in the 1400s DR, the Chessentan war hero Ishual Karanok fought his first battle against the Abolethic Sovereignty at Samnur. He won and forced them back into the water.

Inhabitants
It was home to some 1,300 people around 1479 DR.

Notable Locations
On a hill looking over the village was a granite monument dedicated to Ishual Karanok's victory over the Abolethic Sovereignty. Samnur also had a hidden temple to Umberlee that could only be reached a secret grate in the lagoon floor.

Saff was a dwarvish man with a light skin tone, long straight brown hair, and blue eyes. He kept his severe face clean-shaven.

History
In the, a gang assembled by Churg Elvek, secretly a cultist of the Absolute, sought to strengthen the cult's grasp on Baldur's Gate. When the famed writer Volothamp Geddarm returned to the Gate after misadventures in the Emerald Grove and coming face-to-face with the Absolute's army, Volo became a target for Churg and his gang. The master traveler was captured and tied to a stack of smokepowder barrels, ready to be "brought to justice" for being an alleged agent of the Absolute at the hands of the gang and street gawkers. Saff Fargoer was one of the commoners in the gathered crowd, who was swayed by Churg Elvek's accusations and called for the writer's blood. ## Salty Jack

Salty Jack, or just Salty, was an ex-sailor and a mad beggar from the city of Westgate in the late 14 century DR.

Description

Activities
Salty Jack believed that he had a job, income, and a title—"vagrant." The old madman proudly declared that occupation a badge of honor. He also blamed his need for coin on inflation.

History
In the, Salty Jack could be found on Westgate's Harbor Loop, begging for coin and offering advice in exchange. In his mad state, he thought the city of Westgate to be his ship, a fine vessel, and its inhabitants – the ship's crew. He regularly would scream in terror, claiming that the ship sprang a leak. Salty Jack told newcomers that not all crew members were upstanding citizens. Despite this, Jack could offer some insight, like where the local gang could be found, the Ebon Claws, whom he called rats, and pointed to wards their lair in the sewers that smelled of rats and poop, "Poop in the Loop," as he produly rhymed.

## Savage priest

Savage priest was a broad term for priests that lived beyond "civilization", in societies that were considered "primitive" by the standards and opinions of some Faerûnians, the unifying factor between the disparate cultures host so such priests being low technological progress and a lack of agriculture. For example, it would be quite unlikely to encounter such priests in the Dalelands. They were distinct from shamans, which were the more common form of priest for barbaric and savage societies, but could be misidentified as such. The term could also refer to priests who originated from such cultures and maintained a connection to them whilst living in another land or culture and priests that were adopted by such a culture.

Culture
Savage priest were finely in tuned with the nature and varied quite considerably in their faiths, with some even using animal totemss, but regardless of faith their rites were often viewed by "civilized" lay folk and priests as being flawed compared to their own. In terms of portfolio, a savage priest typically followed deities that represented animals, hunting, nature, the sky and weather, vegetation, earth, fire, or elemental forces in general. They were never found to follow deities representing evil, good, or disease.

The regions and cultures in the Realms where one could encounter those labeled as "savage priests" included Evermeet, among the most primitive tribes of green elves and possibly the Mazticans of Maztica, particularly the Dog People.

These priests usually ceased to be considered "savage" once they renounced their tribal membership and accepted being part of some other culture. This frequently occurred with priests that traveled far afield in adventuring companies, as they grew to learn so much of the outside world that they would feel less attached to their tribe.

Abilities
Depending upon their tribe, a savage priest was typically skilled in the arts of fishing, forestry, hunting, or trapping, as well as a variety of other nature and survivalist related skills. Due to their connection to nature, such priests had an innate ability to sense the presence of magic in their vicinity, which functioned similarly to the spell detect magic.

Combat
The weaponry typically found in the hands of a savage priest included the likes of blowguns (mainly in jungle environs), clubs, daggers, javelins, knives, longbows and shortbows, slings, and spears.

## Sahdein

House Sahdein ( was a noble family of the city of Procampur in the Vast. They were one of the younger noble families of the city, having originally emigrated from the far-off land of Zakhara.

Activities
The Sahdeins engaged in long-distance trade with a number of foreign lands, including their ancestral home of Zakhara.

In terms of faith, the Sahdeins revered the Cold Gods of the Elements, those who were known in Faerûn as the elemental lords. Their house's primary patron was Istishia, the goddess of elemental water. Over time, House Sahdein played an instrumental role in making worship of the elemental lords, primarily Istishia, acceptable in Procampur.

Relationships
The Sahdeins had neither any strong allies among the noble house of Procampur, nor any serious enemies. At the most they had a friendly rivalry with House Hannith, with the two houses always trying to outdo one another in being the most luxurious house in all of Procampur. They were relatively friendly with House Krimpach. And a disastrous failed effort of matchmaking with Seabury nearly led to war between the two houses.

Base of Operations
They had a house in the Nobles District of the city. Built with the funds of the house's founder, it was purposely designed with the intention of being the most ornate home in Procampur. Their manor had tan walls trimmed with gold and slender cylindrical towers, making it stand out against the more traditional architectural stylings of its neighbors. The inner courtyard had a garden with palm trees and other forms of plant life typical of oases.

History
This noble house's history in Procampur began roughly in the 1100s DR, when a Zakharan merchant vessel arrived at the city's docks. The eldest son of the vessel, Mustafa Sahdein, brought most of his family to settle in the city and over the next twenty years worked hard to build up both the respect and fortune necessary to be considered a Procampan noble.

Sometime in the 14 century DR, when Hamayarch Alamondh announced the dissolution of House Arawl, the Sahdeins were one of nine noble houses that offered to honor any unredeemed favors that the house had given out to adventurers.

Members
Members of House Sahdein as of 1370 DR were:
 Lord Consul Yezeed Sahdein, head of the family.
 High Lady Maneira Sahdein, spouse of Yezeed
 Akeem Mashara
 Azeem Sahdein
 Fayiz Sahdein, one of the sons of Yezeed.
 Garolik, an elite half-ogre guard.
 Ibrahim Sahdein
 Isaac Sahdein
 Kitsu
 Mustafa Sahdein, the founder of the noble house.
 Sheira Sahdein

## Savage wizard

Savage wizard was a broad term for wizards that lived beyond "civilization", in societies that were considered "primitive" by the standards and opinions of some Faerûnians, the unifying factor between the disparate cultures host so such mages being low technological progress and a lack of agriculture. For example, it would be quite unlikely to encounter such mages in the Dalelands, if not alarming, or on the Rock of Bral. They were distinct from witch doctors, a variation of shamans found in barbaric and savage humanoid societies that wielded both arcane and divine magics, though could be misidentified as such. The term could also refer to mages who originated from such cultures and maintained a connection to them whilst living in another land or culture.

Culture
Savage wizards were finely in tune with the nature, possessing a deep understanding of both plant and animal life. Though perceived by some as possibly aggressive, grim, or unsophisticated, the perceived "primitivism" of a wizard's tribal culture was not reflective of its moral, ethical, or alignment values. A "savage" tribe could just easily be a peaceful community of farmers and herdsmen as they could be barbarians.

The regions and cultures in the Realms where one could encounter those labeled as "savage wizards" included Evermeet, among the tribal green elves, and some of the people of northwest Faerûn's Savage Frontier. Though Mazticans were labeled by some as "primitives", there were wizards of this sort among them, as Maztica was instead home to unique spellcasters known as hishnashapers and plumaweavers. These wizards were also distinct from the anagakok, tribal wizards found among the Ulutiuns of the Great Glacier.

Where these wizards were found, they often held positions of power in their tribe, being either respected teachers, tribal counselors, or even leaders. They oversaw a tribe's purely magical rituals and protections, sometimes coming into conflict with shamans or witch doctors. In some cases, due to either superstition or the accusations of those seeking to usurp power, a wizard's tribesmen may come to believe that he is consisting with fiends or meddling with dark forces. Whenever this occurred the wizard was at risk of facing expulsion, accompanied by a threat of execution should they ever return.

Abilities
In terms of the schools of magic, savage wizards lacked the natural aptitude for mastery of abjuration or enchantment, but typically excelled in conjuration, invocation, and the conjuration subschool of summoning. In some cases they were even found to make adept practitioners of necromancy.

They were also typically quite physically fit and highly versatile due to part of their lives revolving around wilderness survival. The wide range of disciplines they were skilled in included the likes of fishing, fletching, forestry, the handling and training of animals, herbalism, hunting, tracking, and trapping.

Additionally, savage wizards had a handful of magical specialties that set them apart from some spellcasters. For one they were uniquely skilled in crafting protective talismans, which took the form of small pouches of herbs meant to be worn around the neck on a leather cord. These pouches exhibited an effect identical to the spell protection from evil and functioned for a full day, after which the herbs inside the pouch would disintegrate. Any manner of magic cancelling effects, such as dispel magic, could also end the effects of a talisman prematurely.

They were also skilled in constructing effigies,  tall clay dolls crafted in the likeness of another individual and containing some small organic material from the target, such as a lock of their hair or a fingernail. Regardless of their distance to the effigy, anything afflicted upon it would be suffered by the victim to whose likeness they were modeled after. This effigy would eventually disintegrate after either inflicting a certain amount of harm, being affected by dispel magic or similar effects, or simply after a whole tenday had passed since its creation.

Lastly, savage wizards were skilled at divining a major undertaking's fate by interpreting various omens found in nature. For example, gathering clouds, a swarm of insects, and veins on a leaf could all potentially be interpreted as omens. These mages typically consulted the omens of nature before embarking on a journey, engaging in a major battle, or setting out on any form of significant task. If the omens were found to be vague, they were considered neutral, but they were otherwise ranged from auspicious and favorable to ill omens and warnings of great danger ahead of the spellcaster.

Combat
When not casting spells a savage wizard typically wielded either a blowgun (mainly in jungle environs), dagger, knife, or sling.

Appendix
Notes

See Also
 Savage druid
 Savage priest
 Savage warrior
 Witch doctor
Background
The "Savage" archetype of class kits introduced in AD&D with the PHBR series of accessories, starting with The Complete Fighter's Handbook, are rooted in the Noble Savage stock character of Western anthropology and literature and to some extent the Wild Man archetype. The Complete Fighter's Handbook makes this evident with its multiple uses of the term "noble" and one of its examples of a "savage" character: "... a beautiful native princess from a culture which the characters consider impossibly primitive and yet uncorrupted and very noble..." The text even suggests that players coming "from a particularly noble tribe" could choose to act as a "voice of conscience", critiquing the seeming civilized practices of other players, much as Noble Savage characters were used to critique contemporary European societies.

It should also be noted that 'primitive' originally meant 'first', 'earliest', or 'original', while 'savage' meant 'untamed' or 'wild' in the sense of wilderness and living in nature rather than cities, and have been misapplied to mean 'crude' or 'violent', but the earlier definitions are still in use. Hence, the former meanings are more accurate to the case of primitive or savage characters: peoples living in the wilderness with the earliest tools and societies, as reflected above.
